# Dead_Metal_Rhapsody-_Core_Rulebook.pdf

 
 
 
Dead Metal Rhapsody 
 
 
 
 
 
 
The Heavy Metal Roleplaying Game of Undead 
Rockstars in the Ruins of Heaven and Hell 


 
 
 
Live Fast 
Die Young 
Live Again 
Try Not to Fucking Die Twice 
- 
Mantra of the Necromusician 
Circa. 2025 
 
 
 
This is a work of fiction. Any resemblance to any persons, living or dead, is purely coincidental. 
Honest. The author asserts that he wrote all this stuff himself, and refuses to use AI-generated 
art to pad out the page count because fuck AI art in particular. In a perfect world, where this 
wasn’t made for free, I’d pay talented artists for their work like a normal human being. Because 
they deserve it. The author also asks that you kindly don’t pass his shit off as your own, but is 
more than happy for you to share it, photocopy it, do whatever you want with the thing you chose 
to buy. Finally, this version of ‘Dead Metal Rhapsody’ will always be free, so if someone’s 
charged you for it, you’ve been ripped off. Fuck THAT person too. Not everything has to make 
money to be worth making. Have fun, and be good people! 
 


 
 
THERE WAS ONCE A CRACK IN THE 
HOUSE OF GOD 
 
ONE DAY, THE CRACK BECAME A 
CHASM 
 
AND THEN THE CHASM BECAME AN 
IMPOSSIBLE PIT 
 
AND THE CITY WHERE GOD’S HOUSE 
ONCE STOOD SLID SLOWLY INTO HELL 
 
THE LIVING ARE NOT WELCOME HERE 
 
HERE, IN THE DUST OF DEVILS AND 
RUIN 
 
HERE, ONLY THE DEAD REMAIN 


 
 
What’s in the Labyrinth, my lover, my love? 
 
A grave of gods and angels. 
 
How deep does it go, my darling, my dear? 
 
Into the bones and the roots of the Earth. 
 
Who is its master, my heart, my sweet heart? 
 
The choirs of devils, the dreaming, the dead. 
 
And what is within it, my precious, my prince? 
 
Flesh. Power. And dominion. 
 
You are not the first to seek the heart of the Labyrinth. 
You are not the only one to have sold your soul. 
You are not alone in the Sacrament, nor in the powers you 
wield. 
Perhaps you will find the secrets of the gods that failed. 
Or perhaps your songs will be forgotten. 


 
 
“Hear my call! By the binding of blood and sinew, I 
command you. Rise, my creatures of metal and flesh. Rise, 
and obey your master’s will!” 
 
“Fuck me, Clive. Bit intense. I was onboard with the 
Frankenstein shit, but the spiel’s a bit much, eh? We’re a 
metal band, not a cult.” 
 
“I dunno, Bruce. Maybe he’s onto something. Every band 
needs a gimmick, right? Perhaps ours can be… fucked up 
undead cyborgs? I mean, it sounds metal as hell.” 
 
“Steve, before you died, you were a builder. I was a 
plumber, and Clive was a fucking guitar teacher. I’m just 
saying, it’s weird that we’re just going along with this. I 
mean, my mic has sprouted legs and breathes acid now. 
That’s not normal for East London!” 
 
“East London is a thousand feet below the earth, Bruce. 
Its ruins are scattered in our wake. The Infernal Choirs 
offer us power. We must embrace it. Embrace it!” 
 
“Jesus, Clive. Still… maybe it gets us a record deal. 
Fuck. Fuck! Alright. Guess I’m in.” 


 
 
The band is playing your favourite song, in 
tunnels painted like the dawn. 
 
The music screams with infernal choirs, in the 
waste of old empires. 
 
The devils speak with honeyed tongues, and 
whisper pacts from blackened lungs. 
 
Deep below in shadowed tombs, deep within 
the catacombs… 
 
There’s buried power from beyond time, too 
twisted foul for this mere rhyme. 
 
To seek this power, for near it lies… 
 
All flesh must perish. You must die. 


 
 
Our first show was at a venue called the Cavern. 
Dingy little place, no bigger than a shed, small bar, 
sticky floors, lights that didn’t work and a smoke 
machine that shot straight into the drummer’s face. 
We played thirty minutes. Sounded like shit. When we 
got off the stage, we went to the dressers. That’s 
when we met him. The Man with the Iron Smile. 
 
Next time we played, it was at the local arena. 
About two thousand in the crowd- all lit up with gels 
and spotlights like a fucking airstrip. It was a hell of a 
shot in the arm, and the sound was brutal. Seven 
layers of fuzz, growls like a bear, drums rattling on 
like a jackhammer on pavement. We didn’t even notice 
the change back then. That came six shows later. 
When we met him again. 
 
When all my skin rotted off, and the singer’s 
skull forced its way out of his head. The drummer spat 
out maggots. 
 
We only play for the corpses now. And he’s still 
smiling. He never stops. He says he never will.

Welcome to Hell 
On the day the dome of St. Paul’s Cathedral 
cracked, some say that the sky was wreathed in 
sulphurous clouds. Some say that the River 
Thames ran red, others that rats abandoned the 
city streets, others still that the bells of every 
church tolled sonorous peals of thirteen. The 
truth is that it was just another day in London. 
The crack was nothing more than a crack. But 
when darkness fell and the crack grew wider, 
well… that was when shit really hit the fan. 
 
The crack became a chasm. Chunks of the 
dome collapsed. Stained glass windows 
shattered into storms of multicoloured shards 
and old statues toppled and broke into pieces. 
The mosaic floors split open. The ground gave 
way to a yawning crevasse which grew wider and 
wider, so wide that the cathedral itself toppled 
calamitously down into the long cold dark. But it 
didn’t stop growing. One by one, the buildings of 
London were swallowed up. Skyscrapers 
disappeared in landslides of steel; pavements 
buckled, split and snapped into tarmac 
fractures; pubs, police stations and the Houses 
of Parliament were utterly consumed. It only 
took an hour. Until nothing of the city was left. 
All that remained was the Pit.  
 
There were survivors who found themselves 
down there in the dark. The Pit was cavernous, 
almost impossible to climb. But, as their eyes 
adjusted to the gloom, they saw passages of 
rock, steel and flesh winding their ways down 
into the roots of the Earth. As they wandered, 
fearful and trembling, along these paths, they 
found the Labyrinth. And, within the Labyrinth, 
those entities that made the impossible place 
their home. 
 
Most of London’s population never emerged 
from the Pit. Countless millions, gone in an 
instant. But, in the days that followed, some 
staggered up and out, lurching up to the surface 
world in a hazy stupor. They were the first. The 
New Wave. The originators. They were rockstars, 
metalheads, musicians bound and branded with 
the mark of the Infernal Choirs below. 
 
And they were all undead. 
 
Well, un-alive. Or dead and fucking loving it. 
The World of Dead Metal Rhapsody 
 
The year is 2024, and the city of London has 
been engulfed by the Pit. Nearly everyone in the 
city has vanished, but a handful of survivors 
have emerged from the ruins. They are 
musicians, rockstars and metalheads, who 
survived the collapse by making deals with the 
Infernal Choirs who dwell in the Labyrinth 
below. The Labyrinth is an impossible maze of 
flesh, rock and ancient machinery fused into the 
bones of the world. The Infernal Choirs are a 
lineage of seventy-two Archdevils seeking to 
unearth its secrets- to do that, their music and 
black magic need vessels. That’s where the 
Necromusicians come in. 
 
A Necromusician is an undead rockstar, having 
experienced First Death and selling their Second 
to the Archdevils of the Labyrinth. They wield 
necromagical powers, channelled through their 
instruments and their raw charisma, and are 
driven by their infernal masters to explore the 
Labyrinth and seek out its mysteries. And to look 
and sound damn good while doing it. 
Necromusicians almost exclusively play rock, 
metal and punk- other genres struggle to 
channel the magic of the Archdevils, though the 
odd classical bloke gets a pass. They use the 
energy of their music to bind undead servants to 
their will, control and sculpt flesh, unleash 
terrifying sorcerous feats and strengthen their 
own bodies beyond the limits of mortal men. 
 
 
Why Play Dead Metal Rhapsody? 
 
Dead Metal Rhapsody is a roleplaying game 
about living (or un-living) fast and preferably 
dying as old as possible. It channels the 
anarchic and hardcore spirit of classic heavy 
metal, punk and hard rock, with a dash of gothic 
fantasy and urban horror mixed in. As a 
Necromusician, you’ll wield incredible powers, 
explore impossible places and maybe even 
make next month’s rent if the checks keep 
pouring in! It’s a game of reckless choices, style 
over substance and wild, uncertain times. 
 
So, what are you waiting for? Let’s tune up! 


 
 
Out of the Shadows 
How to Play Dead Metal Rhapsody 
 
What You’ll Need to Play 
 
Dead Metal Rhapsody is played using six-sided 
dice (D6s). Each player will need a handful of 
D6s in order to resolve their Necromusician’s 
actions. Players shouldn’t need more than six 
D6s, and can get by with three most of the time. 
 
Dead Metal Rhapsody doesn’t use a Combat 
Grid for those moments when shit gets violent. 
Instead, combat is designed to be wholly 
abstract- quick, dirty and full of theatrical, over-
the-top flair, but with enough brutal back-and-
forth to keep things interesting. After all, you are 
playing as hellraising undead rockstars with 
magical powers- the downside being that a lot of 
your foes will be too! 
 
The final thing you’ll need to play is one of the 
players to act as a Game Master, or GM. This 
player is responsible for setting the scene, 
roleplaying as the colourful cast of non-player 
characters (NPCs) that populate the world of 
Dead Metal Rhapsody, and acting as an 
arbitrator for the rules and for the spirit of 
everyone having a good time. Being a GM is 
quite a bit more of a responsibility than being a 
player- don’t worry, though! Being a GM is also 
hugely rewarding, and there’s a section later on 
designed to help you get to grips with it if it’s 
your first time. 
 
With all these bits and pieces assembled, you 
now just need a table, a time and a place to get 
together, and boom! You’re ready to play. 
 
Introducing Myra Skinless, Your Tour Guide to 
the Labyrinth 
 
Yo! The name’s Myra- Myra Skinless, scream queen for 
Morbid Visions, the most brutal necromantic deathcore 
group this side of the Pit. They asked me to give 
‘insights’ on what life is like in this fucking place. More 
than just ‘shit sucks’, y’know? So I guess I’ll be here to 
get y’all sorted out. Life’s shit, then you die, and then 
death’s just as bad. See you in the Pit, motherfuckers! 
The Core Mechanic 
 
Whenever a character tries to do something 
difficult or dangerous in Dead Metal Rhapsody, 
they’ll have to make a Test. Tests determine 
whether the character’s action Succeeds or 
Fails. In addition, if a character is particularly 
lucky, Tests can generate Star Power, which can 
be used for powerful bonus effects. Making a 
Test works as follows: 
 
1. Describe the Action: The player 
describes what they want their 
character to do. 
 
2. Determine Attribute: The GM 
determines which of the character’s 
three Attributes, Guts, Brains or Spine, 
will be used for the Test. 
 
3. Edges, Advantages and 
Disadvantages: If the character has 
any Edges that apply to the Test, or if 
they have any situational Advantages or 
Disadvantages, these are applied now. 
 
4. Roll Dice: The character rolls a number 
of D6 equal to their Attribute that is 
being used for the Test, +1D6 for every 
Edge or Advantage, minus -1D6 for 
every Disadvantage, to a maximum of 
6D6 or a minimum of 1D6. If at least 
one result is a 4+, the Test Succeeds. If 
no results are 4+, the Test Fails. 
 
5. Build Star Power: For every result of 4+ 
beyond the first, the character gains 
one Star Power (to a maximum of three). 
Star Power can be spent for Flourishes, 
bonus effects that apply to the Test’s 
outcome. Any Star Power not spent is 
lost. 
 
6. Determine Outcome: The GM 
describes the result of the Test with the 
help of the player. Test? Completed it, 
mate. 


 
 
Tests in More Detail 
 
So, you think you know everything there is about surviving 
in this shithole? Think again! There’s devils, dead dudes 
and darker things besides in this deep ditch of a dump, 
and they’re all out to get you. You need to know the ins 
and outs of being a Necromusician, and I ain’t talking 
about scooping out the guts of your latest experimental 
victim. We’re gonna go through how Tests work in more 
detail now- pay attention, ‘cause it might just save your 
death. 
 
Step One- Describe the Action 
 
Tests are only needed when there’s a chance a 
character’s action will fail. If what you’re trying 
to do is trivial, you probably don’t even need to 
make a Test to begin with! On the other hand, if 
you’re trying something risky, dangerous or 
downright suicidal, it makes sense to call for a 
Test. Where’s the fun in succeeding all the time 
without a little spark of danger? 
 
Before you do anything else, you need to 
describe what your character’s attempting to 
achieve should their Test be successful. Maybe 
you’re trying to leap across a precipitous chasm 
full of boiling flesh? Maybe you’re trying to 
invoke a dark patron to suffuse the corpse of 
your dead cat with black magic? Or maybe 
you’re running for your life as a skinless horde 
pursues you down a corridor made out of spines 
and blinking LEDs? Whatever the case, you 
should try to be as descriptive as possible, to 
really set the mood and the scene for you and 
your fellow players. Be creative! 
 
Example: Ben, playing as Kizzi “Gravebloom” 
Galder, is attempting to decipher an ancient 
tome of macabre rituals, written in a daemonic 
script. He describes to Dan, the GM, how Kizzi 
attempts to draw on her knowledge of the occult 
and archaic languages to decode the spidery 
runes. If successful, she hopes to learn the 
magicks concealed within… 
 
Step Two- Determine Attribute 
 
After you’ve described what your character is 
attempting to do, your GM will decide which of 
the character’s three Attributes is most 
appropriate for the Test at hand. Each Attribute 
represents a core aspect of your character’s 
abilities, and are as follows: 
GUTS 
 
Strength, athleticism, dexterity. Being 
hard as fuck and having the confidence to back 
it up. Surviving, punching out idiots, and lugging 
all your heavy shit back to the van after a show. 
 
 
Your Necromusician’s Guts is a 
measure of your physicality- your strength, 
speed and endurance. A Necromusician with 
high Guts will likely be durable, powerful and as 
physically fit as a walking corpse can be. Use 
Guts when making Tests that involve feats of 
strength, resistance, dexterity or quickness, like 
breaking faces, surviving a zombie mosh pit or 
breaking faces inside of a zombie mosh pit. Also 
for lifting stuff. 
 
BRAINS 
 
Intelligence, wisdom, acuity. Being a 
smart witch bitch and wielding the dark powers 
arcane. Casting spells, researching dangerous 
magical artefacts and making sure that the 
record label doesn’t screw you over. 
 
 
Your Necromusician’s Brains is a 
measure of your mental strengths- your 
intellect, perceptiveness and common sense. A 
Necromusician with high Brains will likely be 
smart, quick-witted and a dab hand at black 
magic. Use Brains when making Tests that 
involve quick thinking, research or magical 
aptitude, like deciphering a code, summoning a 
lurching horror from the depths of the Labyrinth, 
or just hucking a cool-ass fireball at someone’s 
head. 
 
SPINE 
 
Charisma, self-confidence, force of 
personality. Being a natural born leader with all 
the headstrong cockiness and swagger of the 
80s and 90s. Persuading people, keeping the 
band together and smiling for the cameras. 
 
 
Your Necromusician’s Spine is a 
measure of your personality- your charisma, 
natural charm and ability to talk the talk. A 
Necromusician with high Spine will likely be a 
great conversationalist, a persuasive smooth 
talker and a confident figurehead for their band. 
Use Spine when making Tests that involve 
interpersonal skills, like negotiating a better deal 
at a subterranean death cult club, dodging your 
way out of a devil’s contract or simply looking 
really cool as a big explosion goes off. 
 
The GM chooses whichever Attribute makes the 
most sense for the Test- their say is final! 


 
 
Example: Given that Ben’s character is 
attempting to decipher and learn of a macabre 
ritual, Dan, as the GM, decides that Brains is the 
most appropriate Attribute for Ben to use. Makes 
sense, given that the Test focuses on the 
exercise of knowledge and the learning of 
forbidden magic! 
 
Step Three- Edges, Advantages and 
Disadvantages 
 
All characters will start the game with a number 
of Edges, and may earn more during gameplay. 
An Edge represents a unique aptitude or talent a 
character has. Perhaps they’re particularly 
skilled at repairing damaged touring gear, or 
have exceptional upper body strength for 
hauling weight, or are a trained archaeologist or 
hypnotherapist- the possibilities for Edges are 
endless! 
 
If the GM deems that one or more of your 
character’s Edges apply to a Test, you add +1D6 
to the amount of dice you roll for each. The GM 
has the final say on when Edges apply. 
 
In addition to Edges, characters may encounter 
situational Advantages and Disadvantages 
throughout their journeys through the Labyrinth. 
These can take the form of favourable terrain, 
like a tactical vantage point overlooking a 
swathe of walking corpses, to fortunate 
circumstances, such as a deluge of blood 
concealing your footprints when trying to remain 
undetected, to straight-up bad luck, such as a 
watchful Gore-Hound sniffing you out as you 
turn a corner! 
 
If the GM deems that your character is 
benefitting from one or more Advantages, or 
suffering from one or more Disadvantages, you 
either add +1D6 per Advantage or subtract -1D6 
per Disadvantage. Advantages and 
Disadvantages cancel each other out. For 
example, if you had two Advantages and one 
Disadvantage, you’d have one Advantage overall 
for a +1D6 bonus. 
 
Remember that you can never roll more than 
6D6 for a Test, and can never roll less than 1D6, 
no matter how many Edges, Advantages and 
Disadvantages you may have. 
 
Example: Ben’s character has an Edge in 
“Academic Research”, given her background as 
a PhD student. Dan rules that this Edge will 
apply to the Test, giving Ben +1D6 to roll. In 
addition, as Ben has access to the ruins of the 
old UCL library within the Pit, Dan also rules he 
has an Advantage, meaning that he’ll get to roll a 
total of +2D6! 
 
Most Necromagicians are pretty similar to each other. A 
lot of misfits, tryin’ to make a living down here in the 
dark. It’s your Edges that give you, well… they give 
you a fuckin’ edge! Try to play to your strengths, and 
you won’t go far wrong. If you can’t play to ‘em, fight 
dirty instead. Eke out every Advantage you can, from the 
ground you’re standing on, to the tools you’re using, to 
the sorry state you’re trying to put the other fucker in. 
And for the devil’s sake, don’t stack up more 
Disadvantages than you can afford. In the long run, that’s 
what will getcha. 
 
Step Four- Roll Dice 
 
At this Step, you’ll need to add a few values 
together. First, take the value of the Attribute 
being used for the Test. Then, add any bonus 
D6s from Edges and Advantages. Then, take 
away any penalty D6s from Disadvantages. This 
will give you a number of D6 to roll (remember 
than it can’t be less than 1 or greater than 6!). 
Get those cubes together- it’s time to roll out! 
 
Roll all the D6s in your pool. As long as at least 
one of them results in a 4+, your Test is 
Successful. There are exceptions to this, such 
as in Combat, but for the most part this is the 
way things work. If none of your D6s results in a 
4+, your Test has Failed. Hard luck! 
 
Example: Ben has a Brains Attribute of 3, one 
Edge from his “Academic Research” and one 
Advantage from the UCL library ruins. That gives 
him a pool of 3 + 1 + 1, for a total of 5D6. He 
collects his pool of 5D6 and gives it a roll, 
resulting in 1, 2, 2, 4 and 6. As Ben’s got at least 
one result of 4+, his Test has Succeeded! 
 
THE DEVIL LIVES INSIDE US ALL. 
 
NO, IT AIN’T SOME FUCKIN’ 
COMMENTARY ON THE HUMAN 
CONDITION. 
 
YOU KNOW WHAT DEAL YOU MADE. 


 
 
Step Five- Build Star Power 
 
If you rolled at least two results of 4+ on your 
Test, not only did you Succeed, but you also 
unlocked your Star Power! Characters can 
generate up to three points of Star Power from a 
Successful Test, which can be spent to use 
Flourishes. You generate one point of Star 
Power, to a maximum of three, for every result of 
4+ on your Test beyond the first. Flourishes 
represent bonus effects that apply to the 
outcome of the Test, demonstrating exceptional 
effort, skill or plain dumb luck. 
 
A Flourish costs one point of Star Power to use. 
You can spend multiple points of Star Power on 
one Flourish, or split your points across several. 
If you ‘double-up’ on a Flourish, it becomes 
doubly potent, meaning its effects are 
amplified. If you use multiple Flourishes at 
once, you get all of their effects combined. Must 
be nice, eh? 
 
There are five Flourishes you can spend Star 
Power on when resolving the outcome of a Test. 
The player- NOT the GM- has the final say on 
how a Flourish influences the outcome of a Test. 
These Flourishes are as follows: 
 
BADASSERY 
 
Being impressive, over-the-top, larger 
than life and decidedly overconfident. 
Impressing the shit out of people. 
 
 
When you use the Badassery Flourish, 
you act in an impressive, showy and confident 
fashion, aweing those around you. This Flourish 
makes your action cooler, crazier and straight-
up more metal. People around you will want to 
be you, and you will make yourself look like a 
real rockstar at last. 
 
SLICKNESS 
 
Being efficient, smooth and annoyingly 
capable for someone who wakes up at noon. 
Being a consummate professional. 
 
 
When you use the Slickness Flourish, 
you complete your task efficiently, 
professionally and with the minimum amount of 
fuss. You do a top-notch and thorough job, 
covering all your bases, and keeping you from 
being blindsided by hidden details. You are on 
top on this, and you’re here to get shit done. 
 
 
LOUDNESS 
 
Being noisy, destructive and as 
emphatic as an electric guitar in the eye socket. 
Smashing that shit out of the park. 
 
 
When you use the Loudness Flourish, 
you approach the task with the aggression and 
‘git her done’ attitude of a long-serving Dead-
Head Roadie. The outcome is louder than 
everything else, but boy, does it deliver. It might 
not be graceful, but by the devil is it direct- and 
who’s got time in their unlife to be waiting 
around anyway? Right? 
 
SUBTLETY 
 
Being sneaky, secretive and 
underhanded, because sometimes that’s how 
you actually get things done. Being quick and 
quiet. 
 
 
When you use the Subtlety Flourish, you 
contain your raw rockstar charisma to act 
stealthily, going unnoticed or pulling off sneaky 
tricks that you don’t want others to see. 
Sometimes being a rockstar isn’t all about the 
fire and brimstone- sometimes, all it takes is a 
word in the right place, and a little elbow grease, 
to keep the world turning. 
 
SPEED 
 
Being fast. That’s it. What, you wanted 
more? OK, then… Shredding as fast as a thrash 
metaller playing their first widdly solo. There. 
Happy? 
 
 
When you use the Speed Flourish, you 
get things done rapidly. What would have taken 
a full hour takes twenty minutes. What would 
have taken two men a day takes you half of one. 
You blaze through problems like a workaholic 
seven coffees deep into their morning routine, 
without pausing for air. 
 
Example: As Ben rolled a 1, 2, 2, 4 and 6 on his 
Test, he has one extra result of 4+ to play with. 
He generates one point of Star Power. He 
decides he’s going to spend it on the Speed 
Flourish, as he wants to get the job done as fast 
as possible. 
 
As Ben gets to decide how the Flourish affects 
the Test, he describes how Kizzi gets a sudden 
burst of mental energy, blacks out and comes to 
with the book translated- all in less than an hour. 
 
 
 


 
 
Stage Six- Determine Outcome 
 
Depending on whether your Test Succeeded or 
Failed, the GM now narrates the outcome, with a 
little help from you and your Flourishes. If you 
Succeeded, you achieve what you set out to do- 
congratulations, mate, you won. If you Failed, 
there’s going to be a consequence. Perhaps 
someone gets hurt, you fall short at a crucial 
moment or life simply sucks for a little while. 
The exact details are up to you and your GM. 
 
Remember that roleplaying is a collaborative 
process, and that the end goal is for a fun play 
experience. If a character must Fail, and 
sometimes they must, it’s important to give 
them chances later down the line to make up for 
it, or do something cool or badass that makes 
their time less sucky. Remember, light and 
shade are two halves of the same coin. You 
need failure for tension and for keeping the 
stakes high, just as you need success to make 
the players feel like big damn rockstars. It’s 
about striking a balance- remember that, no 
matter which way the dice may fall! 
 
And try to have fun. You may be a walking 
corpse, but you’re a walking corpse with style, 
baby! Live your best unlife as fast as you can, 
before it inevitably burns out. 
 
Example: Ben and Dan work together to briefly 
describe Kizzi leafing through the text, rapidly 
solving its cryptic clues and learning what she 
can. Dan then describes the secrets hidden in 
the mystical tome, to which Ben reacts in 
character with eager glee. The Necromusicians 
have a new lead, and a new source of power. 
Where will that shit take them next? Who 
knows? They’ll have to keep playing to find out… 
 
The truth is that, one day, the devils will collect on their 
due. We’re all due a second death, and there ain’t no 
denying him once the reaper comes a-calling. Sometimes, 
when you fuck up, there are no second chances, no escape 
routes, no secret plans or backups or nothing. Sometimes, 
you’re just done. 
But that’s OK. Not every story has a happy ending- hell, 
for those of us that sell our souls to the devil, barely any 
of ours do. The important thing is to dance on that knife 
edge until that final day. Live fast, die young- die again 
but older. Enjoy the life you have, but embrace that 
you’ll bite it, one of these days. If death comes… let it 
be a good death. 
THE DAY THE DEVIL CAME 
 
They used to say “let you be in Heaven before 
the Devil knows you’re dead”. Only now we know 
there ain’t no Heaven. And that the devils are 
already here. My daddy used to warn me about 
ghouls and ghosts, fairies in the forest with their 
puzzles and their deals. I should’ve listened to 
him. Maybe then I wouldn’t be here. 
 
I used to live in London, sixty-two years, man 
and boy. Had a place down near the river with my 
mates, a shithole through and through. Smelt of 
liverwurst and tobacco, and was full of empty 
bottles and amps we fucked up playing. When the 
cracks broke through the riverbank, we were 
playing my daddy’s favourite song. Tuned down a 
few octaves, but there we were. Then the water 
hit. I watched the others float away. Hung there, 
breathing on empty. All swirling dark. It found 
me there. 
 
It spoke my name. 
 
I play an old guitar in Skin City, hooked up to an 
amp made out of all the old bones. Lot of ‘em 
down here, in the ruins. They resonate. Full of 
fuzz and the twelve-bar blues. The crowds down 
here don’t have much time for the classics, but I 
make my way. “Come see the Captain,” they all 
say, them folks down at the bar. “Come see the 
man with salt in his lungs.” ‘Course, I breathe 
water now, all brackish and full o’ foam. It spills 
from me like blood. Only stops when I play those 
old forgotten tunes. 
 
I think the devils have a sense of humour. Oh 
yeah, deep down, they do. I know a whole host of 
Necromusicians who go down in the Labyrinth, 
searching for all kinds of shit. But not me. 
Funny, isn’t it? I just keep playing. Strumming 
away, here at the end of the world.  


 
 
At the bottom of the Pit, there’s a ruin. 
 
What’s in the ruin, my child? 
 
The ruin’s an old house. I think I used to live there. 
It’s got hopscotch painted on a cracked driveway and 
smells like summer memories. I hold it close like a 
stuffed toy before it drifts away. 
 
There’s nothing in the ruin but dreams. 
 
Underneath the ruin, there’s the Labyrinth. 
 
Come, my child. Inside. I have sights to show you. 
 
The Labyrinth opens up into a stage of leather and 
metal. Ever-burning fires surround a drumkit. The 
guitarist’s corpse stands to attention; the singer’s at 
his mic. I sit on my stool with drumsticks made of his 
bones. 
I play the Devil’s songs now. 


 
 
Bonded by Blood 
Creating Your Necromusicians 
 
Creating a Character 
 
In order to play Dead Metal Rhapsody, you’ll 
need to create a character. Your character is the 
vessel through which you’ll explore the world of 
the Pit and the Labyrinth, and is bound by blood 
and Black Sacrament to the will of the Infernal 
Choirs as a hard-as-hell Necromusician. With 
the power of dark magic and heavy metal on 
their side, your Necromusician will burn brightly 
in the face of all the world’s horror, seeking out 
glory, power and fame before at last their 
Second Death comes to claim them. 
 
Creating a Necromusician is a simple process, 
consisting of 7 Steps. These Steps are as 
follows: 
 
1. Infernal Choir: All Necromusicians owe 
their unlives to one of the seven Infernal 
Choirs of Archdevils. Each of the 
Infernal Choirs has its own hidden 
agendas, sources of power and 
demonic aesthetics. Whilst this choice 
has no mechanical bearing on your 
Necromusician, it has a very important 
bearing on their backstory and 
personality. After all, your Infernal Choir 
represents the fuckers to whom you 
owe your soul. Heavy is the head that 
wears the crown, right? PICK ONE 
INFERNAL CHOIR TO WHOM YOUR 
NECROMUSICIAN IS BOUND. 
 
2. Attributes: All Necromusicians have 
three core Attributes that define them. 
We’ve met these Attributes already- 
Guts, Brains and Spine. At this Step, 
allocate values of 1, 2 and 3 to each of 
these Attributes. This will give your 
Necromusician something they’re good 
at, something at which they’re bang 
average and something at which they 
kinda suck. Can’t win them all. ASSIGN 
EACH ATTRIBUTE A VALUE OF 1, 2 OR 3. 
YOU CAN’T ASSIGN THE SAME VALUE 
TO AN ATTRIBUTE MORE THAN ONCE. 
 
3. Secondaries: Secondaries are less 
definitive than Attributes, but still 
provide some key details about your 
character- especially during Combat. A 
Necromusician’s Secondaries are 
Armour, Defence, Necromagic, Speed, 
Will and Wounds. A few of these are 
dependent on your character’s 
Attributes; a few you can choose for 
yourself. DETERMINE THE VALUES OF 
YOUR SECONDARIES. 
 
4. Edges: Edges represent unique abilities 
or talents your Necromusician has- a bit 
like skills, but with more flexibility. At 
this Step, choose four Edges for your 
character. These can be anything you 
can think of, but each of them should 
be fairly specific. For example, “Good 
with Technology” is too vague for an 
Edge, but “Instrument Repair and 
Maintenance” is A-OK. Your GM has the 
final say on whether your Edges are 
specific enough. PICK FOUR EDGES 
FOR YOUR NECROMUSICIAN. 
 
5. Necromagical Powers and 
Instrument: Each Necromusician 
starts the game with three 
Necromagical Powers- one Minor, one 
Major and one Metal. These powers are 
tied to their Instrument, which can be 
anything they choose. What 
Necromagical Powers do is entirely up 
to you, so get creative! However, your 
GM has the final say on your powers, 
and whether they are appropriate for 
each power level. CHOOSE AN 
INSTRUMENT, AND THEN CHOOSE 
THREE NECROMAGICAL POWERS, ONE 
MINOR, ONE MAJOR AND ONE METAL. 
 
6. Signature Gear and Drachma: For the 
sake of convenience, Dead Metal 
Rhapsody assumes that each 
Necromusician has enough day-to-day 
bits and pieces to get by. Y’know, boring 


 
 
stuff that we don’t need to keep track of 
to make the game fun. However, they 
will also have items of Signature Gear 
and some money that are a bit more 
important. You can choose three pieces 
of Signature Gear your Necromusician 
is never seen without, such as 
weapons, trinkets or ritual fetish 
chalices full of lizard blood. The choice 
is yours! You also start the game with 
some Drachma, the currency of the 
Labyrinth, and are assumed to own the 
Instrument you chose back in Step Five. 
You can also choose as many 
inconsequential little Trinkets to start 
the game with as you wish, which 
should be more about expressing your 
character’s personality than 
mechanical advantage. The GM has the 
final say on what constitutes a Trinket. 
CHOOSE THREE PIECES OF 
SIGNATURE GEAR. YOU START THE 
GAME WITH A “SKINT” LEVEL OF 
DRACHMA AND YOUR INSTRUMENT. 
YOU CAN HAVE AS MANY TRINKETS AS 
YOU WISH. 
 
7. Personality: Now that all the 
mechanical elements of your 
Necromusician are ready to go, you 
need to give them a name and flesh out 
their personality. The name part’s easy- 
for the personality, try to answer the 
following eight questions about your 
Necromusician in as much or as little 
detail as you like: 
 
a. What was your old life like, 
before you died? 
 
b. How did you die and why did 
you take the Archdevil’s deal? 
 
c. What do you love most? 
 
d. What do you stand for? 
 
e. What is your greatest failing? 
 
f. 
What do you want most out of 
your unlife? 
 
g. Who are your closest friends? 
 
h. Who are your deadliest 
enemies? 
 
THE BALLAD OF ALICE THE AXE 
 
Gather round, friends, and I’ll sing you a song, 
A tale of blood, tears and woe. 
Of a woman, parted for so long, 
From the lover she used to know. 
 
Alice her name, yes, Alice the Axe, 
Eyes burning like fire. 
With a bass with blades of broken glass, 
Blazing with hatred and ire. 
 
In the dark of the Pit she lived with her girl, 
Both dead in an Archdevil’s grasp. 
But evil came knocking, in a fateful swirl, 
And her girl once again breathed her last. 
 
Alice the Axe watched the raiders retreat, 
Down into the shadowy tombs. 
Her heart was torn with furious heat, 
She knew what she must do. 
 
So Alice the Axe went down in the dark, 
Bass like a hunter’s heartbeat. 
Sniffing for blood like a monstrous shark, 
‘Till she found the raiders’ seat. 
 
Their leader laughed loud- a muscular man, 
Seven foot two and twice as wide, 
But Alice stuck to her vengeful plan, 
And slashed her bladed bass inside. 
 
She tore him apart, that murderous fuck, 
Glass shredding his flesh and bones, 
His gang were also shit out of luck, 
As she cut them down one by one. 
 
So if you kill off someone’s love, 
Beware the price on your head, 
For Alice the Axe will hunt you down, 
And make sure you stay fucking dead. 
 
 


 
 
BUT WHAT OF THE UNBOUND TWO? 
 
“Hold on, Gordo. You said seventy-two Archdevils, 
right? And seven choirs of ten? I’m not much for 
maths, mate, but ain’t that leaving two left 
over?” 
 
“…Don’t know what you mean, Frank. Sounds 
plain enough to me.” 
 
“Mate, come on. Seventy-two, minus seven times 
ten, that’s two left over. What about them, 
then? They got a special choir?” 
 
“Frank, please. Just leave it. Shit, it’s getting 
cold in here. Is it cold in here? Just me?” 
 
“Nah, feels toasty as to me. But, seriously- the 
two of ‘em just don’t like company or something? 
Is it possible for them Archdevils to hate each 
other so much that some of ‘em don’t get to sit 
at the table? Hey, you alright, Gordo? Where 
you off in such a hurry?” 
 
“I warned you, Frank. I fucking warned you. God, 
it’s cold! It’s cold, cold, cold. I’ve gotta go. I 
need to get out of this place.” 
 
“Sheesh. What’s eating him, huh?” 
 
THERE IS A BETTER 
QUESTION. 
 
BREATHE IN OUR FOG, 
FRANK. 
 
WHAT WILL BE EATING 
YOU?  
Step One- Infernal Choir 
 
The Archdevils of the Labyrinth are seventy-two 
in number, chronicled in the Lemegeton of 
Borgir. They form the seven Infernal Choirs, each 
of which consists of ten Archdevils whose 
sinister schemes and desires align. Each 
Infernal Choir draws its power from the 
Symphonies of the Spheres, the last echoes of 
the divine music of a heaven long dead. Once 
angelic, this music has been corrupted and 
twisted, and is more commonly referred to as 
the Symphonies of Sickness. Each Infernal Choir 
commands a particular Symphony, or, at least, 
its broken husk. From this music comes their 
power, which, to mortals and the newly dead, 
appears as black magic. 
 
The Infernal Choirs each rule over a swathe of 
the Upper Labyrinth, which has mutated to take 
on the aspect of its masters. Those that 
experience First Death within the Labyrinth are 
entreated by the Archdevils as they stand on the 
brink between life and death. They are promised 
great and terrible power if they sign their Second 
Death over to the Archdevils, arising as a 
Necromusician should the deal be struck. When 
a Necromusician perishes for the second time, 
the Archdevils collect their due. They feast on 
the meat of souls, consigning a Necromusician 
to agonising torment, slow decay and eventually 
total emptiness. The mantra of the 
Necromusicians has thus become ‘live fast, die 
young, die again older’, as they attempt to stave 
off the Archdevils’ constant hunger. 
 
Each Infernal Choir’s Symphony is not dissimilar 
to the rock and metal music of the modern day. 
After all, they say the Devil has the best tunes. 
The Archdevils and their servants tend to take 
on the aesthetic of their aligned genre. Some 
bind pallid, corpse-painted ghouls in black 
leather fetish clothes, others zombie punks in 
riot gear with lurid spiky hair, others still rotting 
masses of diseased and mangled flesh shacked 
in metal.  
 
What You Need to Do 
 
Choose one of the following Infernal Choirs to 
be that to which your Necromusician is bound. 
This has no mechanical effect, but it offers 
inspiration for your character’s look, powers, 
personality and goals. It also helps situate them 
in the world of Dead Metal Rhapsody. 
 


 
 
Angels of Death 
“Death is Quick. Be Fucking Quicker” 
 
King 
Baal 
He Who Reigns in Blood 
 
Prince 
Vassago 
Invoker of the Tornado of Souls 
 
Dukes 
Agares, Amdusias, Avnas, Valefar 
The Four Horsemen 
 
Earls 
Aamon, Gamigin, Leraje, Valac 
They of the Burning Leather and Chains 
 
The Angels of Death are born of denim and leather, bound in hellfire and bathed 
in the blood of those consumed by the Pit during London’s fall. They take the 
form of grinning skeletal colossi clad in black, many-limbed and buzzing, the air 
around them alive with churning riffs and the distant sound of screaming. Where 
they walk the Labyrinth comes alive. Its very walls warp and run like sweat, 
dancing to a discordant rhythm that grows ever faster, until- at last- they crash to 
a halt and freeze in anguished, jagged shapes. The Angels of Death are gripped 
by ceaseless motion, a ferocious and all-consuming impatience that burns in their 
veins like a heartbeat on the brink of cardiac arrest. Even their bodies never stop 
thrashing and squirming, their bones scraping against leather and screeching 
furiously on metal studs and chains. Each has four heads, constantly banging, 
mottled with lank tresses of sweat-encrusted hair. 
 
Those bound to the Angels of Death bleed flames and breathe adrenaline. Their 
skin and flesh melts from the inside out, leaving charred skeletons behind. Some 
sprout wings of ignited petrol; others grow additional limbs that endlessly strum 
at invisible instruments or beat unseen drums; others still have their bones 
loosen entirely, becoming spiralling circle pits of flying femurs and thrashing 
tibias that expand and contract like miniature tornados. Their powers centre on 
speed, recklessness and sheer bloody-minded aggression. If its back cannot be 
broken by perseverance, if acceleration cannot overcome the mountain ahead, then 
these Necromagicians crash and burn, overwhelmed by the speed of their 
existence. They are the definition of “it is better to burn out, than it is to fade 
away.” 
 
Playlist: Metallica, Megadeth, Slayer, Anthrax, Exodus, Kreator, Sodom, Testament 


 
 
Apokalypsis 
“Our Grief is Our Strength. Our Shadows are Security” 
 
King 
Paimon 
The Swallower of Every Sun 
 
Prince 
Sitri 
She of the Black Cathedral 
 
Dukes 
Barbatos, Camio, Eligos, Gusion 
The Candlelit Mass 
 
Earls 
Forneus, Naberius, Ose, Ronove 
The Last Fair Deal Gone Down 
 
Apokalypsis is a convent of fallen angels who dwell in the darkest reaches of the 
Labyrinth. There, only the sickly heartbeat of the Symphonies remains, a cold 
and forlorn reminder of all the past glories of a long-dead Heaven. It is in this 
near silence that the Archdevils of Apokalypsis lurk. They are beings of freezing 
shadow, indistinct shapes of looming dread that speak in the sonorous tones of a 
coffin lid being slammed shut. Their forms invoke a primal terror in all who 
witness them, for they prey on the latent fears of the unknown, evoking half-seen 
horrors just at the corner of sight. They are among the oldest beings within the 
Labyrinth, and they are patient. They will wait until the world’s ending if it 
means spiting those that would stand against them, and this is reflected in the 
long abyss of their home. The Labyrinth unfurls into murk around them, an 
echoing cathedral, an empty cavern, a hall or forgotten home that holds nothing but 
dead memories and regrets. There are few that consort with them. They are 
anathema. 
 
The servants of Apokalypsis are just as formless as their masters. They are 
spectres, shadowy ghosts, indistinct shades or bubbling half-formed things that 
itch and crawl with miasma. They draw on the cold inevitability of death and its 
weapons of terror, dispassionately invading the minds and bodies of others to 
serve the inscrutable will of the convent. They are slow, frail but utterly 
purposeful. Even in the face of adversity they seldom falter. For what is a year, a 
decade, a century, in the eyes of a deathless being? What, ultimately, can stop the 
spread of a darkness that does not sleep? In the end, everything decays. And 
everything that decays will be their dominion. 
 
Playlist: Swallow the Sun, Candlemass, Sleep, Black Sabbath, Electric Wizard 


 
 
Corpse Crusaders 
“By Our Will Shall the Living Be Slain” 
 
King 
Beleth 
Singer of the Flaming Rhapsody 
 
Prince 
Aiperos 
Keeper of the Seventh Key 
 
Dukes 
Bathin, Malphas, Saleos, Zepar 
The Beasts in Black 
 
Earls 
Haagenti, Marchosias, Phenex, Sabnach 
The Grace and Might of their Lord 
 
Corpse Crusaders are those that have sworn their unlives to the destruction of the 
living. In the depths of the Labyrinth, the living seldom venture- those that do are 
often met with serried ranks of undead soldiers in lacquered black armour, meting 
out the twisted justice of their demonic lords in the face of this invasion. For the 
Corpse Crusaders will not suffer the living in the halls of the dead. These 
Archdevils take the form of towering winged knights, robed in blazing sunlight 
that masks the decaying flesh beneath. Their voices echo with fervour, and their 
blades are ablaze with living lightning that crackles brightly in the presence of the 
living. When they fight, the Archdevils sing triumphant war cries and songs of 
battle in voices that resonate with the chorus of the victorious dead, though their 
forms become more chaotic and unhinged as blood is spilled. As the living are 
purged, the true nature of the Archdevils is revealed: rotting, embittered husks 
that merely echo past glories, grown savage and insane from years underground. 
 
The Necromusicians in service to the Corpse Crusaders appear as mighty 
warriors of history: Roman centurions, Norse barbarians, samurai of the far 
east and knights of the Middle Ages. Beneath this heroic veneer, however, they 
are just as foul and corrupt as their masters, their glittering armour cracked and 
rusted, their luscious silvery locks bleached of all colour and dried out like burnt 
straw. They speak of valour, of heroic deeds, of legendary battles and great 
rallies against evil in an era of glory and war. The reality is that they dwell 
within the darkness of the Labyrinth, and their delusions of grandeur are just as 
deep as the depravity of those that embrace its dark gifts. 
 
Playlist: Rhapsody of Fire, Blind Guardian, Helloween, Manowar, Angra, Sabaton 


 
 
Cult of the Frozen Moon 
“We Walk With the Beasts of the Long Dark” 
 
King 
Purson 
She Upon the Darkest Throne 
 
Prince 
Gaap 
Anthem of the Dead Suns 
 
Dukes 
Aym, Berith, Bune, Glasya-Labolas 
The Light’s Bane 
 
Earls 
Andras, Forrasis, Oriax, Shax 
Sons of Northern Darkness 
 
The Cult of the Frozen Moon emerged from a shadowed forest of snow-capped 
pines, snared in the ruined coils of the Labyrinth. There mountains rose against 
a skyline of wreckage, burnt-out buildings sheltering caves of ice and graveyards 
of frozen corpses. Nothing moved in the silence of the forest. The dark reaches of 
the caverns of the Labyrinth remained faceless and black. And, in the stillness, 
the Archdevils of the Cult skulked out into the snow. They are bestial creatures, 
amalgamations of prehistoric beasts, northern animals, insects and parodies of 
man. Their skin is pure white, devoid of colour, with eyes so pitch black as to 
steal the light from the world around them. They speak in tongues, rasping 
languages no man could hope to know, and yet their words are as clear as 
meltwater on a winter’s morning. In line with their monstrous forms, the Cult 
have no interest in the machinations of the mortal world. They lust for the 
destruction of wrought things, the ruin of all that’s made. They hunger for a 
return to the woods, to the deep places. And their hunger is growing. 
 
Servants of the Cult often sport bestial mutations and growths that tie them to the 
forest below. Bear-like jaws, antlers, horns and shaggy pelts of fur locked in 
permafrost are common sights, as are vicious claws in place of weapons. The 
powers of these Necromusicians are primitive but ferocious, anguished howls 
against all the heinous complexities of the modern world. Jets of freezing mist, 
bestial transmutations and hosts of undead animals are at their command, as they 
pursue the solitary goal of their masters. Destroy, leave barren, and then return 
to the freezing cold of the unmade world. 
 
Playlist: Darkthrone, Dimmu Borgir, Saidan, Spectral Wound, Leviathan, Emperor 


 
 
Why are there no lights here? 
 
My skin… Oh, God. I can smell my blood, but I can’t 
see it. I feel the cold, the branches of the trees. But 
there is no light. There is no fucking light! The roots 
catch at my feet. This forest doesn’t end. I stumble on, 
until I reach the river. It’s frozen shut. 
 
I stand at the bank and scream into the night. 
 
And the beasts in the forest scream with me. 
 
A day has passed, and I escaped the forest, by the light 
of the freezing moon. I see the glimmer of the 
settlement’s electric lamps- the old ruin of a convenience 
store, collapsed down here. Seven or eight survivors. 
Huddled close for safety. 
 
Why are there lights here? 
 
I snarl, and draw my lips back from my teeth. It’s like a 
mouth full of broken glass. I hear the drums in the 
distance. Then I am upon them. 


 
 
Engine of Destruction 
“In Death, Is Death. In Metal, Is Salvation” 
 
King 
Asmodeus 
Master of the Factory of Terror 
 
Prince 
Stolas 
Born of a Soulless Machine 
 
Dukes 
Astarot, Botis, Focalor, Vephar 
The Tools of Progress 
 
Earls 
Andrealphus, Decarabia, Kimaris, Morax 
Blood of Oil and Darkness 
 
Not all of the Labyrinth is comprised of ancient passageways and the ruins of a 
bygone civilisation. In some corners, the Labyrinth becomes a spidery network of 
brushed steel, a chemical stench and the flicker of hazard lights, casting jagged 
shadows over industrial machinery. The Engine of Destruction is a choir of 
Archdevils who have embraced the lure of technology, seeing metal and gears as 
the path to progress and all organic life as a stain. These Archdevils appear as 
hideous cyborg fusions of machinery and rotting skin, their thousand tendrils and 
wiry limbs stripping away their blood and offal and replacing it with crackling, 
rust-shod devices like an assembly line. Their domain is a boiling, sweltering 
factory of corrugated iron, churning out creatures that are malformed experiments 
and rejects of LEDs, mutilated bodies and industrial machines. The Archdevils 
of the Engine admire the relentless march of industry in the surface world, and 
seek, one day, to shoe the entire Labyrinth in a metal coat. 
 
The Necromusicians of the Engine of Destruction are stoic fusions of man and 
machine. Their powers are just as much technological as they are sorcerous, the 
gifts of their dark masters sometimes being ersatz and horrifying machines 
instead of magical endowments. What little organic matter is left of them shivers 
and quivers between whirring works of engineering embedded in their bodies. 
Barely recognisable as the people they once were, these post-human undead see 
themselves as the next logical stage in human evolution. All those that resist the 
call of the Engine are treated as nothing more than raw materials, fuel for the 
endless furnaces that burn ceaselessly, spitting out smoke and bile. 
 
Playlist: Nine Inch Nails, Rammstein, Fear Factory, Ministry, Meshuggah, Type 0 
Negative 


 
 
Legion of the Night 
“Slither, my Sisters- Our Power is Renewed in Blood” 
 
King 
Vinea 
Songstress of the Damned 
 
Prince 
Orobas 
Voice of a Thousand Ships 
 
Dukes 
Alloces, Crokel, Dantalion, Vreal 
They Who Wish Upon the Night 
 
Earls 
Buer, Furfur, Malthus, Raum 
The Beautiful Ones 
 
Devils often beguile and mislead to ensnare unwary victims in their nets. No 
Infernal Choir does so more deviously than the Legion of the Night, the 
Archdevils of which garb themselves as impossibly beautiful and bewitching 
figures that seem to radiate dark temptation. Their bodies androgynous, their 
faces smiling beatifically, it would be easy to mistake the Legion of the Night for 
an order of angels- but the truth is far more sinister. Once the deal is struck, the 
true form of the Legion is revealed. From beauteous porcelain flesh crawl 
festering knots of maggots the size of fingers. From eye sockets once bright and 
blue spill leeches so thick and fat as to engulf a limb. From mouths speaking 
words of truth and love, ticks, fleas and lampreys slither, until the newly sworn is 
entirely infested with these demonic parasites, a new host for the Legion’s ever-
growing vampiric swarm. Their blood devoured, their flesh eaten, their skin but a 
mere sack for the creatures within, the still-conscious Necromusician is then 
consumed with the same hunger as their masters. The desire to bewitch, seduce 
and then consume the living and the dead. 
 
 
Servants of the Legion have powers of trickery and deception on their side. 
Vampires all, their bodies may appear youthful and comely, but their true selves 
are hideous composite abominations that defy description. They are capable of 
manipulating blood and bile, of calling to the insects buried deep within the earth 
and singing their siren songs to entrap all who would listen. The Legion seeks 
to grow in number. Those that answer its call can never shed its gift, lurking in 
foul swamps masquerading as manors until the call to feed comes again. 
 
Playlist: Nightwish, Epica, Within Temptation, Avantasia, Delain, Twilight Force 


 
 
The Way of All Flesh 
“Bound in Blood, Skin and Gore” 
 
King 
Balaam 
Grinder of a Million Corpses 
 
Prince 
Seir 
Blood Scribe of God’s Obituary 
 
Dukes 
Flauros, Gremory, Murmur, Naphula 
The Depraved Amalgams 
 
Earls 
Andromalius, Bifrons, Forcas, Marbas 
The Rotten 
 
The winding corridors of the Labyrinth often give way to pulsing passages of 
living flesh, skin like molten glass stretched over bony protrusions, twisting 
rivulets of blood mingling with oil and discharge. These tunnels open into 
hideous chambers sculpted of raw organic matter, stitched into sculptures of 
broken and violated human form. These are the workshops of the Way of All 
Flesh, artisans and sculptors of the dead. The Archdevils of the Way are many-
limbed surgeons, vast colonies of the dead sewn together, their spider-like limbs 
winding threads through their own corpses. The architecture of their bodies is 
defiant to all laws of nature. It is as if a maddened artist had crushed in oversized 
hands the works of  God, leaving the bloody and indistinct ruin as a smear on the 
face of creation. The Way of All Flesh see the mortal body as a vessel for their 
insane experimentation, the ends of which have long since been lost to time. 
Those who fall into their grasp are irrevocably and sickeningly changed- that is, 
if they do not simply become a heaving component of the Labyrinth’s 
biomechanical architecture. 
 
Servants of the Way of All Flesh are amalgams in themselves, golems of 
severed limbs, grafts of fat and stolen flesh. They have command over profane 
surgery and rituals of blood magic; their bound minions are creations of demonic 
science, covered in stitches and metal staples that pockmark their gangrenous 
skin. Necromusicians following the Way consider themselves visionaries, daring 
artistes operating beyond the boundaries of art and birthing new and provocative 
creations. To those outside, they are demented and perverse, mangling cadavers 
and delighting in the sordid. 
 
Playlist: Autopsy, Suffocation, Cannibal Corpse, Death, Obituary, Possession 


 
 
Step Two- Attributes 
 
Now that you have chosen your 
Necromusician’s Infernal Choir, you need to 
determine your Attributes. Attributes represent 
the fundamental abilities of your character- 
where Edges represent unique skills and talents, 
Attributes are at the core of your character’s 
identity, and will influence how they interact 
with the world of Dead Metal Rhapsody. As a 
reminder, there are three Attributes in the game: 
Guts, Brains and Spine. 
 
GUTS 
 
Strength, athleticism, dexterity. Being 
hard as fuck and having the confidence to back 
it up. Surviving, punching out idiots, and lugging 
all your heavy shit back to the van after a show. 
 
 
Your Necromusician’s Guts is a 
measure of your physicality- your strength, 
speed and endurance. A Necromusician with 
high Guts will likely be durable, powerful and as 
physically fit as a walking corpse can be. Use 
Guts when making Tests that involve feats of 
strength, resistance, dexterity or quickness, like 
breaking faces, surviving a zombie mosh pit or 
breaking faces inside of a zombie mosh pit. Also 
for lifting stuff. 
 
BRAINS 
 
Intelligence, wisdom, acuity. Being a 
smart witch bitch and wielding the dark powers 
arcane. Casting spells, researching dangerous 
magical artefacts and making sure that the 
record label doesn’t screw you over. 
 
 
Your Necromusician’s Brains is a 
measure of your mental strengths- your 
intellect, perceptiveness and common sense. A 
Necromusician with high Brains will likely be 
smart, quick-witted and a dab hand at black 
magic. Use Brains when making Tests that 
involve quick thinking, research or magical 
aptitude, like deciphering a code, summoning a 
lurching horror from the depths of the Labyrinth, 
or just hucking a cool-ass fireball at someone’s 
head. 
 
SPINE 
 
Charisma, self-confidence, force of 
personality. Being a natural born leader with all 
the headstrong cockiness and swagger of the 
80s and 90s. Persuading people, keeping the 
band together and smiling for the cameras. 
 
 
Your Necromusician’s Spine is a 
measure of your personality- your charisma, 
natural charm and ability to talk the talk. A 
Necromusician with high Spine will likely be a 
great conversationalist, a persuasive smooth 
talker and a confident figurehead for their band. 
Use Spine when making Tests that involve 
interpersonal skills, like negotiating a better deal 
at a subterranean death cult club, dodging your 
way out of a devil’s contract or simply looking 
really cool as a big explosion goes off. 
 
You have three values to assign to your 
Attributes: 1, 2 and 3. 1 represents a weakness, 
2 an average level of ability, and 3 a strength. 
Assign each of these values to your Attributes; 
you cannot assign the same value more than 
once. This means that every Necromusician in 
Dead Metal Rhapsody will start with one of their 
Attributes at 3, one of their Attributes at 2 and 
one of their Attributes at 1. The choice of which 
is, of course, up to you! 
 
Example: Ben is in the process of creating his 
Necromusician, Kizzi “Gravebloom” Galder. He 
has decided on Apokalypsis as his Infernal 
Choir, and now needs to determine her 
Attributes. He decides he wants Kizzi to be a 
powerful spellcaster with a quick wit and snarky 
attitude, but with an indistinct shadowy body 
that often fails her. As such, he decides to give 
her Brains of 3, representing her intelligence and 
magical aptitude, a Spine of 2, representing her 
fast-talking but sometimes abrasive personality, 
and a Guts of 1, representing her physical 
weakness. Boom- Kizzi’s already starting to take 
shape as a fully-formed character! 
 
 
Not every Necromusician is created equal. And you know 
what? That’s A-fucking-OK with me. We can’t all be 
good at everything, and we can’t all be losers either. 
Sometimes, you gotta play to your strengths, and 
understand where your weaknesses lie. Hell yeah, you’re a 
rockstar- but you sure as shit ain’t a god. This shit comes 
slowly. Builds up, over time. Besides, there ain’t much 
fun to be had, being all indestructible out of the gate. 
You got to work at life- or, well, death, I guess. Make 
death work for you. And hey, if you live long enough, and 
keep dodging the hands of the devils, maybe one day 
you’ll be strong enough to challenge them. Heh. Fucking 
funny. Just my little joke. Carry on. 
 
 
 


 
 
Step Three- Secondaries 
 
A Necromusician’s Secondaries are 
characteristics that primarily define their 
abilities during Combat- the hard-and-fast 
numbers that will keep them alive when push 
comes to shove. Some of them are derived from 
your character’s Attributes; some of them, you 
get to decide for yourself. Your Secondaries are 
as follows: Armour, Defence, Necromagic, 
Speed, Will and Wounds. 
 
ARMOUR 
 
Resilience, durability, sheer bloody-
mindedness. Enduring injury, whether physical, 
mental or goddamn magical. 
 
 
Armour offers your character a specific 
form of protection against the dangers of the 
Labyrinth. During Character Creation, you can 
pick one of the following types of Armour, which 
starts with a value of 5+. Whenever you suffer 
Damage from a source you have Armour 
against, you can roll an Armour Save to mitigate 
it, which is described in more detail in the 
“Combat” chapter of this book. The types of 
Armour are as follows: 
 
- 
Physical: Physical Armour protects 
from bodily harm and injury, such as 
gunshots, switchblades, explosions 
and giant fuck-off demon claws. 
 
- 
Mental: Mental Armour protects from 
psychological horrors, such as terrifying 
entities, creeping insanity, isolation and 
the existential crisis of being undead. 
 
- 
Magical: Magical Armour protects 
against sorcery and black magic, such 
as malicious spells, words of power and 
the predations of the Archdevils.  
 
DEFENCE 
 
Evasiveness, finesse and combative 
skill. Defending yourself from horrible fuckers 
who keep trying to kill you. 
 
 
Defence is a measure of how capable 
you are of avoiding harm. Whereas Armour 
mitigates the effect of attacks that have already 
hit you, Defence stops you getting hit in the first 
place. When enemies make a Test to try and hit 
you with an attack, your Defence is the number 
they need to roll on their Test in order for it to be 
successful- the higher the better! 
 
Choose a starting value for your 
Defence- either 3+, 4+ or 5+. It’s important to 
note that, the higher your Defence, the lower 
your starting Will and Wounds will be. That’s the 
trade-off for being a slippery fucker! 
 
NECROMAGIC 
 
Spellcasting, sorcery, the power to 
wield badass demonic abilities. Raising hell, 
raising the dead, unleashing horrors beyond 
mortal comprehension. 
 
 
Necromagic is a measure of magical 
potential, and is directly tied to your ability to 
use your character’s Necromagical Powers. The 
more Necromagic you have, the more you will be 
able to draw upon your powers before needing 
to recharge. Your character’s starting 
Necromagic is equal to their Brains Attribute 
multiplied by 3. 
 
SPEED 
 
Speed. How fast you can move. The 
speed at which you traverse. Movement speed. 
What aren’t you getting? 
 
 
Speed is a measure of how quickly your 
character gets around, and can either be Slow, 
Average or Fast. The effects of Speed are 
described in more detail in the “Combat” 
chapter of this book. Your character’s Speed is 
determined by their Guts Attribute. If they have a 
Guts of 1, their Speed is Slow. A Guts of 2 gives 
them a Speed of Average, and a Guts of 3 gives 
them a Speed of Fast. 
 
WILL 
 
Mental fortitude, willpower, fragile grasp 
of sanity. How much psychological punishment 
you can take. 
 
 
Will is your character’s mental ‘health 
bar’, and is a measure of how close you are to 
total insanity. It is equal to 6, minus your 
Defence, plus your Brains Attribute. 
 
WOUNDS 
 
Physical fortitude, constitution, fragile 
grip on this undead coil. How much bodily 
punishment you can take. 
 
 
Wounds are your character’s physical 
‘health bar’, and are a measure of how close you 
are to Second Death. It is equal to 6, minus your 
Defence, plus your Guts Attribute. 
 
 


 
 
Example: Now that Ben has decided on Kizzi’s 
Attributes, he moves on to her Secondaries. First 
off, he has to decide on her Armour. He decides 
that she will start with Magical Armour (5+), 
representing her incorporeal form’s resilience to 
black magic. Moving on to her Defence, he 
decides that she’ll also be quite elusive, 
blending in with the shadows and shimmering 
out of the path of gunfire, and gives her a 
Defence of 5+.  
 
The rest of her Secondaries are predetermined. 
Because Kizzi has a Brains Attribute of 3, her 
Necromagic starts at a very respectable 9 / 9. By 
contrast, her Speed starts off as Slow, as she 
only has a Body Attribute of 1. Her Will is equal 
to 6, minus her Defence of 5+, plus her Brains 
Attribute of 3, giving her a Will of 4 / 4 (6-5+3). 
Finally, her Wounds are equal to 6, minus her 
Defence of 5+, plus her Guts Attribute of 1, giving 
her a Wounds of 2 / 2 (6-5+1). Kizzi’s 
Secondaries are done. Time for Ben to move on 
to the next Step of Character Creation! 
 
Step Four- Edges 
 
Where your character’s Attributes represent 
their raw ability, their Edges represent their 
unique skills, quirks and talents, their personal 
and professional interests, the little things that 
they’re exceptionally good at. A 
Necromusician’s Edges give them just that- an 
Edge in certain situations, or when faced with 
certain challenges or dangers. They are what 
distinguish one Necromusician from another, 
and give each member of the band their niche.  
 
During Character Creation, a Necromusician 
can choose up to four Edges. An Edge can be 
anything you like, but should be fairly specific- a 
niche talent or spark of potential, rather than an 
overarching, all-encompassing thing. As a 
general rule of thumb, an Edge should be: 
 
- 
Specialised: An Edge should be 
focused, centring on a specific skill. For 
example, saying your Necromusician is 
‘Good with Technology’ is not 
specialised enough to be an Edge. 
Saying that they’re ‘A Kickass Music 
Technician’ is. 
 
- 
Evocative: An Edge should be evocative 
and provide an interesting insight into 
your character. For example, saying 
your Necromusician is a ‘Hand-to-Hand 
Fighter’ is sufficiently specialised, but it 
isn’t particularly flavourful. Saying that 
they’re a ‘Hard-Bitten Bare-Knuckle 
Boxer’ is far more interesting, whilst 
keeping the same skillset in mind. 
 
- 
Unique: An Edge should set you apart 
from the pack, not make you a copy of 
someone else’s character. For example, 
if you want your character to be ‘A 
Strong Leader’, but someone else is 
doing something similar, you could get 
creative and instead make your Edge ‘A 
Classically Trained Orator’, ‘Confident 
Military Strategist’, ‘Well-Meaning Team 
Player’ or ‘Spunky Band Mascot’. 
 
EXAMPLE EDGES FOR EACH ATTRIBUTE 
 
GUTS 
- 
Spider-Like Speed Climber 
 
- 
Lethal Martial Artist and Karate Black 
Belt 
 
- 
Eagle-Eyed Rifleman 
 
- 
Hard as Nails in the Face of Torture 
 
- 
Professional Face-Breaker 
 
 
BRAINS 
- 
Trained Sparky and Electrical Engineer 
 
- 
Black Magic Music Tech 
 
- 
Knowledge of Arcane and Mystical 
Languages 
 
- 
University Degree in Applied Biology 
 
- 
Slightly Mad Surgeon 
 
 
SPINE 
- 
Compassionate and Selfless for the 
Needy 
 
- 
Projects an Aura of Confidence 
 
- 
Intimidating Vibe and Scary Tattoos 
 
- 
Loudest Person in the Room 
 
- 
Unflinching Courage on the Brink of 
Death 


 
 
Example: Ben starts to think about what Edges 
Kizzi should have in order to make her unique. 
He wants her to be a capable necromancer, but 
also to have grounded, surface-world skills from 
her life before London’s collapse. He decides on 
the following Edges: 
 
- 
Understands the Old Magic 
- 
Vehicle Repair Skills- Especially 
Motorbikes 
- 
Good at Going Unnoticed When She 
Wants to Be 
- 
Cracking Jokes and Telling Stories 
 
These Edges flesh out Kizzi’s personality, are 
specific enough and make her feel unique in the 
face of other magic-wielding Necromusicians. 
Ben runs them past his GM, Dan, who agrees 
that these are appropriate for Kizzi and doesn’t 
ask Ben to make any changes. Bosh. Job done. 
 
Edges are a chance for you to really set yourself apart 
from the pack. A lotta people can be chatty, but it takes 
a special kind of egotist to be the frontman for a glam 
metal band. Same as a lotta people can be smart, but only 
one or two will know how to fix a Demon Keyboardist’s 
Pain Engine when it’s on the fritz. Above all else, your 
Edges make you you. Ain’t no point in every 
Necromusician playing exactly the same tunes. Plus, down 
here, in this devil-infested maze of dead people and death 
metal, you got a chance to do something totally off the 
wall. What’s the point of living a second unlife and 
choosing to be boring with it? 
 
WRITING ON THE WALL 
 
“Hey. Hey, Tony. I think I’ve got a new song for 
us. Something to really take the band in a radical 
new direction.” 
 
“Nice one. Let’s hear it, then.” 
 
“It needs a bit of polish, but I think this could 
be a real breakthrough for us. It’s like, artistic, 
and meaningful, and will really set us apart from 
all the other bands, y’know?” 
 
“Alright, mate- enough selling. What’s it called?” 
 
“’Ripped Apart by Badgers’” 
Step Five- Necromagical Powers and 
Instrument 
 
Becoming a Necromusician doesn’t come 
without its advantages. The Archdevils have 
grand designs for their servants, designs that 
won’t exactly come to fruition if handled by any 
old newly-dead. Necromusicians are thus 
bestowed with a bewildering array of demonic 
powers, known in the underworld of the Pit and 
the Labyrinth as Necromagic. These powers are 
channelled from the Symphonies of Sickness, 
and are manifested through a Necromusician’s 
Instrument, which mutates and swells with 
power into both a weapon and a tool. 
Necromusicians fuel their Necromagical Powers 
with the stolen essence of their First Death; this 
essence is not infinite, however, and must be 
recharged if expended too rapidly. Unless a 
Necromusician likes the idea of literally burning 
the fabric of their unliving soul. 
 
The first thing you need to do at this Step of 
Character Creation is decide on an Instrument. 
This Instrument is both your musical 
moneymaker, your black magical fetish and a 
deadly semi-sentient living weapon. It’s 
assumed that, whatever your choice, you always 
have access to your Instrument and that you’re 
able to play it pretty damn well. Other than that, 
the details are up to you.  
 
When choosing your Instrument, try to come 
with an evocative name and a brief description 
too. Here are some examples: 
 
- 
Necromangler, Axe of Broken Spines: 
an electric guitar with grisly twists of 
sinew for strings. Doubles as a 
battleaxe that shears through bones as 
cleanly as it shreds through a solo. 
 
- 
Child of Thunder: A drumkit made of 
meteoric iron and bound in livewires. 
Unleashes gouts of static electricity like 
a flamethrower when played and has a 
rumble like an oncoming storm. 
 
- 
The Master Exploder: A microphone 
made out of an Archdevil’s horn. Its 
throat-shredding shrieks can literally 
blow heads apart and shake down the 
foundations of buildings. 
 
Once you’ve chosen your Instrument, it’s time to 
decide on your Necromagical Powers. 


 
 
There are three categories of Necromagical 
Powers in Dead Metal Rhapsody. These are 
Minor, Major and Metal.  
 
MINOR NECROMAGIC 
 
Gnarly and hardcore as fuck, but not so 
brutal as to blow the bloody doors off. Powers 
that are ‘useful’, or are otherwise limited to 
affecting yourself or some other unlucky dude in 
a sick but not earth-shattering way. 
 
Minor Necromagic affects a small handful of 
people, places or objects in relatively modest 
ways- at least, modest for literal devil sorcery. 
Using a Minor Necromagical Power requires you 
to expend one point of Necromagic. 
 
MAJOR NECROMAGIC 
 
Now we’re cooking with gas! Strong 
enough to blast whole rooms, dissolve 
motherfuckers from the inside out or raise gangs 
of undead bikers to break the bones of people 
that look at you funny. 
 
Major Necromagic affects a group of people, a 
medium-sized area or several large objects in a 
pretty substantial fashion- whether devastating 
or debilitating. Using a Major Necromagical 
Power requires you to expend two points of 
Necromagic. 
 
METAL NECROMAGIC 
 
The big leagues. Invocations that can 
summon demonic armies, fill a village with a 
savage wind of plague, snap spines, bang heads 
or hurl an elitist straight down into Hell. Break 
glass in case of emergency. 
 
Metal Necromagic is the big shit, the spells you 
break out when all else has failed or when you 
really need to look like an unstoppable badass. 
Using a Metal Necromagical Power requires you 
to expend three points of Necromagic. 
 
During Character Creation, you get to design 
three Necromagical Powers for your character: 
one Minor, one Major and one Metal. These 
Powers can be anything you can imagine- a 
whole heap of suggestions and examples can be 
found on the next page. Be creative, and channel 
the spirit of heavy metal excess. After all, these 
are the kick-ass powers you sold your Second 
Death for- they sure as hell need to be worth it! 
 
That said, the GM does have the final say on 
whether your Powers are appropriate. Also, 
effects should be narrative, not just mechanical! 
FUCK YOU: A NECROMUSICIAN 
ARGUMENT IN FOUR PARTS 
 
Scene: a dive bar in the back alleys of Skin City. 
“Leprous Decay”, a local revival thrash band, have 
just finished a set. Their lead singer, Ricky 
‘Knuckle-Cutter’ Flash, is having a cold one while 
Big Suz packs away their gear. Suddenly, a drunk 
young corpse comes staggering in. Fuck- he’s got 
a knife! 
 
DRUNK CORPSE (Slurring his Words): You… 
you’re fuckin’… fuckin’ Ricky Flash, aren’t you, 
you fuckin’ FUCK. 
 
He staggers towards him, holding out the knife. 
 
BIG SUZ: Leave it, son. You don’t want none of 
this. Go home. Sleep it off. Just leave it. 
 
DRUNK CORPSE: Shaddup, bitch! I got business 
with Ricky. That… that bastard… I ought to, 
well, I ought to… (He throws up a little, 
spitting out maggots and cheap cider) 
 
RICKY FLASH: You go careful there, mate. Big 
Suz, she don’t like being called names. Gets real 
touchy. Ain’t that right, Suz? 
 
BIG SUZ (Advancing): Last chance, boy. 
There’s usually three of ‘em, but ‘bitch’ cost you 
one. The door’s right there. Be smart. 
 
DRUNK CORPSE: And what? And what? What 
you gonna fuckin’ do, bitch? What you gonna 
fuckin’ do? 
 
Big Suz cracks her knuckles. The air is suddenly 
very cold. 
 
The next morning. The bar’s being cleaned. They 
scrape the Drunk Corpse off the walls. And the 
ceiling. And the floor. 


 
 
Examples of Necromagic 
 
Minor Necromagic 
Bleed from Within: Your spectral hands reach inside your foe and play their internal organs like a 
fretboard, rupturing their innards and doubling them over in a spasm of agony. 
Chord of Crushing: A mighty power chord thunders through the air, resonating through your palms. At a 
touch, you can crush concrete, steel and glass into fragments with pure sonic force. 
Crawling Swarm: Your body disseminates into a squirming, squalling swarm of centipedes, cockroaches 
and spiders that slither across the floor, allowing you to slide into places you couldn’t normally reach. 
Flesh and Metal: A guitar harmony bonds a mechanical device to flesh, fusing the two together in an 
amalgam of steel and skin. 
Wake the Punk-Dead: A rallying call of ‘Oi, Oi, Oi’ and the promise of cheap booze summons a Punk-
Dead from its grave, binding it to your will. 
Whiplash: You bang your head. Your enemy bangs their head. You keep banging. They keep banging. 
You’re in a headbang-off, and the first one to blink gets their neck broken. 
 
Major Necromagic 
Bloodrocuted: You spill your blood, or what’s left of it, to form a river that you then send an electrical 
surge jolting through. All who stand within it are Bloodrocuted. Fuckin’ metal. 
Circle Pit of Sawblades: The ground yawns wide and forms a razor-edged pit that starts to spin. Now 
your foes must contend with circular saws, chainsaws, buzz-blades, knives… whatever’s sharp and spiky. 
Mosh Frenzy: A rapid riff sends you and your allies into a raging thrasher of a frenzy, bolstering your 
speed and your strength. Your movements are also jagged and unpredictable, making it hard to keep up. 
Siren Song of the Counter-Culture: Your song becomes a rallying cry that sways the minds of those 
that hears it, convincing them of the truth of your lyrics and giving you brief mesmeric control over them. 
Tendrils of the Black Abyss: Summons some big primeval tentacles to crush the shit out of people 
that made the mistake of getting on your bad side. Enough said. 
 
Metal Necromagic 
Black Death Horizon: A black sun rises, imbued with all the malice and cruelty of your patron Archdevil. 
Lurching out of the darkness come the Demons of your Infernal Choir. They will aid you… for now. 
Evisceration Plague: Ever wanted to watch a horde of undead vomit out their own intestines? Have I got 
a song for you… 
Satanic Panic: Fear is a powerful weapon, and the fear of the dark is strong within the Labyrinth. Put the 
very terror of the Archdevils into your foes by adopting the guise of your patron Archdevil in illusory glory. 
Testament of Decay: Sometimes it’s not enough to just summon one undead minion. Command a 
legion of walking, shuddering Thrashers by putting some speed (metal) into their shambling bones. 
 
 


 
 
A NOTE ON NECROMAGIC AND TONE 
 
Some of the more experienced role-players 
among you might be asking: “how in the hell can 
Necromagical Powers be balanced?” Surely, 
they must completely warp and destabilise the 
playability of the game. Yes. Yes, they do. But 
Necromagic always comes at a price. You’ll 
need to bear the following things in mind when 
coming up with your Necromagical Powers, and 
considering how to use them when you play 
Dead Metal Rhapsody. It’ll be fun. We promise. 
 
Necromagical Powers are Up for 
Interpretation 
 
None of your Necromagical Powers 
have a hard-and-fast mechanical effect. No ‘roll 
8D6 Fire Damage in a 20-foot sphere’ effects 
over here! Instead, whenever you use a 
Necromagical Power, you, your fellow players 
and your GM are responsible for working 
together to come up with the cool, badass effect 
it has on the situation at hand. Your powers 
should always feel good to use, but, with a little 
bit of give and take, should never trivialise the 
story. Let players have their moments of glory- 
but don’t let every moment be 100% glorious. 
Roleplaying’s a co-operative experience, after 
all, where it’s everyone’s job to make sure 
everyone else is having fun! 
 
Necromagical Powers Cost Necromagic   
 
Minor Powers cost 1 Necromagic, Major 
Powers 2 and Metal Powers a heavy 3. That 
means even a Necromusician with a Brains 
Attribute of 3 can only use their most powerful 
ability three times out of the gate before needing 
to recharge. Recharging is explained later in this 
book, but essentially involves seeking out a 
source of Necromagic and feeding off of it, or 
performing a service for your Archdevil to get 
your strength back. It’s never just free. As such, 
using a power will always take just as much as it 
gives. By all means, revel in that Apocalyptic 
Corpse Explosion you’ve just pulled off- but 
remember you’re going to be paying it back, at 
some point down the line… 
 
You’re Not the Only Necromusicians Down 
Here! 
 
You think you’re special because you 
made a deal with the devil? Don’t make me 
laugh! That applies to, like, half the undead 
population of London. You might get to chuck 
around awesome Necromagical Powers, but so 
does everyone else. The Demons and Archdevils 
even get to draw on the Symphonies of Sickness 
directly. What I’m trying to say is that this ain’t a 
scene- it’s a god damn arms race. As powerful 
as you are, there’ll always be a bigger fish, just 
waiting to catch you out and ruin your day. 
 
Necromagical Powers are Supposed to be Big, 
Over-The-Top and Crazy 
 
Dead Metal Rhapsody is designed with 
all the balls-to-the-wall intensity of classic 
heavy metal, all the over-the-top pizzazz, 
explosions, larger-than-life characters and 
ridiculous brutality. Necromagical Powers are a 
chance to really lean into that, to channel your 
inner undead rockstar. If all of your players 
accept that, if your GM accepts that, and you 
embrace the tone of the game, you won’t go far 
wrong. Remember, it’s not a competition. No-
one’s here to win. You’re here to tell the stories 
of doomed badasses struggling against demon 
possession in the subterranean ruins of Heaven 
and Hell. Have fun with it. Embrace it. Your 
games will be better for it. 
 
 
FROM THE SPELLBOOK OF ANABELLE 
‘BEL-SHAMHORATH IV’ SPENCER, 
CHAPTER III: “FUN SPELLS TO DO IN 
THE DARK” 
 
Curse of Impotency 
Bragging and overconfidence got you down? Never 
fear- he won’t be talking so big when it turns into 
dust. 
 
Flames of the End 
When asked for a lighter in the Burn-Lin Sulphur 
Club, don’t just pull a zippo. Girls love girls who 
can set their lipstick on fire. 
 
Brutali-tea 
I used to drink whiskey and coke before 
discovering this nifty little number. One sip, and 
you’ll be growling so gutturally that the Labyrinth 
itself will shake. 
 
Apocalyptic Corpse Explosion 
Does what it says on the tin. Bring a body bag. 
Sanitising products optional. 


 
 
Step Six- Signature Gear and Drachma 
 
Unlike some other tabletop RPGs, Dead Metal
