roleplaying games to explore fantastical 
worlds and take on the roles of warriors, 
wizards and wonderful weirdos. Most of 
us don’t play to have our arcing 
overhead guitar swing be reduced to 
pure, functional numbers. When using 
clocks, make sure they don’t replace 
the fiction of the story around you. 
Don’t outright say ‘gas clock ticks to 
three’- say ‘the gas billows, and now is 
halfway up your throat!” Obviously, be 
clear about mechanics, but don’t 
sacrifice immersion and storytelling for 
them. Travel mechanics are particularly 
egregious for this- don’t spoil the fun of 
exploring a jungle by making it a series 
of rolls with no engaging storytelling to 
accompany them! 
 
3. Be Flexible: Like everything in a 
roleplaying game, clocks work best 
when you’re willing to be flexible and 
allow for on-the-fly adjustments that 
make the game more fun. You’re here to 
have a good time, right? Ask yourself 
honestly- would following all the rules 
110% to the letter make the game 
better, or worse? Do you need to strictly 
adhere to that clock you set, or could 
you skip a step if the outcome is a moot 
point or wouldn’t really change? Don’t 
hold yourself rigidly to clocks if they’re 
not adding anything to the scene! 
 
 


 
 
The stage was alive with fire that night. 
 
The torches burned, and sulphur filled the 
air alongside the roar of her guitar. 
 
When she played, it was as if all the rage 
of Hell was in her. A righteous anger, 
channelled and focused, at all the 
injustice of the world. 
 
I’ve never felt anything like it since. 
 
It made me feel like I belonged. 


 
 
Evil Spells 
Necromagic in the World of Dead Metal Rhapsody 
 
Behind the veil of creation, like a steady 
heartbeat, pulse the Music of the Spheres. It is 
from this divine song that God Herself was born, 
and it flows through reality as the lifeblood of 
being and time. Its notes are inaudible to 
mortals, angels and demons alike, yet they 
resonate within us and touch every aspect of 
our lives. To master the Music of the Spheres 
would be to master every aspect of the world in 
the totality of its past, present and future. Only 
God ever wielded that kind of power. And it’s 
likely than the Music of the Spheres, in all of its 
impossible beauty and complexity, was that 
which drove her insane. 
 
The angels who served under God were 
attuned to emanations of the Music of the 
Spheres, like ripples reflected and refracted 
through the mirror of a still pool. Even diluted, 
the power this music afforded them was 
intoxicating. At an angel’s touch, the world bent 
and broke, reshaping to their will like clay in a 
sculptor’s palm. The demons, by contrast, stole 
their power from echoes of the Music heard in 
the absent void of space. This Diabolus In 
Musica is a perversity, an aberration. It is the 
source of Necromagic. It is the source of all 
Necromusicians’ power. 
 
What is Necromagic? 
 
 
In exchange for their Second Death, and 
for possession of their eternal soul, a 
Necromusician is gifted with the power to 
channel the Diabolus In Musica through 
Necromagic. Necromagic is a catch-all term for 
the esoteric, horrifying and metal-as-fuck 
sorceries wielded by Necromusicians of all 
stripes. It’s not just necromancy- it’s anything 
that channels the anarchic spirit of metal, rock, 
punk and hardcore music, suffused with 
demonic patronage and channelled through 
guitars made of Hell Steel, flesh and bones. 
 
 
In game terms, all of the players’ 
Necromusicians will have the ability to use 
Necromagical Powers, derived from their patron 
Archdevil. These Powers range in potency from 
Minor, to Major, to Metal, and the only limit to 
them is your players’ imagination. Seems 
unfair? Seems unbalanced? Well, here’s the 
catch. Necromagic is common as fuck in the Pit, 
and, as such, NPCs and adversaries will use it 
too! You can find some examples in the Bestiary 
later in this book of enemies that use 
Necromagic- for now, let’s dive a bit deeper into 
how Necromagic applies to your players, and 
how it’s used in-game. 
 
The Necromagic Secondary 
 
 
All characters in Dead Metal Rhapsody 
that can use Necromagic have a Necromagic 
Secondary, which for Player Characters (PCs) 
starts at a value equal to their Brains Attribute 
multiplied by 3. This gives any given PC a 
starting Necromagic of 3, 6 or 9, a range from 
‘death metal dabbler’ all the way up to ‘scholar 
of the metal arts’ and beyond. 
 
 
Whenever a character wishes to use a 
Necromagical Power, they must spend the 
appropriate amount of Necromagic depending 
on the strength of the Power. For example, a 
Minor Necromagical Power costs 1 Necromagic 
to invoke- a Metal Power instead costs a full 3! If 
a character does not have enough Necromagic 
remaining to use a Power, they cannot do so 
until they recover some Necromagic points. 
Note that a character’s Necromagic Secondary 
can never rise above its starting value, and can 
never fall below 0. 
 
 
Recovering Necromagic 
 
 
Recovering Necromagic is not without 
its costs. Necromagic derives directly from an 
Archdevil’s patronage- it is they who control its 
ebb and flow. PCs can recover Necromagic in a 
variety of ways, which are as follows: 
 
 
 
 
 


 
 
Minor Recovery (+1 Necromagic) 
 
- 
Blood Sacrifice: By offering up their 
flesh to their Archdevil patron, a 
Necromusician can derive new power 
from spilled blood. Once per day, a 
Necromusician can voluntarily lose D3 
Wounds in a ritualistic blood sacrifice- 
doing so restores 1 point of 
Necromagic. 
 
- 
Embrace the Madness: The mind is a 
terrible thing to waste, but insanity is a 
potent source of power for those willing 
to become unhinged. Once per day, a 
Necromusician can voluntarily lose D3 
Will in a ceremony of smoke and 
madness- doing so restores 1 point of 
Necromagic. 
 
- 
Minor Act of Service: By devoting 
themselves to the will of their Archdevil 
patron, Necromusicians can beseech 
them to bestow more magical energy. 
Completing a Minor Act of Service for 
their Archdevil restores 1 point of 
Necromagic- examples of Minor Acts of 
Service are as follows: 
o 
Exploring a dangerous area of 
the Labyrinth. 
o 
Seizing a Black Market Artefact 
o 
Slaying a troublesome foe or 
rival 
o 
Carrying out a ritual or 
ceremony in their name. 
o 
Dedicating your new single to 
your patron (hey, sycophancy 
works!) 
 
Major Recovery (+2 Necromagic) 
- 
Pact of Sacrifice: Archdevils subsist on 
a diet of mortal souls; by sacrificing the 
soul of an innocent, a Necromusician 
can regain a substantial amount of their 
power. Sacrificing a suitable victim to 
an Archdevil restores 2 points of 
Necromagic. 
 
- 
Major Act of Service: By devoting 
themselves to the will of their Archdevil 
patron, Necromusicians can beseech 
them to bestow more magical energy. 
Completing a Major Act of Service for 
their Archdevil restores 2 points of 
Necromagic- examples of Major Acts of 
Service are as follows: 
o 
Delving into hostile territory 
with a high chance of injury, 
maiming or Second Death. 
o 
Destroying a rival cult or band 
of Necromusicians. 
o 
Gathering a set of artefacts for 
use in a potent act of sorcery. 
o 
Deconsecrating a former 
temple of Heaven. 
o 
Dedicating your new album to 
your patron (hey, if it works for 
singles, it works even better for 
albums!) 
 
Metal Recovery (+3 Necromagic) 
- 
Metal Act of Service: By devoting 
themselves to the will of their Archdevil 
patron, Necromusicians can beseech 
them to bestow more magical energy. 
Completing a Metal Act of Service for 
their Archdevil restores 3 points of 
Necromagic- examples of Metal Acts of 
Service are as follows: 
o 
Venturing into the ruins of 
Heaven to seek a powerful 
Black Magic Artefact. 
o 
Destroying a demon or angel of 
considerable power that 
opposes your Archdevil’s will. 
o 
A mass sacrifice or esoteric 
ritual ceremony in the name of 
your patron. 
o 
Doing something 
apocalyptically dangerous or 
stupid to please your patron. 
o 
Dedicating your firstborn child 
to your patron (difficult for the 
Undead, but adoption’s always 
an option for really dedicated 
satanists).  
 
“Bill, I refuse to call the new album 
‘The Fuckening, Pt. 2: Electric Fuck-A-Loo’. 
We’re the goddamn Venom Crucible for Hell’s 
sake, not some pissing Steel Panther tribute act.” 
 
“Frankie. Frankie, listen to me. We ain’t got a 
choice. We’re down in the seventh circle 
tomorrow, and I ain’t got any juice. The 
Archdevil said he’s gonna give me what I need. 
So, get down off your high horse, accept that it’s 
what this shit will be called, and let’s get on, 
eh?” 


 
 
Necromagical Powers 
As a reminder from Character Creation, 
there are three categories of Necromagical 
Powers in Dead Metal Rhapsody. These are 
Minor, Major and Metal.  
 
MINOR NECROMAGIC 
 
Gnarly and hardcore as fuck, but not so 
brutal as to blow the bloody doors off. Powers 
that are ‘useful’, or are otherwise limited to 
affecting yourself or some other unlucky dude in 
a sick but not earth-shattering way. 
 
Minor Necromagic affects a small handful of 
people, places or objects in relatively modest 
ways- at least, modest for literal devil sorcery. 
Using a Minor Necromagical Power requires you 
to expend one point of Necromagic. 
 
MAJOR NECROMAGIC 
 
Now we’re cooking with gas! Strong 
enough to blast whole rooms, dissolve 
motherfuckers from the inside out or raise gangs 
of undead bikers to break the bones of people 
that look at you funny. 
 
Major Necromagic affects a group of people, a 
medium-sized area or several large objects in a 
pretty substantial fashion- whether devastating 
or debilitating. Using a Major Necromagical 
Power requires you to expend two points of 
Necromagic. 
 
METAL NECROMAGIC 
 
The big leagues. Invocations that can 
summon demonic armies, fill a village with a 
savage wind of plague, snap spines, bang heads 
or hurl an elitist straight down into Hell. Break 
glass in case of emergency. 
 
Metal Necromagic is the big shit, the spells you 
break out when all else has failed or when you 
really need to look like an unstoppable badass. 
Using a Metal Necromagical Power requires you 
to expend three points of Necromagic. 
 
 
God of thunder, bitch! 
Hear me roar. 
Storm clouds on the horizon, 
Gonna knock down the fuckin’ door. 
- 
Incantation of Unlocking (Suck It, D&D) 
 
As another reminder from Character 
Creation, here are the guidelines on tone, style 
and how to balance Necromagical Powers for 
your campaigns. Sorry for repeating, but it’s for 
ease of reference. It’ll all make sense in the 
index. Promise. 
 
Some of the more experienced role-
players among you might be asking: “how in the 
hell can Necromagical Powers be balanced?” 
Surely, they must completely warp and 
destabilise the playability of the game. Yes. Yes, 
they do. But Necromagic always comes at a 
price. You’ll need to bear the following things in 
mind when coming up with your Necromagical 
Powers, and considering how to use them when 
you play Dead Metal Rhapsody. It’ll be fun. We 
promise. 
 
Necromagical Powers are Up for 
Interpretation 
 
None of your Necromagical Powers 
have a hard-and-fast mechanical effect. No ‘roll 
8D6 Fire Damage in a 20-foot sphere’ effects 
over here! Instead, whenever you use a 
Necromagical Power, you, your fellow players 
and your GM are responsible for working 
together to come up with the cool, badass effect 
it has on the situation at hand. Your powers 
should always feel good to use, but, with a little 
bit of give and take, should never trivialise the 
story. Let players have their moments of glory- 
but don’t let every moment be 100% glorious. 
Roleplaying’s a co-operative experience, after 
all, where it’s everyone’s job to make sure 
everyone else is having fun! 
 
Necromagical Powers Cost Necromagic   
 
Minor Powers cost 1 Necromagic, Major 
Powers 2 and Metal Powers a heavy 3. That 
means even a Necromusician with a Brains 
Attribute of 3 can only use their most powerful 
ability three times out of the gate before needing 
to recharge. Recharging is explained later in this 
book, but essentially involves seeking out a 
source of Necromagic and feeding off of it, or 
performing a service for your Archdevil to get 
your strength back. It’s never just free. As such, 
using a power will always take just as much as it 
gives. By all means, revel in that Apocalyptic 
Corpse Explosion you’ve just pulled off- but 
remember you’re going to be paying it back, at 
some point down the line… 
 
 
 


 
 
You’re Not the Only Necromusicians Down 
Here! 
 
You think you’re special because you 
made a deal with the devil? Don’t make me 
laugh! That applies to, like, half the undead 
population of London. You might get to chuck 
around awesome Necromagical Powers, but so 
does everyone else. The Demons and Archdevils 
even get to draw on the Symphonies of Sickness 
directly. What I’m trying to say is that this ain’t a 
scene- it’s a god damn arms race. As powerful 
as you are, there’ll always be a bigger fish, just 
waiting to catch you out and ruin your day. 
 
Necromagical Powers are Supposed to be Big, 
Over-The-Top and Crazy 
 
Dead Metal Rhapsody is designed with 
all the balls-to-the-wall intensity of classic 
heavy metal, all the over-the-top pizzazz, 
explosions, larger-than-life characters and 
ridiculous brutality. Necromagical Powers are a 
chance to really lean into that, to channel your 
inner undead rockstar. If all of your players 
accept that, if your GM accepts that, and you 
embrace the tone of the game, you won’t go far 
wrong. Remember, it’s not a competition. No-
one’s here to win. You’re here to tell the stories 
of doomed badasses struggling against demon 
possession in the subterranean ruins of Heaven 
and Hell. Have fun with it. Embrace it. Your 
games will be better for it. 
 
 
Top Tips for GMs (and Players) on 
Using Necromagic in Your Games 
 
1. Embrace the Chaos- but Recognise 
that Chaos has Limits: As a system, 
Necromagic is designed to be anarchic 
and empowering, enabling your players 
to channel the spirit of heavy metal into 
a rockstar power fantasy of epic 
proportions. That means that it’s not 
going to be balanced, and it’s not 
always going to be easy to adjudicate. 
You need to work with your players to 
ensure that their Necromagical Powers 
are injecting the right amount of 
zaniness and chaotic energy into your 
games- that means over-the-top 
enough to be exciting, but not so game-
breakingly ridiculous that everything 
becomes trivial. There’s a social 
contract at play here. 
 
2. When in Doubt, Use the Rule of Cool: 
If you’re not sure what the impact of a 
Necromagical Power should be, ask 
yourself this- what would be the most 
awesome and, above all, fun thing for it 
to do? This applies to the players AND 
to your Necromagical NPCs. Powers 
should make the game more enjoyable 
to play, introducing spectacle, heart-
pounding danger, encouraging balls-to-
the-wall risk taking and throwing 
yourselves in harms’ way. Remember- 
‘cool’ doesn’t mean ‘solves every 
problem’, nor does it mean 
‘overshadows everyone else’. There 
needs to be a balance. Talk to all your 
players when a Power comes on screen. 
See what they think it should do. 
 
3. Do No Harm, and Take No Shit: When 
you’re the one wielding the Necromagic 
against your players, it can be tempting 
to unleash fire and brimstone and wipe 
them off the face of the planet. Resist 
that temptation, slugger! A GM should 
use Necromagical Powers to introduce 
exciting new challenges and put your 
players on edge. They should be things 
the players can overcome, resist or 
work together to change. Equally, 
though, don’t pussyfoot about and pull 
your punches. Sometimes, a big fuckin’ 
danger is necessary to challenge a big 
fuckin’ hero. 
 
4. Let Your Players Do Cool Things: 
Roleplaying games are about having 
fun. That fun can take a variety of forms, 
of course. Whether it be a hard-boiled 
investigation game where the fun 
comes from solving clues, or an 
emotional, cathartic experience 
exploring identity in a hungry blade 
lesbians (please don’t sue me) kind of 
way, or even just by twatting a goblin 
with a mace- these are all fun parts of 
roleplay from different people’s 
perspectives, and that’s to be 
celebrated. But they all have one thing 
in common- players get to do cool 
things. Even games that purposefully 
disempower your characters still give 
them cool quirks, or talents, or things 
that make them unique. In Dead Metal 
Rhapsody, Necromagic is one of those 
cool things. Don’t take the chance to be 
cool away from your players. Bring 


 
 
people up- don’t bash them down. It’s 
easy as a GM to worry about things like 
balance and challenge and pacing, but 
just step back and ask yourself this: are 
your players enjoying the experience? If 
the answer’s yes, keep doing what 
you’re doing. And keep letting your 
players have the chance to be amazing. 
 
5. Let Yourself Do Cool Things: People 
sometimes forget that the GM in a 
roleplaying game is just as much a 
player as, well, the players. They’re also 
there to have fun and enjoy the game- 
NOT just to facilitate whatever the 
players want, and bend over backwards 
to baby them like some spoiled players 
on Reddit seem to think. As such, use 
Necromagic as a chance for your NPCs 
and story to shine. It’s a big part of the 
world of Dead Metal Rhapsody, and 
should tie in to the stories you’re trying 
to tell. Go for it. Jump right in. Double-
fist that shit. Sometimes, it’s OK for the 
GM to enjoy things just as much as the 
players. Fuck me, right? Who knew? You 
might be there to make sure they have 
fun, but don’t forget about yourself in 
the process. 
 
6. Always Use Necromagic 
Responsibility, i.e. in the Spirit of the 
Game: Let’s be absolutely clear about 
the ‘Spirit of the Game’ here. The Spirit 
of the Game means acting in a way 
that’s conducive to telling an exciting, 
engaging and emotional story that is 
satisfying for all the players involved. 
That means bucking up, and acting like 
a mature, self-regulating person. DON’T 
let your players use Necromagic as an 
excuse to trivialise the story. DON’T let 
them marginalise or overshadow other 
players by always being the one to steal 
the show. DON’T let every challenge be 
solved by ‘and then we use this 
Power…”. And DON’T let your players 
take the piss. Some people, if you give 
them an inch, will take a mile. Set out 
clear expectations (more on that in the 
GM’s section later!) and make sure that 
your players fully understand what 
Necromagical Powers are supposed to 
be for. 
 
 
The darkness sweeps back from 
the blood that soaks the stone. 
She holds his still-beating heart 
aloft, staring in desperation into 
the fog. She waits, intoning the 
prayers the priests taught her, 
though she knows not what the 
words even mean. 
 
Something moves. A shadow. 
Alive and out of sight. 
 
It speaks her name. 
 
“You offer us the life of one 
who was innocent. His love had no 
device. He was intent on no evil. 
And yet his life was the one you 
chose to take.” 
 
“I chose my own path.” Her 
voice is unsteady. “I would bargain 
with you, demon. I seek your 
power, in exchange for this gift.” 
 
“And you shall have it.” The 
shadow boils. She sees the outline 
of grinning mouths, eyeballs licked 
with livid tongues- and then the 
magic is in her. 


 
 
The Grimoire of Sally ‘Slaughterborn’ Mac 
Carmarthen 
 
Spells, Incantations and Rituals for the Aspiring Necro-
Witch 
(Keep Out, Mum- and Fuck You, Dad) 
 
The Grimoire of Sally ‘Slaughterborn’ Mac Carmarthen (aged 19 ½ ) is one of the most 
potent tomes of Necromagical lore known to man. Written on the flayed skin of her 
closest friends, the words of Sally Slaughterborn are inked in blood and resonate with 
unearthly, horrifying power. The Grimoire itself appears in the night to those that speak 
aloud its true name, an incomprehensible mess of Enochian that burns the tongue and 
sears the senses, driving all but the most devoted necromancers utterly insane. Sally 
Slaughterborn herself was… a troubled child. Raised into the ranks of the dead following 
London’s fall, she had already established for herself a grim reputation as 
Whitechapel’s new Spring-Heeled Jack, having murdered no less than twelve 
investment bankers by the age of fourteen. Some viewed this as a public service; some 
were disturbed by the vile inscriptions she carved into them with her parents’ butter 
knife. A violent and deranged self-professed Necro-Witch, Sally Slaughterborn now 
dwells somewhere deep in the Labyrinth, collecting the souls of those foolish enough to 
use her Grimoire and succumb to the black magic within. But hey, you’re not so foolish 
as it read it. You’re not going to delve into the words of a lunatic, a madwoman and a 
murderer? Her insane, crawling ramblings, like spiders in your mind? 
 
Are you? 
 
 
The Grimoire of Sally ‘Slaughterborn’ Mac Carmarthen (aged 19 ½ ) is intended as a 
representative list of Necromagical Powers, ranging from the Minor, to the Major, and finally to 
the Metal. Feel free to use the Powers listed here in your own games of Dead Metal Rhapsody, or 
to use them as inspiration to design your own Necromagical Powers- the choice is yours! 
 
Each Power comes with a description and an associated Infernal Choir, i.e. the type of Archdevil 
most likely to bestow such a Power on its servants. The effects of each Power are broadly 
described- players are encouraged to get creative as to how they interpret each Power, as that’s 
half the fun of being a necromantic rockstar. Just… try not to go insane reading them, yeah? 
That’s how Sally gets your soul… 
 


 
 
Litany I: Minor Necromagical Incantations 
 
Empty Inside 
 
The Necromusician reaches out a hand and twists, temporarily banishing all of 
their victim’s internal organs from existence. The resulting confusion, and agonising 
pain, leaves them stricken as a bag of meat and jelly for a few moments before their 
organs return (The Way of All Flesh) 
 
Unholy Confessions 
 
The Necromusician delves into another’s mind in a storm of whispers, exposing 
their deepest fears and darkest secrets. The victim is compelled to spill forth their own 
personal history of wickedness, leaving aside no detail, and can have hidden 
information juiced from them like water from a sponge (Legion of the Night) 
 
Necrophobic 
 
Primal, animal terror is one hell of a drug, and one that an undead’s body does 
not inure them to. This Power opens the taps to all the horror, insecurity and doubt 
inside a victim’s brain. The shock of this is enough to snap anyone’ s fragile grasp on 
sanity and cause them to quake at the smallest shadow (Cult of the Frozen Moon) 
 
Demonic Science 
 
To fuse flesh and machine requires rite and ritual, appropriate sacrifices of 
virginal blood and motor oil and prayers upon the altar of speed. This incantation binds 
together demonically-possessed Hell Steel and flesh, creating cyborg amalgams of the 
undead and the infernal, bound to the will of their creator (Engine of Destruction) 
 
Heartwork 
 
Imagine a beating heart, slow and rhythmic, pulsing with blood and muscle. Now 
kickstart that heart into overdrive. With a shot of sorcerous adrenaline, the target of this 
invocation is fuelled by fire and desire, bursting with energy until the inevitable burnout- 
likely in an alleyway, half-drunk, two or three days later (Corpse Crusaders) 
 
Ov Fire and the Void 
 
There is much to be learned of the future and past from the darkness between 
the stars. This prophetic hymnal invokes the mysteries of heaven and earth, providing 
fleeting visions of what could come to pass in fragmentary visions, flickers of motion, 
and moving images in static and gore (Apokalypsis) 
 
Fucking Hostile 
Make someone really fucking angry. Like, REALLY fucking angry. Angry enough to 
chew through steel, and punch someone’s skull out.  ‘nuff said (Angels of Death) 


 
 
Litany II: Major Necromagical Incantations 
 
Symphony of Destruction 
The hated edifices of civilisation hold us back from our primal birthright; better to 
let them all crumble to dust. With a word of command, the Necromusician unbinds the 
very fabric of artifice around them, casting down buildings in a savage blast radius of 
broken stone and glass (Cult of the Frozen Moon) 
 
Blood Fire Death 
Sometimes, the solution to every problem is a raging ball of fire. And boiling 
blood, razor blades, excessive amounts of barbed wire and some more fire, as a treat, 
on the side. This spell is as unsubtle as they get, but, hey, sometimes you just need 
something to really fucking die (Angels of Death) 
 
Hail and Kill 
 
Loyalty is hard to come by in a cutthroat world. This incantation raises the souls 
of ancient warriors, bound together in oaths of warrior creed and brotherhood, and 
imbues them with the fighting spirit of a stadium full of metalheads. Together, as a 
warped battalion, they fight alongside their summoner (Corpse Crusaders) 
 
Run Silent, Run Deep 
 
Garbing themselves in a choking mist, the Necromusician slips out of reality and 
enters a dreamlike realm of mirrors. Whilst inside this liminal space, the constraints of 
the physical world no longer apply. They may glide through solid rock, inhabit the bodies 
of passers-by like a ghost, and remain invisible to the naked eye (Apokalypsis) 
 
Needled 24/7 
 
The ground bursts open. Jets of spikes erupt, powered by demonic pistons and 
emitting gouts of steam. Anything impaled on these spikes is hooked by living barbs of 
bone and flesh (Engine of Destruction) 
 
Pierced from Within 
 
Almost always fatal to the living, and agonising to the dead, this invocation fills 
the victim’s bloodstream with razor-edged guitar plectrums and drumsticks sharpened 
to vicious points. With the strum of a power chord, these implements erupt out of the 
victim’s skin, spraying their surroundings in hideous rents of blood (The Way of All Flesh) 
 
Cry of the Unheard 
 
This incantation inspires those who hear it: a rallying cry against oppression, 
inuring those that hear it to fear and doubt. And perhaps convincing them to sacrifice a 
little more of themselves to protect the one who spoke its words… (Legion of the Night) 


 
 
Litany III: Metal Necromagical Incantations 
 
Slaughter of the Soul 
Second Death for those bound to the Archdevils results in the destruction or 
imprisonment of their very soul; for those without this binding, their soul wanders free, 
and may yet be reborn. Not so with this incantation- at the moment of death, the 
Necromusician utterly obliterates all that remains of their victim’s being (Apokalypsis) 
 
Destroy Erase Improve 
 
The flesh of man is weak, vulnerable and, above all, painfully mortal. This 
invocation replaces fragility with the strength and endurance of steel. A tide of 
spasming, biomechanical mutation washes over those the Necromusician wishes to 
imbue with horrific strength and cold, clinical, unfeeling logic (Engine of Destruction) 
 
Black Hole Sun 
 
For when swords don’t cut it. Tear open a rift in the fucking space-time 
continuum. Bring forth a black hole of swirling, malicious energy that consumes all 
matter it touches. Live the dream of basking in an event horizon of horror before the last 
of the light is snuffed out. Fuckin’ metal (Corpse Crusaders) 
 
Winds of Plague 
 
The Necromusician is born aloft on a tide of green gas and chattering insects, 
carried forth in a raging tide that spews bile and disease. There is no shelter from this 
carnivorous swarm, which flows like water under doorways and through cracks, 
infecting its victims even as it shields its master from harm (Cult of the Frozen Moon) 
 
Painkiller 
 
With a terrifying scream, the limits of the mortal body are unbound in a rush of 
adrenaline, high-octane flames and sheer bloody-minded force of will. Those who 
scream along find their bodies becoming inhumanly resilient, able to shrug off blows 
that would fell even a greater demon with ease (Angels of Death) 
 
My Curse 
 
The fate of one becomes the fate of all. Like a living voodoo doll, the 
Necromusician entwines their being with those they wish to curse, reflecting any harm 
and suffering they experience upon them. Like voodoo pins, the Necromusician can 
mutilate themselves to inflict the same mutilation on their victims (Legion of the Night) 
 
 
 
 


 
 
 
“You know what I hate, more than anything? It’s when little bitches like 
you- pathetic, fragile little man-babies, pissing and mewling in the dark- 
think they have any hope in HELL.” 
 
She stands with her foot on the wreckage of the London Eye. Its glass 
capsule has ruptured, spilling out wires and struts of metal like the carcass 
of some grotesque insect. Desiccated corpses, skeletal remains, lie strewn in 
the rubble. It must have been a hell of a view, to ride the wheel down, to 
plunge into the Pit with the Thames rushing below you, as the massive 
structure heaved, roared and- finally- dashed you into meat and gore 
against the earth. 
 
He struggles in their grip. Knotted tendrils, shod in scabrous, leathery hide, 
wrap around his wrists and feet. His muscles burn. What’s left of them. 
Tatters of skin, flensed under his denim jacket. Patches and chains, 
spattered in blood. He spits out a tooth. She smirks. Twiddles the laces of 
her knee-high boots. Each of her teeth has been filed down to a razor-
sharp point, and carved with heinous runes. He can’t help but smile. She 
looked so human. Didn’t even seem like she was dead. Much less… 
well… 
 
Like this. 
 
“You’re going to die now, pretty boy. You’re going to die, and all those 
gym sessions, all that preening, all your big swinging dick energy and cheap 
hairspray, that ain’t gonna matter worth shit. You’re going to die like a 
bitch, face-down in the mud, and I’m going to pick your limbs off like a kid 
tearing the legs off a fly.” 
 
The tentacles squirm. She grins wider. There’s a crack. Bones start to break 


 
 
Peace Sells (But Who’s Buying?) 
Gear, Trinkets and Magical Items 
 
Tuning Up, or “How to Buy More 
Cool Shit” 
 
 
So, you’re a successful Necromusician 
and have just grabbed your first haul out of the 
Labyrinth. Congratulations- you’re still 
somehow barely scraping together cash for beer 
and cigarettes, much less than fancy new guitar. 
You need to know how to make money, how the 
rich get richer, and, most importantly, what you 
can spend all that cash on.  
 
Drachma Level 
 
 
As a reminder, Dead Metal Rhapsody 
doesn’t expect players to track every single coin 
in their possession. Instead, a Necromusician’s 
spending power is tracked by their Drachma 
Level. There are five Levels of Drachma, which 
are as follows: 
 
- 
Skint: A Necromusician who’s Skint is 
barely making ends meet, living from gig 
to gig on a heady mix of pot noodles, 
friends’ mattresses and cheap beer. A 
Skint Necromusician can only afford 
the most basic of necessities and a 
pretty barebones lifestyle; you likely live 
in a crummy bedsit or sofa-surf week to 
week, and have to take odd jobs to fill 
out the income when the band shit 
doesn’t cut it. A Skint Necromusician 
can only regularly afford Items with an 
Expense of ‘Cheap’ or less. They can 
afford up to three ‘Average’ items and 
only one ‘Costly’ item per Session. They 
cannot afford ‘Expensive’ or ‘Luxury’ 
items at all! 
 
- 
Getting By: A Necromusician who’s 
Getting By has enough of an income to 
afford their own place and get by well 
enough, minus a few scrapes here and 
there. They make enough to live 
comfortably, buy decent food and have 
some hobbies and pastimes on the 
side; they likely live in a small 
apartment or share a house 
somewhere, and earn enough money 
from their band that only the occasional 
side hustle is necessary. A Getting By 
Necromusician can regularly afford 
items with an Expense of ‘Cheap’ or 
‘Average’. They can afford up to three 
‘Costly’ items and only one ‘Expensive’ 
item per Session. They cannot afford 
‘Luxury’ items at all! 
 
- 
Well-Off: A Necromusician who’s Well-
Off is doing pretty damn good, having 
enough juice to pay their way through 
the finer things in a comfortable life. 
They can afford to indulge some 
expensive tastes and a handful of 
luxuries; they likely live in a house of 
their own, or a quality apartment in one 
of the nicer districts of Skin City. Side 
jobs? Forget that shit! A Well-Off 
Necromusician can regularly afford 
items with an Expense of ‘Cheap’, 
‘Average’ or ‘Costly’. They can afford up 
to three ‘Expensive’ items and only one 
‘Luxury’ item per Session. 
 
- 
Rich: A Necromusician who’s Rich has 
made it to the big leagues, baby. They 
have cash in hand, and enough reckless 
self-assurance to be able to spend it. 
They can get into exclusive clubs, bribe 
their way past bouncers, cash in on 
pricey instruments and get explosively 
high with the A-Listers when they feel 
like going to town. They likely live in a 
luxury home, or maybe have multiple 
apartments they flit between. A Rich 
Necromusician can regularly afford 
items with an Expense of ‘Cheap’, 
‘Average’, ‘Costly’ and ‘Expensive’. They 


 
 
can afford up to three ‘Luxury’ items per 
Session. 
 
- 
Stupid Rich: A Necromusician who’s 
Stupid Rich has more cash than sense. 
They have multiple homes, designer 
wardrobes, whole rooms full of shiny 
cars and guitars and… profitable 
investments (yuck). Their homes are 
luxurious and more than a little showy. 
A Stupid Rich Necromusician can 
regularly afford any items they want, 
including ‘Luxury’ items. 
 
A Necromusician’s Drachma Level is a 
rough aggregate of the cash in their pocket, as 
well as any assets or other financial resources at 
their disposal. Either you’ve got a manor house, 
six investment portfolios and a bar of 
Heavensgold, or you’ve got a fiver, a half-empty 
can of Stella and some smokes. Bummer. You 
get to decide what your Necromusician’s 
Drachma Level looks like. It’s more of a 
roleplaying thing than a hard-and-fast rule. Just 
try to make it make sense! 
 
After all, your Skint fresh-faced bassist ain’t 
gonna be lugging around a crate of Godflesh and 
diamonds, is he? 
 
Acquiring New Items 
 
 
All Items in Dead Metal Rhapsody have 
an Expense level. This represents how difficult 
the Item is to acquire, and how much of a price 
it typically demands. The Expense levels are as 
follows: 
 
- 
Cheap: Cheap Items are everyday bits 
and pieces, stuff you wouldn’t give half 
a glance unless you were really on the 
financial ropes. Cheap Items include a 
case of Motorhead Beer, a pack of 
Corpse-Ground Rolling Tobacco, a 
quick ‘n’ dirty meal at a run-down local 
diner, or an ill-fitting band t-shirt from 
the bootlegger down a dark alley. 
 
- 
Average: Average Items are pricy 
enough to make you think, but not so 
expensive as to make you stop. Average 
Items include a new mundane amp for 
your guitar, a folding pushbike to get 
around the city, a new wardrobe of 
denim and leather, and even some 
basic necromagical artefacts like 
talking skulls or ever-burning candles 
that never go out. Pff. Cheap trick. 
 
- 
Costly: Costly Items are either 
annoyingly scarce or bordering on 
financially unsustainable for most 
normal folks. Costly Items include a 
new necromagical instrument, a flask 
of Ambrosial Blood, a sliver of premium 
Godflesh or that sick new car you’ve 
been looking at for aaaaggggeeees. 
 
- 
Expensive: Expensive Items are the 
guard post between the rich and the 
poor- if you can afford this shit, you’ve 
broken through into the social elite and 
can no longer call yourself a true punk 
rocker. Expensive Items include a fresh 
home in Skin City’s heights, a 
membership at an exclusive 
gentleman’s club, extortionately priced 
delicacies that massively matter to 
someone (presumably), or other 
overpriced but high-status garbage. 
 
- 
Luxury: Luxury Items are stupidly 
expensive. That’s it. That’s the whole 
description. Luxury Items include 
whole-ass manor houses, fleets of 
ensorcelled, blacked-out limos, access 
to the penthouses of the rich and 
famous and some of the most potent 
demonic and heavenly artefacts this 
side of the Circles of Hell. 
 
Depending on your Drachma Level, you can 
afford a limited number of new Items of a 
certain Expense level every session, and may 
even be able to regularly afford certain Items 
without counting against this limit. The Items 
you can afford and regularly afford are detailed 
in the description for your Drachma Level. 
 
 
For example, a Necromusician with a 
Drachma Level of ‘Getting By’ can regularly 
afford items with an Expense of ‘Cheap’ or 
‘Average’. They can afford up to three ‘Costly’ 
items and only one ‘Expensive’ item per Session. 
They cannot afford ‘Luxury’ items at all! 
 
 
In order to acquire a new Item, you’ll 
need to roleplay. Describe how you’re sourcing 
it, where it’s coming from, what strings you had 
to pull to get a hold of it- the more expensive, the 
more justification you need. If your GM 
approves, and you haven’t exhausted your 
acquisitions for the session, congrats! You got it. 


 
 
 
It might sound like it’s dead easy for 
Necromusicians to acquire new shit, once their money gets 
its arse moving. Yeah. Yeah, you’re right. But that’s the 
thing with Necromusicians- once the money’s moving, it 
goes up and down like a pendulum and never stops. One 
day, they’ll be feeling flush, and the next they’ll be 
shuddering down the downward spiral towards TV dinners 
and a night spent drunk in the gutter. Let them have the 
wins when they can- obviously, don’t let them get away 
with murder, and set your own limits, but remember that 
this world is supposed to be chaotic, full of danger and, 
above all, fun. You like fun, right? Besides, if you need 
to punish ‘em, have a demon eat their arses when they 
next fuck up in the Labyrinth. See how much that fancy 
new gun matters then, hey? 
 
Changes to Drachma Level 
 
 
Throughout the course of a campaign of 
Dead Metal Rhapsody, the Necromusicians’ 
Drachma Level will rise and fall. To keep track of 
changes in their financial situation, each 
Necromusician will need to track their Fortune. 
Fortune starts at 0 and can rise to a maximum of 
6. 
 
 
Each time a Necromusician makes a 
financial gain, or builds connections, or secures 
an asset, or steals something shiny, their 
Fortune will increase. Each time a 
Necromusician loses out on a deal, misplaces 
something valuable, gets stolen from or has 
their house burn down, their Fortune will 
decrease. Some example increases and 
decreases in Fortune are listed below: 
 
- 
+1 Fortune: Getting paid for a show; 
pawning off a Necromagical trinket at a 
Skin City broker; finding a wallet stuffed 
with Drachma on a street corner; 
getting lucky at the slots. 
- 
+2 Fortune: Signing a record deal; 
finding and selling off some Ambrosial 
Blood or Heavensgold; knocking over a 
rich fucker’s mansion. 
- 
+3 Fortune: Getting paid for a whole 
damn festival tour; selling a valuable 
artefact from the ruins of Heaven; 
selling your firstborn to the Archdevils in 
exchange for cash. 
 
- 
-1 Fortune: Getting mugged on a Skin 
City street corner; losing your wallet on 
a night out; having a show promoter 
cancel on you. 
- 
-2 Fortune: Losing a valuable shipment 
of Godflesh from the Labyrinth; having 
your band’s studio robbed; crashing an 
expensive car. 
- 
-3 Fortune: Getting struck off from a 
record label; having your entire house 
burn to cinders; having to pay to rebuild 
your entire house. 
 
The GM has the final say on whether a 
Necromusician’s Fortune increases or 
decreases, and by how much if it does. 
 
Once a Necromusician’s Fortune reaches 6 
or higher, it resets to 0 and they increase their 
Drachma Level by one step. For example, if a 
Necromusician with a Drachma Level of ‘Getting 
By’ hits 6 Fortune, their Fortune drops back 
down to 0 and their Drachma Level increases to 
‘Well-Off’. 
 
By contrast, once a Necromusician’s 
Fortune falls below 0, it resets to 0 and they 
decrease their Drachma Level by one step. For 
example, if a Necromusician with a Drachma 
Level of ‘Getting By’ hit -1 Fortune, their Fortune 
resets to 0 and their Drachma Level decreases 
to ‘Skint’. 
 
A Necromusician’s Drachma Level can 
never decrease below ‘Skint’ and can never rise 
above ‘Stupidly Rich’. If a Necromusician’s 
Fortune would cause them to fall below ‘Skint’ 
or rise above ‘Stupidly Rich’, their Fortune just 
stays at 6 or at 0 respectively and no change in 
Drachma Level occurs. 
 
 
Alright, lads! You know the drill. Got a 
big shipment of Heavensgold for 
Bloodrocution Studios, fresh out of the 
Labyrinth. No, you can’t touch it 
without gloves, unless you want to go 
fucking crazy, Clive. Get those gauntlets 
on, and get those crates moving. We 
ain’t got long before the execs are 
breathing down our necks… 


 
 
Lists of Items in Dead Metal Rhapsody 
 
What follows is a series of lists of the various Items Necromusicians might encounter in the world of Dead 
Metal Rhapsody, categorised by type and by their Expense level. These lists are by no means exhaustive- 
the GM is encouraged to come up with their own Items whenever they want! These lists are designed to 
serve as a useful guide to what sort of things are on offer in the markets of Skin City and the trading posts 
of the Pit. Feel free to use as much or as little of this as you wish. 
 
Weapons 
 
Item Name 
Description 
Expense Level 
Bladed 
Weapons 
Fire axes, bowie knives, butchery cleavers, edgy katanas that 
appeal to a certain demographic- anything sharp designed to slice 
and dice. Whether crafted by a skilled blacksmith or by some 
jobber in a shed with a hunk of pig iron, you can trust in these 
implements to get the job done. 
Average 
Blunt 
Weapons 
Sometimes, you just need to hit someone with something big and 
heavy. Clubs, baseball bats, sledgehammers, bits of concrete on 
the end of a rebar- whatever lump of rock or metal you’re swinging, 
it’s gonna do some damage. 
Cheap 
Bows and 
Thrown 
Weapons 
Most archers these days are sportspeople rather than hunters- 
ain’t much forest left in the Pit, after all. Thrown weaponry tends to 
consist of knives or other spiky bits- throwing stars too, though 
that’s a bit more ‘mall ninja’ than hardened murderer. Still, 
someone must be buying them, eh? 
Average 
Small 
Firearms 
Pistols and other sidearms-  once pretty hard to get a hold of in the 
UK, now often a necessity due to the day-to-day risk of being 
mauled by a zombie or smashed into bits by a devil. Not 
particularly powerful, but easy to conceal and lethal enough in 
most cases. That’s really all you need. 
Average 
Medium 
Firearms 
Long guns, such as rifles and shotties, for when a small bullet in an 
undead’s brainpan isn’t gonna be enough to keep them down. Most 
are smuggled in via the nomad merchants at the edge of the Pit; 
some are hand-made in dingy basements by weirdos and gun nuts. 
Some old military tech can also be salvaged, here and there. 
Costly 
Large 
Firearms 
Nothing says ‘urban redevelopment’ like a heavy machinegun or a 
rocket launcher. Sometimes, the deadliest monstrosities of the 
Labyrinth require a military-grade solution in order to keep them 
down. Highly illegal, highly challenging to get hold of, even harder 
to hide from jealous, prying eyes. 
Expensive 
Low-Grade 
Explosives 
For when you need to blow the bloody doors off, but not too hard. 
Encompasses simple charges, such as those used for mining or 
low-scale demolitions, as well as improvised alternatives. Likely 
cooked up by a beardy anarchist chap named Alan. 
Average 
High-Grade 
Explosives 
Grenades and landmines, either stolen from military contractors or 
put together on a shoestring budget. Also includes C4, deep 
mining charges and blasting rods. 
Costly 
Military-
Grade 
Explosives 
Anything designed to blow up an entire building or turn a tank into 
scrap metal. Usually only used by the deranged or suicidally 
insane, given that the Labyrinth is, well, for the most part deep 
underground. You lunatics. 
Expensive 


 
 
Clothing 
 
Item Name 
Description 
Expense Level 
Clothing 
(Street Punk) 
Torn jeans, tatty battle jackets, leather patches, high-top boots, 
knock-off band t-shirts, studded wristbands and a bad attitude. 
Anything that a self-disrespecting metalhead down on their luck 
would wear. It’s all about the swagger, baby- who gives a shit if 
there are holes in your socks? 
Cheap 
Clothing 
(Run-of-the-
Mill Rocker) 
You wanna look like a respectable member of society? No? Well, 
too bad- these types of clothes are for people that want to fit in. 
Just about any modern style- and some classic ones, too- falls into 
this category of clothing. 
Average 
Clothing 
(Fancy Shit) 
Well, look at you… You can actually afford official band merch, a 
nice suit, a tie that fits and an aftershave that doesn’t smell like a  
college bedroom. If you want to look stylish, chic, cutting-edge or 
hard-as-nails with flair, these clothes are what you’re looking for. 
Just street enough to be cool, too. 
Costly 
Clothing 
(Now You’re 
Just Showing 
Off) 
Fancy couture, suits actually made by a tailor, dresses to die for, 
avant-garde fashion that flies in the face of God. These are the 
clothes for people who want to dress to kill, good taste, expense 
and societal norms be damned. Mostly worn by rich execs and 
delusional old rockers, but sometimes by people who are just 
suave enough to pull it off. 
Expensive 
Light Armour 
(Physical) 
Give yourself a bit of protection against a cricket bat to the face 
with a hard hat, a padded leather jacket or even some bits of scrap 
metal tied together. Hey, whatever works. When worn, gain 
Physical (6+) Armour. You can only wear one type of Physical 
Armour at a time, and Physical Armour does not stack. 
Average 
Medium 
Armour 
(Physical) 
When you need something to stop a good stabbing. Military flak 
jackets, beaten panels of polycarbonate, old-style plate armour for 
the retro crowd and LARP’ers alike. When worn, gain Physical (5+) 
Armour. You can only wear one type of Physical Armour at a time, 
and Physical Armour does not stack. 
Costly 
Heavy 
Armour 
(Physical) 
Dense and hard-wearing protection for the discerning customer 
looking to stop a literal bullet. Bulletproof vests, the latest in 
ceramic plating used for tanks, chainmail made out of 
Heavensgold- it’s chunky, it’s clunky, it’ll save your life. When worn, 
gain Physical (4+) Armour. You can only wear one type of Physical 
Armour at a time, and Physical Armour does not stack. 
Luxury 
Necromagical 
Shielding 
(Minor) 
Carven bone fetishes, runestones inscribed with the script of Hell, 
rings of steel imbued with curses, even necklaces of guitar picks 
baptised in blood and strung together- these will keep you safe(ish) 
from Necromagic. When worn, gain Magical (6+) Armour. You can 
only wear one type of Magical Armour at a time, and Magical 
Armour does not stack. 
Costly 
Necromagical 
Shielding 
(Major) 
Grimoires printed on pages of molten brass that glow with power. 
Guitar strings forged into bloody crucifixes. Still-beating hearts 
shot through with nails and runic scarring. No better defence 
against magic in the business. When worn, gain Magical (5+) 
Armour. You can only wear one type of Magical Armour at a time, 
and Magical Armour does not stack. 
Luxury 
 
 


 
 
Black Magic Artefacts 
 
This list of Black Magic Artefacts is by no means exhaustive. It’s just intended to give you some flavour- an 
outline, if you will, of the sorts of relics, devices and magical objects that get dredged out of the Labyrinth. 
Feel free to use them in your games, but also please use them as inspiration for your own Black Magic 
Artefacts! 
 
Item Name 
Description 
Expense Level 
Eyes of the 
Dead 
A musty ceramic jar full to the brim with livid, staring eyes. When 
opened, the eyes gaze out hatefully on the lights of the world 
they’ve been denied. They make especially good sentries, as what 
one can see is reflected in the irises of every other. Scatter them 
about, and they’re better than any security camera, if requiring a 
larger supply of eyedrops to keep them from crusting up. Ew. 
Average 
Ever-Burning 
Brimstone 
Torch 
Stinking of sulphur and radiating an intense heat, this branch of 
calcified devil bone never stops burning. Like, ever. You can dunk it 
in frigid water, and it’ll still give off the same sickly yellow light and 
warmth. No-one’s tried it in space yet, but the assumption is it’ll 
burn even there. Just try not to set the whole house on fire, yeah? 
Average 
Ensorcelled 
Pick 
A plectrum made out of the shattered teeth of an Archdevil. Yeah, I 
dunno either. Presumably they grow back. Either way, an 
Ensorcelled Pick bestows unnatural skill on the lazy 
Necromusician who doesn’t want to practice to get good at the 
guitar. May come with side-effects, such as histrionic voice and 
random exploding heads in the audience. 
Average 
Infernal 
Familiar 
(Lesser) 
A minor devil, enslaved to a particular command fetish such as a 
ring, talisman or bit of severed finger. About the size of a cherub, 
Infernal Familiars are begrudgingly loyal to whomever holds their 
command fetish, though they’ll often try to… dispose of their 
erstwhile masters once their back is turned. 
Average 
Flensing 
Casket 
A favourite of the gore-obsessed Way of All Flesh, a Flensing 
Casket appears like an instrument case- until its locks are popped. 
When opened, a mass of living barbed wire and copper cabling 
erupts out from the Casket, engulfing hapless victims in a storm of 
razor-edged metal. The inevitable result is complete flaying, tatters 
of skin swallowed up by the Casket before it locks shut. 
Costly 
Dead Fluids 
A flask of neon green liquid, usually in an Erlenmeyer (because it 
looks the coolest- what, you gonna put necromagical potions in a 
Lucozade bottle?). When poured over an amalgamated corpse, 
Dead Fluids reanimate even the most damaged tissue, making it 
possible for flesh surgeons to create horrifying constructs and 
other ghoulish creatures. Albeit ones utterly bereft of any form of 
conscious mind. 
Costly 
Bloodrifuge 
A centrifuge for blood. Alright, fine, it’s got a few more 
necromagical elements than just that. Set the blood off spinning, 
and the Bloodrifuge will tell you who it belonged to, their shoe size, 
their favourite genre of metal- you name it, the device will read it. 
Makes for one hell of a stage show as well, with the blood trails 
whizzing round all over the shop. Gnarly. 
Costly 
Ensorcelled 
‘Hell Amp’ 
For when going up to 11 isn’t quite good enough- this Hell Amp 
goes up to 666. They took a regular Amp, stripped it back, then 
shoved a chorus of devils in it and pumped them full of magic. 
Playing anything through this beast of a machine is guaranteed to 
be 72% more heavy metal than just using a regular amp, like some 
sort of peasant. 
Costly 


 
 
Dead Man’s 
Hand 
A deck of playing cards, emblazoned with artistic depictions of all 
the Necromusicians who sadly experienced Second Death at the 
age of -27. These cards can be used like a tarot deck of sorts, 
divining the skeins of the future through cryptic warnings and 
riddles. It is also said that a hidden riff lies buried within the deck, 
its notes corresponding to a certain hand of cards. When played, 
the riff unshackles the spirits of the dead within the deck. 
Expensive 
Hell Talon 
A kick-ass sword made out of the severed tooth of a greater 
demon. Prone to exploding into wreaths of fire, sprays of acid and 
whirlwinds of chains resonating with machine-gun thrash metal. 
Cool as shit. 
Expensive 
Reaper’s 
Cowbell 
A small silver cowbell for a drumkit, inscribed with a pair of 
crossed scythes inlaid in alabaster filigree. Does nothing to the 
Undead, but when heard by the living the sonorous peals of the 
bell drive them to bang their heads so hard as to break their own 
necks. Has caused more than one mosh pit riot. 
Expensive 
The Black 
Album 
A dust-shrouded LP, the cover of which is completely black. The 
record within is also completely black, and the tracklist and lyric 
booklet are also printed in black on black paper. The record will 
only play in complete darkness on a gramophone that’s also 
painted black plugged into a black power socket set into a black 
wall. Those that listen to it say it’s the greatest, heaviest, most 
head-bangingly awesome metal album to ever exist. 
Expensive 
The Key of 
Solomon 
It is said that Solomon commanded legions of demons with but a 
twist of his bejewelled ring. The Key of Solomon is a grimoire of 
horrifying power printed in Enochian on laminate plates of 
Heavensgold. Those who read from its pages become indefinitely 
insane, but, in return, gain the power to call upon monstrous 
creatures and even the Archdevils themselves with but a whisper 
of their true names. Of course, such power comes at a price… a 
delicious sliver of the very matter of their soul. 
Luxury 
Crown of 
Hellish 
Whispers 
A circlet made of guitar jacks sharpened to razor-edged spikes, the 
Crown of Hellish Whispers fills the mind with… inspiration. The 
songs of the Infernal Choirs play inside the head of the wearer, 
imbuing their own music with dizzying polyrhythms and a 
heaviness to rival the crushing weight of the sea. No-one is sure of 
what befalls the Crown’s wearer, as, inevitably, after they release 
an album, they collapse under their music’s own weight like the 
death of a neutron star. 
Luxury 
Angel’s Tear 
A single golden tear, shed in the last days of the War in Heaven. 
Bottled in a pale crystal vial, this liquid is said to offer whomever 
imbibes it one day of true life, free from the shackles of the 
Archdevils and the purgatory of undeath. Angel’s Tears are thus 
highly prized by the rich and insanely delusional alike (although, in 
the Pit, the Venn Diagram between the two is often just a circle). 
There are those that believe, with enough Angel’s Tears to fuel 
them, that they will truly be able to live forever… 
Luxury 
Incantation of 
Blasphemous 
Command 
The words of an Incantation of Blasphemous Command are written 
on plates of black iron in the blood of the newly-murdered, and 
burn with livid purple flames. Those who know the deep language 
of Hell are capable of reading the words of the Incantation aloud; 
those with the mental fortitude to withstand the necromagical 
backlash may avoid becoming irrevocably insane when doing so. 
The Incantation allows its reader to make ONE irrefusable request 
of an Archdevil. Of course, what the Archdevil will do once this 
request is fulfilled is quite another matter entirely… 
Luxury 


 
 
Pets 
 
This list of Pets available in the Pit is by no means exhaustive. It’s just intended to give you some flavour- 
an outline, if you will, of the sorts of creatures, constructs and undead beasts that populate the homes of 
the Pit. Feel free to use them in your games, but also please use them as inspiration for your own Pets and 
exotic animals! 
 
Item Name 
Description 
Expense Level 
Corpse-
Critter 
The simplest of Undead pets. Take the corpse of a once-living 
animal, soak it in some Dead Fluids, et voila. Your furry, scaly or 
feathery companion is back! Probably smells a lot worse, but, eh, 
what can you do? 
Cheap 
Pumpkin 
Ghoul 
A bad joke on behalf of an experimental bio-botanist, and the 
answer to the question “what would happen if you resurrected a 
Halloween jack-o-lantern?” A Pumpkin Ghoul is exactly what it 
sounds like. Face carving optional. 
Cheap 
Amalgam-inal 
An Amalgam-inal is a sutured combination of animal parts built to 
a custom specification by a necromantic surgeon. Always wanted 
a three-headed Doberman with a scorpion tail and a vestigial 
wing? Congratulations- there’s a mad scientist out there with just 
the thing for you! 
Average 
Actual, 
Honest-to-
Goodness 
Living Animal 
It’s… look, you have an imagination, and presumably a functioning 
brain. Living Animals are still shipped in from ‘Great Britain’ as 
companions and beasts of burden. You just want a cat? Here. You 
can have one. 
Average 
Sabre 
Unicorn 
For the horse girl who’s also an aspiring barbarian warlord, the 
Sabre Unicorn is a majestic, beautiful horse with a fucking 
chainsaw for a horn. Carve your way through your enemies on the 
back of your black beauty, but watch out for the sprays of blood. It 
can be hell to clean out of horsehair. 
Costly 
Living Throne 
A Living Throne is an ornate sedan chair supported by your choice 
of legs: human, spider, bird, octopus- the necromantic surgeons 
have got you covered. The advantage of a Living Throne is that it 
serves a dual purpose- a loving pet for the discerning owner, and 
also a reliable method of transport. 
Costly 
Enthralled 
Demonspawn 
Demons and their ilk are hard to enslave. The correct incantations, 
rites of binding and runic inscriptions are necessary to keep them 
from rebelling, and are costly to maintain. As such, Enthralled 
Demonspawn are status symbols for the wealthy of Skin City, 
showing off the power of those that can afford to keep literal Hell 
creatures on a leash. 
Expensive 
Necro-
Morpher 
(Painkiller 
Pattern) 
What happens when you combine a motorcycle and a 
necromorphic construct stitched together from demonically-
possessed flesh? You get perhaps the most metal-as-fuck chopper 
imaginable. Capable of transforming between a beastly Stitched 
construct and a sweet hog at will, Necro-Morphers are common 
amongst high-ranking Necromusicians. They’re just sweet as. ‘nuff 
said. 
Expensive 
Angel-Flesh 
Construct 
Nah. This one is just a bad idea. Angel flesh never truly dies. It 
retains its vitality for as long as God herself remains in the depths 
of the Labyrinth. Those who would build constructs out of severed 
Angel meat are either stupid, suicidal or both. Seriously- don’t do 
this. You’ll regret it. 
 
Luxury 


 
 
 
The arena is alive tonight. A queue of corpses 
winds its way around the block. It tangles in the 
mouths of the pubs, the bars, the neon glow of the 
offies selling cheap beer. The roadies are unloading 
the trucks ‘round back. And the scalpers are hawking 
fake tickets out front. The bouncers stand, muscular 
arms folded, eight feet of stitched sinews and bad 
temper. They all wait. Wait for the band to arrive. 
 
 
No-one’s sure how the fight broke out. There’s 
suspicion, sure, but nothing’s confirmed. Some say a 
bootlegger tried to pass off a t-shirt as official, and 
got a face full of lead in response. Some say a sing-a-
long became a mosh pit, which became a brawl, which 
became all-out necromagical war. Some say it was just 
some dumb fuck tripping the biggest guy in the queue. 
 
 
In the end, the band just played in the street. 


 
 
Tools and Equipment 
 
Item Name 
Description 
Expense Level 
Mundane Tool 
A hammer, a saw, a screwdriver- anything a normal human could 
make and use. 
Cheap 
Mechanic’s 
Toolbox 
A battered old metal case full of rusting tools; a leather bag 
rammed with screwdrivers, wrenches and little plastic baggies of 
screws; any sort of toolkit that includes all the necessaries to strip 
down a car or replumb a radiator. Nothing fancy, but adaptable and 
reliable. What more could you need? 
Cheap 
Music Tech’s 
Gear 
Coils of spare cable, tuning keys for the drums, basic electrician’s 
bits and bobs you can use to refit an amplifier. Anyone who’s been 
on tour long enough to need to strip and refit a pyro pot or a lighting 
rig knows what’s in here. You might even find a few spare parts at 
the bottom of the bag. 
Cheap 
Medical Tools 
Whilst they might not be ‘private medical standard’, these stainless 
steel tools are still good enough to dress wounds, treat minor 
illnesses and even carry out some surgeries in the hands of a 
skilled professional. Syringes, scalpels, bandages, forceps, other 
words that indicate cold glass and plastic and a disinfectant 
chemical smell. 
Average 
Necro-
Surgery Tools 
If you’ve ever wanted to build your own Stitched, these are the 
tools for you. Bonesaws, razor-edged flensing knives, hatchets to 
chop through limbs and a long needle and wire thread for use in 
attaching all the bits together. Don’t mind the smell. Seriously- you 
can’t afford to mind it. It never goes away. 
Average 
Engineering 
Kit 
Unlike the Mechanic’s Toolbox, this set of tools is a bit fancier. 
More white-collar draftsman than blue-collar electrician- think 
callipers, digital measuring tools and power tools, rather than 
hammers and shit. 
Average 
Labyrinth 
Survival Gear 
Grapnel and line, high-powered torches, safety flares, rock cutters, 
blasting charges, spare cyanide pill in case things go south… What 
constitutes ‘Labyrinth Survival Gear’ is definitely up for debate, but 
imagine a combination of fire rescue equipment and esoteric 
occult devices like magical compasses, and you won’t go far 
wrong. 
Costly 
Necromagical 
Alchemist’s 
Kit 
Necromagical alchemy concerns the study of magical artefacts, 
such as Heavensgold and Godflesh, as well as the concocting of 
potions, tinctures and philtres. A kit likely consists of alembics, 
rubber tubes, dowsing stones of meteoric iron, crystalline and 
herbal reagents and other arcane chemicals. 
Costly 
Demon 
Summoning 
Ritual 
Paraphernalia 
A stick of chalk, a flask of angel’s blood, a pamphlet named 
“Demon Summoning 4 Dummies”, a signed photo of Aleister 
Crowley… No-one’s sure why demons respond to these rites and 
rituals, but there’s speculation out there that they’re just fucking 
with us. Hey, at least it works. 
Expensive 
Arcane 
Library  
An Arcane Library is less of a toolkit and more of a collection of 
necromagical paraphernalia and mind-rendingly complex texts of 
ancient lore. Within an Arcane Library are hidden terrible truths 
and secrets about the nature of the Labyrinth and the creatures 
within it. Just… try to keep a hold of your brains when reading, 
yeah? They have a habit of sluicing out of an unsuspecting reader’s 
ears. 
Luxury 
 


 
 
Consumables 
 
Item Name 
Description 
Expense Level 
Mundane 
Drugs 
The drug trade in Skin City is just as prolific as the drug trade in 
London, if not more so. After all, most undead need to take a hell 
of a lot of something to feel a buzz- the enterprising dealer is only 
too happy to oblige. Most Mundane Drugs flow in and out of the Pit 
to ‘Great Britain’ beyond, and keep nomad merchants in Drachma. 
Cheap 
Cigarettes 
and Alcohol 
No-one’s quite sure why the undead still get by on tobacco and 
booze- an echo of heavy metal attitude, perhaps, or a weird quirk 
of the music of the Infernal Choirs bringing out the hedonism and 
excesses of the living in those bound to them. Either way, smoke 
‘em if you’ve got ‘em, and nurse that wicked hangover- party like 
tomorrow doesn’t exist. 
Cheap 
Surface Food 
The dead can’t metabolise the food of the living, but that doesn’t 
mean they don’t enjoy the taste. Skilled chefs are in demand 
amongst the rich and powerful of Skin City- for what says ‘too rich 
for my own good’ than having food cooked for you that you can’t 
even benefit from? Fresh fruit, vegetables, meat and fish- all 
shipped in inside iceboxes to end up between some skeleton’s 
ribcage. What a waste. 
Average 
Ambrosial 
Blood 
Ambrosial Blood is a sweetened cocktail of blood, sugar and 
alcohol fermented inside oak coffins until a rich and complex 
flavour develops. It’s like designer whiskey for the undead, and just 
as bloody (ha!) expensive to boot. 
Costly 
Hell Steel 
Metals forged into the black furnaces of Hell take on a sepulchral 
midnight sheen, not so much reflecting light as swallowing it up 
and spitting it back out. Hell Steel is prized as a material for 
constructing instruments, as it resonates with the music of the 
Infernal Choirs. It’s also very easy to work, as it never drops below 
a warm residual heat. 
Costly 
Necromusical 
Albums 
(Runic 
Pressings) 
Particularly potent Necromusic is pressed onto plates of rune-
inscribed vinyl, creating a cacophonous sound so pure and 
thunderous as to rival the music of the Infernal Choirs themselves. 
Only the most infamous and influential bands get this treatment, 
and the resultant LPs are in ferociously high demand. 
Expensive 
Bottled Souls 
Those Necromusicians who experience Second Death usually 
have their souls devoured by the Archdevils they once served- 
however, on rare occasions, their souls are bottled in crystal flasks 
and sold to the highest bidder. A hell of a fate, but one that stands 
to make someone a hell of a lot richer. 
Expensive 
Heavensgold 
Heavensgold is an extraordinarily rare and coveted material that 
once plated the towers of Heaven itself. Heavensgold is truly 
radiant, shimmering with the latent glow of a thousand suns, 
causing all who look upon it to stare in rapture and wonder. 
Unfortunately, it’s also volatile as fuck- touching it with your bare 
skin will see you immolated in a column of iridescence so bright as 
to blind any onlookers. 
Luxury 
Godflesh 
When God perished, Her three bodies were violently torn asunder. 
Fragments of Her flesh were scattered throughout the Labyrinth, 
still warm and quivering with divine power. There are those that 
seek out Godflesh, believing to consume it is to invite said divine 
power into themselves. However, God is not dead, merely 
sleeping- even Her corpse is a clear and present danger… 
Luxury 


 
 
Real Estate and Vehicles 
 
N.B. The amount of real estate a Necromusician can own is limited by their Drachma Level, as is the 
amount of vehicles they can own. With a Drachma Level of ‘Skint’, a Necromusician can own up to one 
place to live and up to one vehicle. These limits increase by one apiece for every Drachma Level above 
‘Skint’. At a Drachma Level of ‘Stupid Rich’, these limits no longer apply at all. 
 
Item Name 
Description 
Expense Level 
Low-Quality 
Digs 
Rented apartments above a bowling alley (and below another 
bowling alley). Seedy backrooms that smell of piss and cigarettes. 
Bedsits, shared houses, a mate’s sofa to crash one while you’re 
hard-up. No-one wants to be in these situations, but hey, 
sometimes you just ain’t got the cash. 
Cheap 
Bikes, 
Skateboards, 
Scooters and 
Shit 
Quick ‘n’ easy, and with some street cred attached when you’re in 
the right crowd. Skateboards can be status items, if you’re punk 
enough- even if they won’t get you to work on time in the morning. 
Fuck you if you choose a scooter, though. 
Cheap 
Standard 
Living 
Situation 
An apartment that isn’t so small it makes you feel like you’re being 
crushed. A decent room in a shared house with people you 
actually like. A starter flat in Skin City, or a comfortable coffin out in 
one of the cemeteries if you’re feeling retro. Good enough for most 
people, and good enough for you. 
Average 
Motorbikes, 
Horses and 
Cheap Cars 
Motorbikes are a dime a dozen- it’s all about the customisation, 
baby! Soup that shit up. Cars in Skin City tend to be whatever 
reliable family hatchbacks got salvaged after the fall- lot of Toyota 
Hiluxes, too. Damn resilient. Finally, you can have a horse if you 
want to make a statement. I guess. 
Average 
Necromagical 
Conveyances 
(Small) 
Basically biomechanical war machines, but on a dinky little 
personal scale. Six-legged steel and flesh palanquins. Motorbikes 
with mouths that talk back. Stitched-together animals with 
obedience chips in their brains. Grim, but funky if you’ve ever 
wanted to ride a cyborg zombie tiger. 
Average 
Good Quality 
Homes 
Actual, honest-to-goodness houses that you might even own 
yourself. Apartments that have more than a few rooms and don’t 
make you want to die. Shared houses, too, but big ones, ones 
actually worth being a part of. Fancy crypts for the old-schoolers, 
and Pit lairs for the explorers. 
Costly 
Vanity 
Vehicles 
Fancier cars and bikes for the show-offs and for people with more 
money than sense. Fast, noisy and a pain to maintain, but, hey, 
supposedly the ladies like ‘em. Imported surface vehicles from 
overseas, such as American muscle cars or Italian racers, also fall 
into this category. 
Costly 
Necromagical 
Conveyances 
(Large) 
Ever wanted to pilot a giant hermit crab’s corpse living inside the 
hollowed-out shell of a World War 2 tiger tank? Thanks to some 
particularly imaginative Necrosurgeons, now you can! Covers all 
manner of larger necromechanical constructions that go beyond 
the scope of just augmenting an animal or a chair. 
Expensive 
Big, Fuck-Off 
Houses 
Look, you’ve made it to the big leagues. Who’s going to judge you? 
Buy a big fuck-off house with a swimming pool, a magma fountain, 
six shooting galleries and a room specifically for the consumption 
of buttered rich tea biscuits. It’s your money- you do you. We won’t 
judge you. You billionaire scumbag, you. 
Luxury 
 


 
 
The future is a dead scene. 
 
An arms race to be louder, to be faster, 
to be heavier. 
 
To somehow be more ‘true’. 
 
We’ll kill each other for ‘selling out’. 
We’ll kill each other for ‘giving up’. And 
they’ll be laughing. 
 
The devils will laugh as we feed them our 
souls. 
 


 
 
Power Metal 
Experience and Progression for Necromusicians 
 
Making It Big- How 
Necromusicians Grow in Power 
 
 
So, bigshot, you’ve survived a few turns 
in the depths of the Labyrinth. You’ve braved 
boiling chasms of acid full of screaming faces; 
you survived a stint in the furnaces, in which 
angel corpses were melted down for fat; you’ve 
rocked out like a motherfucker over the bodies 
of the devils and the dead that tried to screw 
you. And now you’re wondering… what the hell 
is my reward? Well, relax there, slugger. Let’s 
discuss Experience Points, and how we can use 
them to progress your Necromusician from 
down-and-out rock wannabe to stadium-filling 
necromagical star. 
 
Experience Points 
 
 
At the end of each session you play of 
Dead Metal Rhapsody, the GM will award each 
of the PCs with Experience Points (EXP). EXP is 
used to improve a Necromusician’s Attributes, 
Secondaries, Edges, Necromagical Powers… 
just about everything you can think of! EXP can 
either be spent immediately, or banked up until 
you need it most. Just bear in mind that you 
cannot spend EXP during a session- you must 
wait for the session to end first! There is no cap 
on the amount of EXP you can save up- but, 
come on, you know you want to spend it. Come 
on. Live a little. 
 
 
At the end of a session, the players and 
the GM should work together to answer the 
following questions. For every question that you 
can unequivocally answer ‘yes’, each PC gains 1 
EXP. For every question you’re unsure about, or 
have to answer ‘no’, forget about it and move on. 
There’s always next time. The questions are as 
follows: 
 
1. Did you make solid progress towards 
one of the group’s overarching goals 
during this session? 
 
2. Did your group do something badass, 
reckless, cool-as-shit or otherwise 
heavy fucking metal? 
 
3. Did your group overcome a dangerous 
challenge, such as vanquishing a 
deadly foe, exploring a horrifying 
location or (gasp!) playing a kick-ass 
show? 
 
4. Did your group roleplay in a new, 
exciting or unusual way that really 
brought new life to the story? 
 
5. Did everyone sat around the table, 
virtual or otherwise, play the game 
today? 
 
You might have figured out that, after any 
given session, the PCs will gain a minimum of 1 
EXP and a maximum of 5. They’ll always be 
getting more powerful- something, something, 
what doesn’t kill you and all that. If you’re the 
GM of your group, do try to be generous when 
answering these questions. It’s not always 
appropriate for your players to gain all 5EXP, but 
it's always nice to give them something.  
 
 
Dead Woman Walking 
 
They say she dragged an angel’s corpse 
out of the depths and used its burning wings to 
light a cigarette. 
They say she hacked her way through 
three thousand corpses on the run from Johnny 
Law, with nothing more than a rusty cleaver. 
They say that, when she sings, the sky 
echoes and cries tears of blood. 
 
 
 
 


 
 
Spending Experience Points 
 
 
A Necromusician can spend EXP on a 
variety of upgrades. These range from improving 
their Attributes, to gaining new Necromagical 
Powers, to developing a new Edge or gaining 
more Will and Wounds. At the end of a session, 
all PCs in the group may spend as much or as 
little of their earned EXP as they like on the 
following upgrades, and mark any changes on 
their Character Sheet. 
 
Improve an Attribute  
 
A Necromusician can increase one of 
their Attributes, i.e. Guts, Brains or Spine, by one 
point. This costs an amount of EXP equal to 
twice the new value of the Attribute. A 
Necromusician’s Attributes may never increase 
above 6. Note that improving an Attribute DOES 
NOT change any of the Secondaries that were 
determined by that Attribute during Character 
Creation. 
 
Example: Ben wants to increase Kizzi’s 
Guts Attribute from 2 to 3. In order to do so, he 
must pay 6 EXP, as improving an Attribute costs 
EXP equal to twice its new value (3 x 2 = 6). 
 
Improve Armour 
 
A Necromusician can improve their 
existing Armour or gain a new type of Armour, 
should they wish. None of a Necromusician’s 
Armours may be improved beyond 3+. 
 
To improve an existing Armour, a 
Necromusician must spend EXP as listed in the 
following table. All EXP costs are cumulative 
(e.g. to improve Armour from 6+ to 4+ would 
cost 9 EXP in total). 
 
Armour 
Improvement 
EXP Cost 
6+ to 5+ 
3 
5+ to 4+ 
6 
4+ to 3+ 
9 
 
 
If a Necromusician instead wants to 
gain a new Armour at 6+, this will cost 3 EXP.  
 
 
 
There are plenty of ways to get stronger 
outside of gaining EXP, but, to be honest, it is kinda 
your bread and butter. Try to ensure you remember to 
spend it, because the Archdevils sure as shit won’t be 
willing to wait for you to catch up! There’s no right or 
wrong way to spend EXP, so just do what feels right for 
you. 
Who are you, in the dark? 
I’m the bitch who’ll make the devils pay. 
Why will you make them pay? 
For putting a chain around my throat. 
What was the chain for? 
To force me to kiss the ring. 
 
No Gods. 
No Masters. 
I will never bow to any of them again. 
 
 
Improve Defence 
 
A Necromusician can improve their 
Defence by spending EXP as listed in the 
following table. A Necromusician’s Defence can 
never increase above 6+. All EXP costs are 
cumulative (e.g. to improve Defence from 4+ to 
6+ would cost 20 EXP in total). 
 
Defence 
Improvement 
EXP Cost 
3+ to 4+ 
4 
4+ to 5+ 
8 
5+ to 6+ 
12 
 
Improve Necromagic 
 
A Necromusician can increase their 
Necromagic points by spending EXP. The 
amount of EXP it costs to gain an additional 
point of Necromagic depends on a 
Necromusician’s current Necromagic points. 
The exact EXP costs are listed in the following 
table. There is no upper limit to how many 
Necromagic points a Necromusician may have. 
 
Current Necromagic 
Points 
EXP Cost for +1 
Necromagic Point 
3 to 6 
1 
7 to 9 
2 
10 to 12 
3 
13 to 15 
4 
16 to 18 
5 
19 to 21 
6 
21 or Greater 
7 
 
 
Example: Ben wants to increase Kizzi’s 
Necromagic from 5 to 9. The first two points he 
buys would cost 1 EXP each, from 5 to 6 and 
then 6 to 7. The second two points would cost 2 
EXP each, from 7 to 8 and then 8 to 9. In total, 
Ben would spend 6 EXP to increase Kizzi’s 
Necromagic from 5 to 9. 


 
 
Improve Speed 
 
A Necromusician can increase their 
Speed by spending EXP. It costs 6 EXP to 
increase Speed from ‘Slow’ to ‘Average’, and 12 
EXP to increase Speed from ‘Average’ to ‘Fast’. 
 
Improve Will or Wounds 
A Necromusician can increase their Will 
and/or Wounds by spending EXP. The amount of 
EXP it costs to gain an additional point of Will 
and/or Wounds depends on a Necromusician’s 
current Will/Wounds. The exact EXP costs are 
listed in the following table. There is no upper 
limit to how much Will or Wounds a 
Necromusician can have. 
 
Current 
Will/Wounds 
EXP Cost for +1 
Will/Wound 
2 to 3 
2 
4 to 5 
3 
6 to 7 
4 
8 to 9 
5 
10 or Greater 
6 
 
Gain an Edge 
 
A Necromusician can gain new Edges 
by spending EXP. It costs 3 EXP to buy a new 
Edge. The GM has the final say on whether an 
Edge is appropriate for a Necromusician to 
purchase. Remember the rules surrounding 
Edges- and also remember that Edges need to 
make sense from a roleplaying perspective as 
well as from a rules one! 
 
Gain a Necromagical Power 
 
A Necromusician can gain new 
Necromagical Powers by spending EXP. 
Depending on how powerful the new 
Necromagical Power is going to be, the cost in 
EXP varies. In addition, the following restrictions 
apply to Necromagical Powers: 
 
- 
A Necromusician cannot have more 
Minor Necromagical Powers than twice 
their Brains Attribute. 
- 
A Necromusician cannot have more 
Major Necromagical Powers than their 
Brains Attribute. 
- 
A Necromusician cannot have more 
Metal Necromagical Powers than half 
their Brains Attribute (rounded up). 
- 
The GM has the final say on whether a 
Necromusician is allowed to purchase 
a particular Necromagical Power. 
 
Type of 
Necromagical 
Power 
EXP Cost 
Minor 
2 
Major 
4 
Metal 
6 
 
Gain an Archdevil’s Boon 
 
A Necromusician can, on occasion, 
beseech their Archdevil patron for 
empowerment- though this always comes at a 
price. An Archdevil’s Boon is a bespoke demonic 
power gifted to a Necromusician in exchange for 
an act of service. The player seeking a Boon and 
the GM should work together to decide what is 
most thematically appropriate, and then decide 
on an EXP cost. As a rule of thumb, Archdevil’s 
Boons should be a minimum of 3 EXP and 
should always come with strings attached- the 
GM has the final say on whether a player has 
earned their Necromusician a Boon, or whether 
their prayers fall on deaf ears! 
 
Example: Dave, playing as Fang-Edged 
Farah, asks Ben, the GM, whether or not he can 
purchase an Archdevil’s Boon from Farah’s 
patron in the Way of All Flesh. He’s looking for 
her blood to mutate into a highly corrosive acid, 
and argues that his recent acts of service to the 
Infernal Choir stands him in good stead to 
receive this gift. Ben agrees.  
 
Ben decides that the Archdevil’s Boon 
Dave’s looking for will cost 3 EXP to purchase. It 
will also require Dave to conduct an infernal 
sacrament using Farah’s newly acidic blood on 
stage at her show tonight- as proof of her loyalty 
and deference. Dave agrees, and spends the 3 
EXP. Ben describes how Farah’s blood glows like 
fire, before changing colour to a brilliant electric 
blue and sizzling like sherbet in her veins. 
 
A Note on Archdevil’s Boons 
 
The design intent behind Archdevil’s 
Boons is to give experienced Necromusicians 
something to spend their EXP on, and to allow 
players to get creative with the ways in which 
their Necromusician progresses. It is NOT 
intended to allow players to acquire 
overpowered mutations and abilities in a gamey 
way. GMs- DON’T let your players get away with 
murder here. They’ve got plenty of ways to be 
powerful, so make sure Archdevil’s Boons are 
only awarded thematically, that they always 
have a cost and that they’re being bought 
because they’re cool- not because of their 
power


 
 
Heaven and Hell 
The World of Dead Metal Rhapsody 
 
God is in Her Heaven, and all is right with the world. 
 
God is in her Heaven, with Her banners now unfurled.
