God is in Her Heaven, and bloody swords are drawn. 
 
God is in Her Heaven, and will be dead by dawn. 
 
Hell awaits in the ruins of all that came before. It writhes like a maggot in 
the corpse of the Divine, seeking false glory in the kingdom of the dead. 
There are no saviours here. Only ghosts. Lepers through the rubble. The 
Labyrinth will eat them all, and make dust of their bones. 
 
God was in Her Heaven, when all the whispers started. 
 
God was in Her Heaven, murderous and triparted. 
 
God was in Her Heaven, when Her civil war began. 
 
God was in Her Heaven, and Her Heaven now is damned. 
 
Hell is empty. 
Heaven is a grave. 
And all the Devils are here. 


 
 
In the Beginning… 
 
No-one knows what drove God insane. Her 
madness burned brightly- no long, slow 
unravelling, no delusions or dark designs, no 
seeping fog across her mind. Just a spark, a 
blossom of heat. Then all of Her came crashing 
down. The towers of Heaven unfurled their 
banners, pledging allegiance to either the 
Mother, the Daughter or the Holy Ghost. And so 
did God declare war upon herself. The corpses 
of the angels littered the streets, and a shadow 
was cast across the face of the divine. Below, 
chained in darkness, the Archdevils watched as 
their jailors tore themselves apart. 
 
Humanity felt only the echoes of the War in 
Heaven. Greed, anger, jealousy and rage 
bubbled within them, for angels are beings of 
passion, and their passions spilled into the 
material world as they fought, killed and died. 
Over thousands of years, the innocence of 
mankind was corrupted. Cities rose and fell. 
Technology, society, civilisation- born of the 
bitterness of a war we could not see. Step by 
step, humanity drove itself towards destruction, 
stepping but lightly in the footsteps of God. 
Then, at last, was the final blow of the War in 
Heaven struck. 
 
God’s three aspects took up arms against each 
other at the Pinnacle of Heaven. Babbling in 
Enochian, consumed by lunacy, they fought with 
such terrible strength that the very cities of 
Heaven were shattered and rent asunder. 
Millennia of untold glories, beauty and civility 
were cast down; the angels were slaughtered to 
extinction, their divine flesh rent asunder and 
scattered as a testament to their failure. At last, 
God accomplished a blasphemous act that was 
to shake the very foundations of the spheres. 
She succeeded in murdering herself. The 
Mother, the Daughter, the Holy Ghost- their 
forms perished, and were cast out of the light. 
But although God had died, she was not truly 
dead. And the echoes of her murder 
reverberated through creation like a peal of 
thunder. The year was 2024. And a crack formed 
in the dome of St. Paul’s cathedral. 
 
The crack became a crevasse. The crevasse 
became a chasm. And then, as the ruins of 
Heaven and the corpses of God fell to Earth, the 
chasm became the Pit. London collapsed into 
the darkness, and the shattered remnants of 
Heaven tangled amongst the wreckage and 
buried themselves into the heart of the world. 
Hell was torn open. The Archdevils spilled out 
into the Pit like blood drawn from a wound. 
Heaven and Hell were twisted together, spewing 
magic from the blood of dead angels, the broken 
seals upon the human dead and the carcasses 
of God herself. The machinery of death was 
broken. The music of the Archdevils was no 
longer discordant. The bounds of Heaven were 
gone. All that remained was the Pit, and the 
Labyrinth. And the newly undead that called 
these places home. 
 
The Archdevils of the Infernal Choirs saw the 
labyrinthine ruins of Heaven beneath them, and 
sensed at their heart an indescribable power. 
For God may have been killed, but she is not 
dead. Her omnipotence resonates from deep 
below, and the Archdevils hunger for it, are 
drawn to it, desire and crave it. But their domain, 
Hell, was awash with confusion, its laws and 
boundaries scattered throughout the Pit and the 
Labyrinth. They could not delve too deep, and 
thus needed servants. They found them in the 
undead, humans, survivors of London’s fall and 
the deceased unchained from the underworld, 
that were staggering from the wreckage of this 
dark new world. The Archdevils tempted them. 
Offered them power. Their music would be 
heard again. The Infernal Choirs would sing. 
And, when they sung, it would be with the roar 
and wail of a guitar. 
 
The year is now 2024. The Infernal Choirs play 
their Machiavellian games with the lives of 
Necromusicians, undead beings suffused with 
the music of Hell. They are the devil’s rejects, 
the newly-dead, the rockstars at the gates of the 
underworld. They’re dead, and loving it. In 
exchange for diabolic power and immortal life, 
the Necromusicians seek out the secrets of the 
Labyrinth, not knowing that they ransack the 
ruins of Heaven in search of the corpses of God. 
Theirs is the power of the guitar, the bass, the 
drums, the keyboards, the screaming heavy 
metal spirit of fire, chains and leather. They are 
the vanguards of a new age, the raiders of the 
grave of angels. They’ll fulfil their demonic 
masters’ dark desires- or embrace Second 
Death in a whirlwind of sex, drugs and rock ‘n’ 
roll. 
 
This is the world of Dead Metal Rhapsody.  
 
This is your world now. 
 
 


 
 
“There are no angels left. 
 
Their wings were torn. Halos, broken. Blood and 
feathers. White robes stained red. They 
screamed, but God bade them be silent. They 
took up spears, and danced in shame and guilt. 
 
Then they burned each other alive.” 
 
The Testament, pp. 217 
 
“You still reading that fucking book, Callie? Come 
on, help us move these rocks. Labyrinth keeps 
shifting- your damn book won’t be no use if the 
ceiling comes down on our heads!” 
 
“Chill out. I’m coming. You’re weak as shit, 
Russ- you and your boys too. Gimme that one- 
no, that one, the boulder, it’s right fucking 
there. Heave-O… Oh. Oh, shit.” 
 
“What is that? What the hell…” 
 
“That ain’t no body I’ve ever seen. Holy shit, 
it’s huge. The flesh is still fresh. Russ, the flesh 
is still warm.” 
 
“Dave. Dave, get your damn knives over here. 
Cut off a piece- we’ll take it back to the 
surgeon, see what she thinks. Whaddya mean, it 
won’t cut? Dave? Dave?” 
 
“Russ? You still there? Why… why can’t I see 
anything? Who’s there? I fucking heard you. 
Russ! Stop pissing about! Russ!” 
 
“And they danced as they burned, and sang their 
mournful songs, as the sky took up flames and 
smoke like a wreath. Though their flesh was 
broken, they still beheld the world. 
 
In all its glory. And in all its horror” 
 
The Pit 
 
Where London once was, there is now the Pit. 
The entire Greater London area was swallowed 
up as the crack in St. Paul’s cathedral yawned 
impossibly wide. Thousands upon thousands of 
homes, millions of people, fell down into the 
darkness of the Earth. The River Thames boiled 
and cascaded into the chasm; its waters still 
flow into the Pit from the north, though the 
Thames estuary has now run dry. The ruins of 
London are scattered throughout the Pit, which 
is hundreds of metres deep and kilometres 
wide. It is a jagged landscape of rubble, 
boulders, splintered buildings, pools of black 
water and plumes of smoke. Beneath it, the 
tunnels of the Labyrinth bore their way deeper. 
Within it, the undead survivors of London dwell. 
 
The Pit consists of five ‘districts’, loosely 
corresponding to regions of the city when it still 
resided on the surface. The largest of these, Skin 
City, is at the Pit’s centre, and comprises the old 
City of London and central London area. The 
other districts surround Skin City, and are 
pockmarked with settlements, villages and 
shanties. They are Tottenham, to the north, 
Greenwich, to the east, Wimbledon, the south, 
and Ealing, to the west.  
 
The Geography of the Pit 
 
The landscape of the Pit is a desolate urban 
sprawl smashed into ruin across a volcanic 
desert. Imagine broken, sulphurous earth 
spitting out gouts of brimstone, magma and 
dust in the shadow of Big Ben, the steel and 
glass wreckage of the Gherkin and the twisted 
frame of the London Eye, on the banks of a 
meandering oil-slicked river. Then imagine the 
blighted earth giving way to a settlement, a 
cultural whirlwind of colours, lights, shapes and 
sounds built out of old London rubble, salvaged 
street signs and council flats. Finally, imagine, 
off in the distance, a cloud of black smoke rising 
from Skin City, an urban mass of rebuilt tower 
blocks, bars, bleached white parks and neon 
glow. 
 
At the edge of the Pit, huge elevators, spools of 
cabling twisting and turning constantly, haul the 
living to and from the surface world. The undead 
seldom wander up there, for the living treat 
them with disdain and horror, and there is little 
to be gained from a world they’ve left behind. 
 


 
 
Skin City 
 
Skin City. Old Dead London Town. The Big Wreckage. 
The place it all came down. Most of us live down there, in 
all the hustle and bustle. On clear days, you can almost 
see the sky through the smog, make out a little wisp of 
cloud or some blue vapour. Most of the time, though, it’s 
like being back underground, standing in the shadow of the 
ruins and all the new builds the ‘enterprising population’ 
have cobbled back together. Skin City’s full of the dead, 
but it’s also one of the few places the living can still be 
found down here. Maybe it’s the music. Maybe it’s the 
people. Maybe it’s the cheap thrills, flesh for sale, devil-
worshipping hell clubs or just the typical rich-kid 
attractions, beaming out in neon haze and a deluge of 
cocaine. Either way… guess it’s home. 
 
Skin City is the biggest settlement within the Pit, 
a melting pot of cultures, creeds and corpses 
built out of the ruins of the old City of London. 
Walking down its streets is like taking a tour 
through history. You’ll pass the caved-in tunnels 
of an underground railway, a Victorian cistern 
choked with moss and stagnant water, the 
looming ruins of Big Ben jutting out of a 
scrapheap and the hastily rebuilt skyscrapers 
that can never seem to rise high enough to see 
the sky. The city’s a mish-mash of the modern 
and the old. For every cable-choked neon street 
of bars, pubs and roaring heavy metal 
nightclubs, there’s a quiet corner given over to a 
mouldering cemetery, the remains of a bridge, 
half a dozen council flats that miraculously 
survived the fall. It’s loud, smelly, sweaty and 
gruesomely alive. Even if most of its population 
are dead. 
 
Skin City is a place defined by its music scene. 
Necromusicians and living touring bands alike 
flock to its arenas and clubs, hungry for the 
chance at a record deal, thirty seconds of fame 
or the attention of the Arch-Devils lurking below. 
Infamous venues like the Hammer Smashed 
Apollo, the fume-drenched C02 Academy and 
the Royal Albert Hall (lovingly restored by a gang 
of historical recreationists) play host to 
notorious mega-bands and wannabe tryhards, 
always buzzing with noise, booze and rock-and-
roll. From the steel and glass at the centre of the 
city, the businessmen watch and scheme. 
Though they can’t pin down a Necromusician’s 
spirit, nor truly own their souls, that won’t stop 
them from trying. More than one band has fallen 
victim to a Skin City contract, reduced to touring 
dead-end venues to squeeze the last ounces of 
cash out of their damaged legacy. 
 
The people of Skin City are diverse, reflective of 
the cultural breadth of London in the days 
before the fall. There are districts given over to 
all manner of communities, and central 
locations such as Trafalgar Square and Hyde 
Park (the latter of which is now awash with 
radioactive debris and magma) play host to 
mass gatherings and traditional fairs, carnivals 
and fetes. Around 85% of Skin City’s population 
are undead, survivors of (or awoken by) the 
collapse of London. The remaining 15% are the 
living, demons in disguise, creatures of the 
Labyrinth or artificial constructs stitched 
together by enterprising surgeons. The living are 
tolerated in Skin City- at least, in the nicer bits. 
On the outskirts, where the going gets tough, it’s 
anyone’s guess as to how long a living interloper 
will survive. 
 
Most Necromusicians will begin their careers in 
Skin City. After all, it’s where the money is, and 
where dreams can- with a little black magic- be 
made real. That’s not to say it offers an easy life. 
Skin City is plagued with all the problems of the 
surface world: greed, arrogance, betrayal, 
inequality. At the fringes, it can be a struggle to 
survive, eking out an uneasy life in a one-bed 
apartment with just enough beer to get you by. 
That said, it’s a place of possibility. One look at 
the glistening towers on the banks of the sunken 
River Thames, and a young Necromusician’s 
head can be filled with all sorts of opportunities. 
The city is also the largest gateway to the 
twisting tunnels of the Labyrinth below. 
 
“Come in, lads. Help yourselves to a quick bevvy- 
there’s some cold ones in the fridge. Cigar, 
anyone? No? Alright, suit yourselves, but I tell 
you, lads, you’re gonna want to enjoy the finer 
things in life. It’s what separates the club 
jockeys from those what play arenas. And that’s 
what you’re gonna do- once we’ve got this 
contract signed.” 
 
“I tell you, lads. You stick with me. I’ve made 
thirteen bands stars of Skin City, and, let me be 
frank, Rotten Zombie Flesh Gorgers will be the 
next. Bit of ink there, mate. Bit of blood, too. 
Sign. Fucking magic.” 


 
 
Sights and Sounds of the Pit 
 
The Townsend Bazaar 
In the burnt-out shell of Covent Garden, the 
Townsend Bazaar sprawls like an iridescent 
multicoloured fungus of colour, spice and noise. 
Here, undead traders from the depths of the 
Labyrinth mingle with surface-dwellers and Skin 
City citizens alike, hawking a dizzying variety of 
produce, artefacts and wares. A stall selling 
jailbroken mobile phones might be right next to 
one selling fresh fruit from the surface, which 
might be next to one selling flesh and skin by the 
pound to surgeons, which might be next to one 
selling accursed relics salvaged from the ruins 
below. The air is alive with barking touts in a 
thousand languages; the smell is exotic, but 
refreshingly familiar. 
 
The Townsend Bazaar is unshackled by the 
record labels or the Archdevils of the Infernal 
Choirs. If it can be sold, it’s being sold. If it can 
be bought, it’s being bought- for the right price. 
It’s just as common to see an undead family 
shopping for some band merch as it is to see a 
suited CEO, flanked by two Flesh Construct 
Bouncers, perusing the wares of daring treasure 
seekers, a fistful of Drachma in his hand. 
 
For Whom the Bell Tolls 
Big Ben lies in ruins. When the Pit opened up, it 
fell two hundred metres straight down onto a 
scrapheap and was then drowned by the 
Thames. Sorry- the tower fell, got smashed and 
drowned. The bell itself survived. Big Ben was 
dragged out of the wreckage by a gang of 
Thrashers and set into an obsidian frame, atop a 
mound of dust overlooking the city. Some say 
the mound was Highgate Cemetery, some that it 
was Notting Hill, others that it’s a mass of burnt 
bodies all fused together. Doesn’t matter- but 
what does are the sonorous peals of the bell 
that mark each passing hour in Skin City. No-
one is sure who rings the bell. But ring it does, 
without fail. 
 
Tottenham- District of Broken Earth 
Tottenham was hit the worst when the city fell, 
splintering into a series of boulders floating on 
black sludge and magma bubbling up from the 
Labyrinth below. Its modern architecture fused 
with the ancient ruins in an aesthetic best 
described as ‘industrial hellscape meets 
sepulchral orthodox cathedral’. Mixed with lava. 
Obviously, this makes it prime real estate for 
aspiring Necromusicians as nothing says ‘heavy 
metal’ quite as emphatically as living next to a 
river of boiling lava in a town house that looks 
like a chain factory fucked a church spire. 
Tottenham is thus home to Skin City’s most 
lively and dangerous indie scene, covered in 
clubs, arenas and dive bars that play host to all 
manner of aspiring young talent- whether they’re 
talented or not. 
 
The outskirts of Tottenham are where the ground 
becomes increasingly unliveable, and where 
servants of the Archdevils begin to lurk. Mires of 
molten rock and brackish effluent from the 
sewer system mingle into the Broken Marshes, 
haunted by spectres of the dead heavens below. 
Those that wander here are tempted by visions 
of glory, fame and wealth; those that give in are 
often found floating dead in the waters, their 
skin scorched off and their souls sucked away. 
This knife-edge between trend and danger is 
what keeps Tottenham feeling fresh and vibrant. 
Perhaps even more so than when the place was 
still alive. 
 
Greenwich- District of Black Rain 
Greenwich is the largest district next to Skin 
City, a warren of shanty towns and tumbledown 
shacks afflicted by a perpetual downpour of oily 
black rain. No-one’s sure where this chilling 
weather comes from, although there are those 
that speculate it was sent by the Archdevils out 
of a sick sense of humour. After all, down in the 
dark, how are the British supposed to complain 
about the horrible weather if the horrible 
weather never comes? The skies above the 
gantries, rope bridges and walkways that 
connect the Greenwich Rookeries are always 
dark and full of stinking fog. Standing on the 
highest eyries, where only the most daring of 
urchins dare to tread, it’s said you can hear 
malevolent whispers from within the storm. 
 
The majority of the Pit’s denizens, outside of 
Skin City, live in Greenwich. This is due to its 
relative safety and the lack of any prominent 
breaches into the dark underworld of the 
Labyrinth below. In addition, close proximity to 
the Gates allows for a constant stream of 
immigrants, traders and other refugees from the 
surface to enter the district, creating a 
thronging, diverse community. That is not to say 
that Greenwich is without its hazards, however. 
Archdevils often prey on the naivety of 
newcomers here, luring them into shady deals in 
too-good-to-be-true clubs, leaving them out of 
pocket and, potentially, out of a soul… 


 
 
Wimbledon- District of Glass and Steel 
Wimbledon is the gated community of the rich 
and powerful, or at least those with pretentions 
to wealth and power. It’s much more like a 
spoiled hive of the nouveau riche and out-of-
touch rockstars who have suckled too hard at 
Mammon’s teat- basically Beverly Hills, but with 
even more delusion and narcissism. Here, 
towering high-rises of polished glass and metal 
hide the sick appetites of the ‘upper classes’ 
from the peons in the city below. Orgies, 
murders, drug abuse, self-abuse, abuse of 
power, organised crime, bribery, blackmail, 
jaywalking… you name it, it happens here, and 
gets covered up. It takes a lot to see you thrown 
out of Wimbledon; it takes a willingness to 
tolerate a lot to secure you a seat at the table. 
 
Despite appearances, Wimbledon isn’t the 
place where the favoured of the Archdevils 
dwell. Instead, it’s just a graveyard for useful 
tools and unwitting fools who are just valuable 
enough to keep on the payroll. They are the 
wayward stars, the down-and-out influencers, 
those with just enough pull to get things done for 
whatever agenda the Archdevils are pushing 
next. When one outlives their usefulness, their 
disappearance is quick and unacknowledged- 
probably a messy end, but one hidden behind 
closed doors. It benefits the Archdevils to sell 
the illusion of the meritocratic, materialist 
dream; it makes it all the easier to lure the next 
unwitting sap into their clutches, spurred on by 
promises of fulfilling their every desire…. 
 
Ealing- District of the Long Death 
Ealing is a strangely intact slice of Edwardian 
London, all beautiful, gabled townhouses and 
refined luxury apartments in tasteful post-gothic 
style. Its streets are well-kept, free of litter and 
dust, and are strangely quiet despite the 
comings and goings of its inhabitants. Here, the 
boundaries between the Pit and the Labyrinth 
are the thinnest. Here, the true chosen of the 
Archdevils dwell, those who wield true power 
whilst the upstarts and popinjays in Wimbledon 
squawk and flap. They are understated, in 
smoked glasses and suits, Venetian masks and 
cocktail dresses, and are undoubtedly amongst 
the most dangerous people in the fallen city. 
 
Many Necromusicians have sought to challenge 
the social elite of Ealing- always to no avail. 
Homes have been torched. Streets have been 
barricaded, windows smashed, demonic 
servants banished back into the depths of the 
Labyrinth. But Ealing still remains. Most 
Necromusicians see it as an affront. An insult to 
a life spent hard rockin’ and hard livin’. But, 
gallingly, there’s frustratingly little they can do 
about it. Lit by gas lamps and the ethereal glow 
of seeping magic bleeding up from the Labyrinth 
below, Ealing is a sinister reminder that, even 
with all the excess and glamour of Skin City, 
there’s always a more insidious power just 
bubbling beneath the surface. 
 
“Awake, are you? Good. Now our lesson can 
begin.” 
 
He’s lying on a mixing desk, naked and shivering. 
His arms have been tied with barbed wire to two 
vinyl record players, their needles shimmering in 
the twilight. She stands over him, adjusting the 
lace cuffs of her shirt. The amethyst beak of her 
Venetian mask is polished to a sheen. Her eyes, 
flaring and violet, stare down at him with cruel 
amusement. 
 
“Before all of this… modern metal, there was a 
finer class of extreme music. Sophisticated, 
technical where it had to be, but with none of the 
adulterations you children seem so keen on. Shall 
we listen to some of it? Just to taste?” 
 
She sets off one of the record players. The 
barbed wire round his wrist begins to retract. The 
sounds of 90s death metal fill the air. She 
conducts the thundering drums with her fingers. 
 
“Beautiful. Simpler music, for a simpler time.” 
 
The barbed wire shreds through the skin of his 
wrist. Bone shivers against metal. He screams. 
She turns off the record, turns to the other. 
 
“A little more, I think.” 
 
The second record crackles into life. Black metal, 
all fuzz and noise like a buzzsaw. She keeps the 
track playing for seven minutes. Enough time for 
his hand to tear itself into pieces. Lovely. 


 
 
Personalities of Skin City 
 
 
Skin City is home to all manner of 
rogues and reprobates, murderers and moguls, 
drummers and demon-possessed psychopaths 
who’ve left Old London far behind. There are 
well-meaning philanthropists and community 
figureheads, trying to scrape together safety for 
the people they’ve sworn to protect, building 
shelters from the homeless out of Hell Iron and 
scraps as they fight off predatory gangs of Dead 
Heads lurking in dark alleys. There are cigar-
puffing executives, full of smoke and money, 
smiling greasily at impressionable youths as 
they agree to sign their lives away. There are 
even devils in human skin, their eyes masked 
behind smoked sunglasses, drinking wine on the 
banks of the Thames as a bound victim squirms 
in the boot of their car. It’s a place full of danger, 
full of character- where a man can make a living, 
but just as surely lose his head. 
 
 
What follows is a quick dramatis 
personae for some of the more… notorious 
personalities of Skin City. They are known by 
reputation, and have left lasting marks on the Pit 
and its people. Cross them with care, 
Necromusician- lest you end up at the bottom of 
the river with a nice pair of concrete shoes… 
 
 
Jackie ‘The Hammer’ Magnolia, People’s 
Champion of Widdershins Lane 
 
 
Widdershins Lane is a run-down street 
on the outskirts of Tottenham, blasted into glass 
and dust by an earthquake in the early days of 
the fall. Its red brick and pebbledash houses 
have fallen into disrepair; trash tangles in the 
gutter, gardens wither away into ash, and a 
sense of despondency hangs heavy over it all 
like an oppressive cloud. The people here are 
amongst Skin City’s poorest, forced out onto the 
edge by years of neglect. But there’s one who 
stands up for them. Who keeps the community 
together. That’s Jackie ‘The Hammer’ Magnolia, 
an East London Necromusician and street punk 
who’s done with the working class paying for 
everyone else’s mistakes. 
 
 
Jackie Hammer, as she’s known to her 
friends, is a towering skeleton with a few slivers 
of meat still hanging off her bones. She must be 
approaching seven foot, her body burned black 
in an industrial accident and animated by a 
ghostly yellow fume. She wears a studded 
leather jacket covered in punk patches that 
casts a looming shadow through her empty 
ribcage. In one hand she grips a neighbourhood 
watch badge, a relic from her past, and in the 
other a fuck-off massive lump hammer wrapped 
in barbed wire and a bad attitude. Most of 
Jackie’s vertebrae have snapped; stress 
fractures from years of headbanging, made all 
the worse now she’s dead. 
 
 
Despite her intimidating appearance, 
Jackie Hammer is one of Skin City’s most 
upstanding citizens. She looks out for struggling 
families, for workers down on their luck, for the 
strung-out, the drunk and the desperate. It’s 
tough love, for sure, but she tries to set them on 
the straight and narrow. One thing’s for certain- 
Jackie Hammer’s never hurt no-one who didn’t 
have it coming. Slum landlords, racist cops, 
overreaching property developers trying to take 
homes away from kids and single mothers- 
they’ve all fallen victim to the fall of her hammer 
like the fall of a neutron star. In her spare time, 
Jackie hangs out at the Dog & Whistle, a seedy 
but characterful pub on the corner of 
Widdershins Lane. The beer’s cheap, and the 
company’s good- as long as no-one’s pint gets 
spilled! 
 
Smiling Johnny, Enforcer of the Regent Street 
Boys 
 
 
Crime is still a fact of life in Skin City- 
the only thing that’s changed is that murderers 
had to get more creative, what with everyone 
being zombies and skeletons and all. No-one 
knows that more clearly than Smiling Johnny, a 
charismatic but terrifying enforcer for the 
Regent Street Boys, one of Skin City’s premier 
extortion rackets. Known for rocking up to small 
bands’ shows, the Boys shake down the easily 
scared for protection money, and have even 
been known to put the screws on the richer and 
more influential bands out there. But the last 
member of the Boys who anyone wants to see is 
Smiling Johnny. When he turns up, you see, 
people die. 
 
 
Smiling Johnny is a small, half-decayed 
elderly gentleman in his mid-sixties, a shock of 
white hair framing a round and gentle face. His 
smile is a brilliant white, and just the right level 
of reassuring. His demeanour is kind and 
pleasant, and he speaks with a mellifluous 
Queen’s English that wouldn’t be out of place on 
an old-time radio broadcast. It’s when he gets 
upset you have to worry. That’s when the patent 


 
 
leather toolbox comes out. That’s when fingers 
get unscrewed, eyeballs pop out of heads and 
bones get broken, one little metatarsal at a time. 
Smiling Johnny never smiles more broadly than 
when he’s torturing- a sadist so cruel that even 
the rest of the Regent Street Boys fear him. 
 
 
Rumour has it that Smiling Johnny was 
once a jazz pianist, before the fall. Rumour has it 
that his musical talent was ripped away from 
him, once demand for anything other than rock, 
metal and classical fizzled out and disappeared. 
He claims he’s not bitter. That he doesn’t hold a 
grudge. But it takes a brave man to discuss 
music with Smiling Johnny. He’s like a coin, 
flipping through the air. You’re never sure what 
side he’ll land on- you’ve just got to hope you 
bet it all the right way. 
 
Ludovic Sz Vladoplan, Necro-Alchemist (PhD) 
 
 
Ludovic Sz Vladoplan is a man most 
people in Skin City will have never heard of. 
However, he’s perhaps touched more lives than 
any other scientist in post-fall history. With 
medicine out the window in the Pit, given that 
most Undead don’t need cough drops or heart 
transplants for a heart that stopped beating six 
years ago, Ludovic decided to re-specialise 
once he found his clinic, buried twelve feet 
under the rubble. He began to experiment with 
the Dead Fluids, with the chemicals of Hell, 
mixing and matching volatile compounds with 
reckless abandon, no longer having to care if 
they gave off poisonous fumes and exploded in 
his hands. Ludovic is the creator of nearly all of 
the post-life hard drugs that swamp the streets 
of Skin City. He’s an eccentric genius. That’s 
code for ‘he’s bat-shit insane’. 
 
 
Ludovic Sz Vladoplan is a young corpse 
with a stained grey labcoat and a manic 
expression. He’s a nervous knot of restless 
energy, siphoning Necromagic from the Angels 
of Death, wired up like a guitar string tuned to 
snap. He’s never without countless clinking 
flasks and alembics, all full of glowing liquid, 
and quickly tires of social norms and niceties in 
his eagerness to get back to the lab. Ludovic has 
spent the last few years flitting between various 
drug syndicates and criminal gangs or, rather, 
they’ve spent the last few years stealing him 
from one another. He doesn’t particularly care- 
give him some chemicals, and watch him go! 
 
 
Ludovic is considered a valuable asset 
by the criminal underworld of Skin City, and has 
been at the centre of many particularly nasty 
turf wars and prisoner trades. It is estimated his 
creations are worth more in aggregate than the 
GDP of most third-world countries. Yet he keeps 
inventing- whether out of sheer boredom, 
restless determination or a need to experiment, 
no-one’s quite sure. Perhaps he’ll only stop 
once he blows his own face off, or when, at last, 
the very last fragments of his brain finally 
disintegrate. 
 
Thomas Hugenot Renfield, Record Executive 
of Skulls and Bone Ltd. 
 
 
Thomas Hugenot Renfield, oldest son of 
the Hugenot-Renfield media dynasty, has a 
smile that’s entirely too wide and white for his 
face. Constantly sweating, he’s a weaselly, 
scrawny specimen, his necrotising skin hung 
loosely over bleached yellow bones and forced 
into an overly expensive suit. He constantly 
dabs at his brow with a handkerchief, dredging 
away dead flesh, pus and blood as well as the 
sweat slicking his tangled black hair. Despite his 
ugliness, Thomas is one of the richest men in 
Skin City. And he intends to keep it that way. 
 
 
Thomas is CEO and managing director 
of the three biggest death metal record labels in 
Skin City. He holds the strings of leading 
Necromusicians: ‘Slash’ Axel, Marianne Ramm 
Ken Stein, Tim ‘Terrifyer’ Goreman, and others 
besides. Those that cross him, or muscle in on 
his business deals, soon find out why he has 
kept his seat for so long. Ruthless, paranoid and 
megalomaniacal, Thomas will stop at nothing to 
make a profit- even if it means burying his 
problems in the depths of the Thames. 
 
 
Speaking to Thomas in his office, you’d 
be forgiven for thinking him a mild-mannered 
and well-spoken aristocrat. But all it takes is the 
tiniest seed of doubt in his mind about you to 
trigger his neurotic and unstable behaviour. At 
that point, when he considers you a threat- well, 
that’s when you have to worry. 
 
 
Kill him. 
Kill his fucking family. 
Kill his dog, and throw the corpse in the 
river with him. 
No-one fucks with my bands. 
Nobody. 


 
 
The Girl with the Amber Eyes 
 
 
There are whispers in neon-drenched 
clubs of a spectre that follows the unwary 
through the lights. She takes the form of a 
beautiful young woman, dressed in stylish 
clothes, grinning lasciviously as she dances to 
the pulsing rhythms and thundering drums. Her 
eyes are always concealed behind a pair of 
smoked sunglasses, and her skin is unnaturally 
white- the colour of fresh ash burnt away from 
charcoal. Yet she is strangely magnetic, drawing 
the unwary and the vulnerable to her until 
they’re lost in her movement. It’s then they 
disappear. 
 
 
Victims who leave the club with the girl 
are found brutally dismembered, their organs, 
flesh and limbs strewn around their homes in 
half-chewed organs of violence and pain. 
Inspection indicates that all the victims stayed 
alive until the very last moment, sustained by 
injections of adrenaline and necromagical 
incantations. Very few have escaped her- those 
that have report glistening amber eyes behind 
her sunglasses, full of animal hunger, a 
predatory intent that swells beyond the limits of 
her flesh. 
 
 
In reality, the Girl with the Amber Eyes is 
a Greater Demon, consuming the souls of its 
victims after basting them in primeval terror. 
Sworn to the Way of All Flesh, its true form is an 
enormous knot of scabrous limbs. Arms and 
legs cascade over one another so densely as it 
make it impossible to imagine a body below 
them at all. Grasping hands and claws rip and 
tear; flailing elbows, shoulders, knees and feet 
bludgeon and trample. So, if you do see a girl in 
the club, dancing feverishly, avoid giving in to 
temptation. It might just save your life… 
 
 
 


 
 
Skin City in the Summer. Fucking horrible place. You 
can see the sun filtering down through the smoke of 
the Pit, and remember what it was like to be human. 
 
Take a walk through the streets. Pass by the clubs, 
the bars, the record stores and dead ruins of old 
London town. Pass by the has-beens, the sick, the 
dying, the dead, those that are trying to die. The 
Thames runs black these days. No fish. Just bones. I 
stare out over the wharfs and remember the sea. 
 
Below, the Labyrinth is waiting. Only a matter of 
time before you’re drawn down away from the faint 
sunlight, the possibility of once again being free. The 
tunnels engulf you and the sky is replaced by rock, 
steel and flesh. Time dies. You forget. Only the music 
and the corpses remain. 
 
Skin City in the summer. No wonder so many of us 
drink. You hate to be reminded of everything you 
used to have.
 
 


 
 
The Labyrinth 
 
Below the Pit lies the Labyrinth, an impossible 
maze of tunnels that consist of the mangled 
wreckage of Heaven and Hell. Plus some 
unlucky bits of London that got caught in the 
blast. There are those that swear they’ve seen 
the dome of Madame Tussaud’s splintered 
through a heavenly colonnade above a river of 
liquid flesh. There are others who claim they’ve 
walked pulsating passageways wrought of half-
crushed underground trains and glass splinters 
from Waterloo station. There are others still who 
say they’ve stood in cathedrals of glowing white 
crystal, shot through with black and brackish 
fluid that seems to pop and bubble with livid, 
bloodshot eyes. Point is, the Labyrinth is a 
strange place. It is also uniquely fucking hostile. 
 
There are many gates into the Labyrinth, some 
of which are known to the people of the Pit and 
others of which are secrets buried in old 
basements, out of sight and out of mind. The 
upper levels near these gates are mapped- at 
least, in as much as you can map an ever-
twisting maze of tunnels, non-Euclidean 
architecture and hellish distortion. The deeper 
you travel, the more disorienting and ersatz the 
Labyrinth becomes. A passageway may loop 
back on itself like a Mobius strip; another may 
feed into a completely different series of 
chambers, depending on an explorer’s 
emotional state; another may devolve into a 
fleshy tube, then a metallic industrial gantry, 
then a colonnade of heavenly pillars, then a 
swamp of brackish oil and water. The only 
consistency is the presence of the broken ruins 
of Heaven. Old cathedrals, statues crushed to 
rubble, the tattered banners of ancient divinity 
trodden into the mud. 
 
Explorers in the Labyrinth are after a whole 
variety of things. Of course, the Archdevils are 
driven by a relentless desire to reach the 
tripartite corpse of God herself, but most don’t 
even know the truth of what lies below London, 
much less that God rots somewhere down in the 
depths. Necromusicians are often just seeking 
hellish artefacts, remnants of Heaven to sell to 
pawnbrokers, knowledge (perhaps) of rituals 
and rites that might let them un-live just a little 
longer. Scavengers may just rip out raw 
materials, whereas the truly insane may just be 
looking for a cheap thrill and the satisfaction of 
mapping out the unmappable. 
 
“First time I went down into the Pit, it was just to scrape 
together enough to pay rent on my shithole of an 
apartment up in the heights. The landlady- rotten bitch, 
not three fingers and teeth left in her- was breathin’ 
down my neck. Metaphorically speaking. I wasn’t in a 
band at the time, much less working for a living. I just 
needed a quick score. So I joined up with a troupe of 
explorers and went on down. Seems like a long time ago 
now.” 
 
Geography of the Labyrinth 
 
The Upper Layers 
The Upper Layers of the Labyrinth are the most 
stable. Their layout seldom changes, and most 
passageways are free of hellish or heavenly 
influence. Tunnels of bare rock wind through 
London’s industrial ruins like worms tracking 
through a corpse. Here and there, you can see 
the odd ancient ruin- Roman, perhaps, or 
medieval British, or some old Victorian house 
buried down in the silt. The smell is all stale 
earth and old memories. Communities of Old 
Dusters can be found in pockets, out of sight of 
what London became. 
 
There is very little left in the Upper Layers that 
hasn’t already been scavenged. The raw 
materials- metals, stone, petrified wood, oil and 
gas deposits- have been harvested and sold off 
for a cheap buck. Wiring has been stripped from 
the walls, broken pipes have been salvaged and 
even the remaining graves have been broken 
into and robbed. Most of the corridors lie silent 
now. Empty chambers, empty staircases. A 
strange lull, an antechamber to the true terrors 
below. 
 
That’s not to say the Upper Layers are without 
their dangers, however. Plenty of killers and 
creatures wander their halls, driven towards the 
surface by the sights, smells and sounds of un-
life. It’s like a heartbeat to a predator. In 
addition, those exiled from, ahem, ‘polite’ 
society often find refuge in the Upper Layers. 
Quiet enough that they can lay low, dangerous 
enough that they can ply their sick trades to 
excess. 
 
Just be careful of which passageway you walk 
down, yeah? The Upper Layers may be the least 
dangerous parts of the Labyrinth- but you’re still 
exploring the very literal gateway into the Hell 
below. 
 


 
 
The Circles of Hell 
Below the Upper Layers, the domains of the 
Infernal Choirs start to bleed into the Labyrinth’s 
architecture. Each Choir once ruled over a circle 
of Hell, before the ruins of Heaven smashed 
them into chaos and confusion. The borders of 
the Circles of Hell are more like guidelines now. 
A cathedral limned with snow-capped oak trees 
may suddenly give way to a boiling pool of acid 
surrounded by stunt track, which in turn may 
become a poisonous fume-filled factory 
churning with biomechanical devices. The 
Labyrinth ceases to behave like real space here. 
The laws of Hell supersede the laws of creation. 
And, here and there, the volatile marks of 
Heaven’s fall can be seen. 
 
The first Circle belongs to the Angels of Death. It 
is a place of leather, chains, magma and the 
overwhelming stench of adrenaline and petrol. 
The very air thrums with barely-contained rage; 
the walls vibrate restlessly, unable to contain 
their desire for speed. The landscape often 
cracks and rearranges itself in a frenzy of broken 
glass and metal. The rock beneath your feet 
opens up into rivulets of lava and superheated 
blood, waterfalls of motor oil and rusting car 
parts that rush in tidal waves through defiled 
castles and looted monasteries. It was here the 
sin of wrath was punished in ages past- the 
screams and roars of anger from old sinners 
reverberates in dark corners, and irrepressible 
heat washes over everything. 
 
The second Circle belongs to Apokalypsis. Here 
was the sin of sloth punished. The Circle 
resonates with emptiness, a sorrowful air and 
gentle cold rain flowing through dark and 
claustrophobic halls. Everything here is full of 
regrets and dead memories. A broken home, 
smashed glass on the bedroom floor. A vacant 
church, the organ standing idle, the words of the 
Bible tear-stained and meaningless. A train 
station platform, hollowed out, no arrivals or 
departures or even the sound of human life. The 
second Circle is a testament to mankind’s 
inexorable entropy and fundamental loneliness; 
it can be jarring, to pass from the noise and 
chaos of the first Circle, into a place as gloomy 
as this. 
 
The third Circle belongs to the Corpse 
Crusaders, and is the place where the sin of 
envy was punished. The parapets and keeps of 
Heaven here lie in ruins, intermingled with the 
blood-soaked tiles of its battlefields. Hell has 
rebuilt what fortresses and redoubts it can, 
overlaying alabaster tiles with forged iron, 
banners in gold and crimson and towering 
edifices of black stone. Here, the Labyrinth gives 
way to impossible, grandiose caverns, through 
which the living are hunted for sport and the 
claims of the undead are held onto with a fierce, 
feudal pride that is as unbending as steel. 
Modern ruins and wreckage have largely been 
swept away; it is in this Circle that a plethora of 
rare ensorcelled items have been dredged from 
the dust, hoarded jealously by the ersatz lords 
and ladies that claim dominion over the rubble. 
 
The fourth Circle belongs to the Cult of the 
Frozen Moon, though there is no true rulership 
over the wild and lawless Circle of gluttony. Here 
there stretches a fathomless and twisting forest 
of gnarled black trees, shod in ice and acrid 
snow. Tunnels twist and churn, transforming 
from stone into throats of roots and fallen leaves 
before giving way to dark and shadowed vistas 
of pines and mountains, shot through with 
distant howls. Glaciers freeze shut over 
London’s bones; frozen lakes reveal heavenly 
ruins trapped in the depths; trails of footprints 
and blood betray the savage denizens that lurk 
just out of sight. There is never a feeling of safety 
here, whether in a claustrophobic passage or 
beneath a sunken, sallow moon. Only hunters- 
and those they hunt. 
 
The fifth Circle is a churning factory of brushed 
steel, conveyor belts and rattling chains, sewn 
through with livid flesh in service of the Engine 
of Destruction. Here was the sin of pride 
punished, but little of that old purpose remains 
in the post-industrial architecture and ceaseless 
clank and churn of the machines. Jets of 
scalding steam and pools of oil intermingle with 
nightmarish Rube-Goldberg devices that seem 
to have every purpose and yet no purpose at all. 
Incomprehensible mechanisms are bolted 
together, infused with necromagic, given life 
then violently disassembled, all while 
monstrosities of cybernetics and dead organs 
lurch across the deck plates singing morbid 
dirges and funereal working chants. There is 
barely anything left of Heaven here, save for that 
shovelled into the belching furnaces. 
 
The sixth Circle is enshrouded in a sweet-
smelling mist, a pleasant wash of illusions and 
miasma that appears like a beautiful English 
garden. Pavilions and manor houses are 
elegantly dotted throughout chambers of green 
grass and white water; the smell of roses and 
lace doilies mingles with the sound of chamber 


 
 
music, whilst trailing willow branches gently 
twist in the sound of the wind. This beguiling 
illusion is just that, though- an illusion. Here 
was the sin of lust punished, and here is where 
the Legion of the Night preys. Those taken in by 
the phantasmagoria are ensnared and drained 
of blood. The reality of the sixth Circle is a 
hideous warren of spidery insect nests, webs 
and gnawed tunnels that play host to lurking 
horrors and insidious traps. A sparkling fountain 
is actually the lair of a bulbous, glistening 
trapdoor spider demon; a manor stairway 
actually winds down into the mouth of a 
chitinous mass of limbs and frothing saliva. 
Everything here is an ever-present danger- the 
only truths are the ruins of Heaven, which resist 
the demons’ trickery. 
 
Finally, the seventh Circle, where greed was 
punished, abandons all reason and becomes a 
pulsating, wet and sticky mass of organic 
tunnels like the inside of an intestine. Surgical 
chambers wrought of livid bone mark this 
demesne as that of The Way of All Flesh, 
maddened artisans of the undead form. Here, 
their hideous creations shudder through 
archways of spinal columns and optic nerves; 
baroque wreckage is overgrown by cancerous 
tissues; brain matter and bile flow like rivers 
through cracks in a pavement of ugly red-raw 
scabs. The stench is hideous, almost 
indescribable, like a morass of rotting meat, 
semi-liquid, buzzing with flies and industrial 
chemicals. It is hot and damp, making the skin 
slick with sweat and dripping fluids. Doorways 
become knotted sphincters; trapdoors blink like 
eyes, and lights blossom only from pockets of 
trapped gas belched out of the suppurating 
walls. 
 
Below the seventh Circle lies the Deep 
Labyrinth, an uncharted place wherein lies the 
heart of Heaven and the tripartite corpse of God 
herself. No two accounts of this place are the 
same. Reality shifts in response to God’s violent 
dreams. The memory of angels haunts the steps 
of trespassers. Some speak of towering silver-
white citadels, fluttering with winged banners of 
snow and gold. Others speak of fractal 
staircases and impossible geometry, where 
cardinal directions fail and imagination 
becomes a surer guide. Some speak of visible 
darkness, liquid thoughts and an endless 
susurrus of Enochian whispers. None have ever 
seen its heart. Few have even penetrated deeper 
than a passageway. It is a terrifying place. And it 
resonates with unearthly power. 
From the Logbook of Andrew Draper, 
Antiquarian Explorer of Skin City, c. 
November 13th, 2024 
- 
Recovered from Draper’s Corpse in the 
Seventh Circle of Hell 
 
 
“I forced my way through the 
translucent yellow membrane and out of the 
corridors of flesh. I found myself standing 
at the foot of a staircase, that made its 
way both up and down, above and below me. 
I took a step, and felt my body lurch. I 
looked through a crystalline kaleidoscope as 
all three of me walked in a different 
direction. I focused, and imagined the 
descent. White lights flickered as they 
swallowed up my sight. Nothing to be done. 
I made my way down.” 
 
 
“I walked the length of a romantic 
notion before catching my breath. Time 
flows around me- I see its colour. The 
pantheon here is made of oak, and it shines 
with silver tears. I stood in a hallway made 
of white marble, and gazed up at a statue 
of the archangels that simply did not exist. 
I took a scroll of Enochian text, and 
watched as the words unravelled and 
touched but lightly on my mind. God’s 
words. Full of memory. I shuddered, and I 
left.” 
 
 
“I am still in the hallway. But it’s 
like staring through a mirror. I can’t see 
myself. I search the ashes. I find my own 
teeth. I will die here. I am dying here.” 
 


 
 
A Day in the Life of a 
Necromusician 
 
11:20: “Wake up”, i.e. the time when the 
booze wears off. 
11:25: Brush tooth. Put on denim patch 
jacket and jeans. Chase last night’s 
hangover with a Guiness and a smoke. 
Regret un-life. 
11:30: Head to band practice. 
12:30: Actually start band practice 
because all the other fuckers are late. 
13:00: Realise that it’d be more fun to 
take a joyride into the Labyrinth wired on 
Hell Snow than keep practicing. 
13:15: Smash into the Labyrinth in the 
van. 
17:00: Smash out of the Labyrinth chased 
by forty-six Brimstone Gargoyles and a 
Resonance Devil. 
17:30: Trade in a handful of angelic metal 
for a twelve-pack of special brew at Lucky 
Dave’s Dive-In Offy. 
18:00: Get to the venue for the night. 
18:15: Call upon Archdevil patron for a 
necromagical suffusion to make the show 
halfway passable. 
18:30: Piss off said Archdevil and 
temporarily lose a limb. 
18:45: Play the warm-up slot at Joey 
Ray’s. Crowd’s a bit quiet. 
20:45: Watch the headline slot. Throw a 
beer at the singer. Lose another limb, but 
permanently this time. 
22:45: Stagger to the afterparty. 
23:45: Burn down the afterparty. 
01:45: Collapse into ‘bed’. Don’t sleep, 
because you’re dead. 
 
Most Necromusicians aren’t big fucking 
rockstars. They live lives of struggle, strung out 
on the edge, forever dancing on the borderline of 
unlife and Second Death until the devils claim 
their due. Most were desperate when they sold 
their souls. Just hoping to hold on, as their city 
collapsed around them, as everything they knew 
and loved slid into the abyss. Some welcomed 
the Archdevils with open arms, eager to escape 
the crushingly boring mundanity of city life- the 
fire of Hell seems exciting when the alternative 
is a grey concrete purgatory. Some even sought 
the Archdevils out, throwing away their mortality 
for a chance to be something better. Sad, really. 
For every breakthrough star, hundreds more end 
up dead. 
 
 
The average Necromusician is part of a 
heavy metal band on the Skin City scene. 
Perhaps a punk group, or denim-clad hard 
rockers, or jazzy avante-garde hipsters, or even 
the odd classical orchestra- the results are the 
same. They live from paycheck to paycheck, 
show to show, sustained on a diet of hard liquor 
(except the straight-edgers) and harder fighting. 
Un-live fast, and never die. That’s the mantra 
most of them follow. After all, you might not be 
rich, but you are still an undead rocker with 
necromagical powers- that’s got to count for 
something! 
 
 
A typical day-in-the-life might start with 
a hangover and end with killing a seven-headed 
deer demon in a frozen wasteland shadowed by 
the wreck of London Bridge. It might end with a 
killer show at the C02 Arena, but have started 
with the signing in blood of a tricky deal with a 
devilish promoter, her smile showing just one 
too many spiky teeth. It might involve ploughing 
through a bloody river in a Humvee covered in 
rusty springs, or stealing booze from an off-
licence in Croydon, or skipping stones over the 
black water of the Thames. If a life in 
Necromusic is one thing, it’s certainly never 
dull. 
 
 
Ultimately, all Necromusicians are 
drawn to the Labyrinth by the will of their 
Archdevil patrons. They explore the forgotten 
depths where Heaven and Hell intertwine. Most 
who delve too deeply fall victim to the horrors 
deep in the dark, and have their souls claimed 
by their infernal masters. Some, however, 
survive. These are the legends. These are the 
stars who’s stories will never die. 
 
 


 
 
The Skin City Music Industry 
 
 
“Yah, Tarquin, baby, I’m telling you- 
metalcore, all that screamo stuff from the mid-
2000s, that’s out of fashion, yah? Yah, I know the 
millennials are nostalgic for it, but it’s not where 
the sales are at, you know? You know? Yeah, you 
know. You’ve got to tell your new signs- what are 
their names, again? ‘Demonocracy’, ‘White Blue 
Sky’ and ‘Terrorfex’? Yeah, those guys. You’ve got 
to tell them that OSDM is on its way back. And 
thrash, don’t forget thrash. No, the 80s revival is 
coming, Tarquin, trust. Squeeze in a bit of post-
punk, too. Kids love that these days. Come on, 
Tarquin. Get with it. When I have ever lied to 
you?” 
 
- 
Randy Rogers, Record Executive and 
Professional Liar 
 
 
The music industry in Skin City is a cutthroat 
rat race where only the most brutal thrive and 
only the strong even survive. For every stadium-
filling, best-selling, metal-as-fuck megastar, 
there are fifty struggling garage bands playing 
the local pub in hopes of a scratchcard and 
enough cash to spin the fruities. Rising to the 
top requires grit, determination and an absolute 
willingness to do whatever it takes to cut your 
way through the competition. Sometimes 
literally. The law’s quite relaxed, these days, 
about that sort of thing. 
 
Heavy metal, punk and hard rock are the 
genres that dominate the Pit- perhaps 
unsurprisingly, given their connection to the 
Infernal Choirs. Gone are the glossy pop stars of 
yesteryear, the pleasant, inoffensive sing-a-long 
tunes, the mass-produced pap the masses used 
to eat up. In their place is the thunder of guitars, 
and the screams of singers who have sworn 
their un-lives away to Hell. Metal sells again, 
baby, and the record execs are cashing in.  
 
Countless new labels have sprung up, eager 
to take advantage of the explosion in the scene. 
Most are spearheaded by Greater Demons in the 
guise of brassy-eyed humans, their smiles far 
too wide as they sign contracts in dirty 
backrooms over whiskey and cigars. The big 
bands are in the Archdevils’ pockets. They 
signed over their rights, their dignity and their 
integrity a long time ago, and in doing so reap 
the rewards of power and fame. However, below 
them, staring defiantly up, are the true 
Necromusicians. The cult hitmakers. The living 
legends. The down-but-not-outer’s, metal 
diehards, stick-it-to-the-man until they die’s, all 
raising a big middle finger to the glass towers 
above. 
 
Going to a metal show in Skin City is quite 
literally taking your life into your hands. It’s not 
just pyro, props and animatronics any more. 
Remember those old pearl-clutchers who 
worried that heavy metal would summon 
demons, or unleash the antichrist, or cover its 
listeners in blood and sin? Well, it turns out they 
were right. A metal show is an exhilarating thrill 
ride of camaraderie, explosions, good-natured 
violence and brimstone ‘n’ fire. The bigger the 
venue, like Skin City’s C02, the more there is. 
Even in the smaller clubs and bars, aggressive 
local bands bang out incredible live 
experiences, their musical talents spurred on by 
the influence of their devilish patrons. 
 
Skin City is home to all sorts of musical 
subcultures and scenes surrounding punk, rock 
and metal. It’s not uncommon to walk into a bar 
and be surrounded by 70s punks- with their 
mohawks, studded collars and confrontational 
attitudes- 80s thrashers- with their tight denim 
jeans, vague smell of Stella Artois and weeded-
out smiles- 90s grunge fans- red-and-black 
chequered shirts, floppy fringes, moody 
expressions- and even 2000s scene kids- all 
skinny jeans, blue streaks and rub-on tattoos 
over necrotising skin.  
 
There are many stories about the 
debauchery and violence inherent in the Skin 
City music scene. Sadly, when money, fame, 
drugs and sex are involved, a lot of people turn 
out to be, well, genuine pieces of shit. The 
demons don’t help matters, of course, feeding 
as they do off of strife and conflict, but there’s 
hope for the scene yet. Young bands, hungry and 
ambitious, full of new ideas and misplaced 
confidence, are starting to explode across the 
city as more of the Labyrinth below is explored. 
They are supported by cunning Archdevils 
looking to snatch power off the Princes and 
Kings, and fly in the face of the stale 
conventional sounds the big bands have been 
putting out for years. Who knows? Perhaps 
everything is about to change… 
 
 


 
 
Tears of a Clown 
 
 
The walls of the tunnel were melting. Copper rivets 
burst; the brushed steel ran with streaks like oily rain on 
glass. Its legs coiled out of the vat, hairy, bolted with 
chains in chitin, hundreds of them stretching towards us, 
clicking and squirming. Its face… God, its face… 
Thousands of faceted eyes. Mandibles like razors. Like 
someone had stepped on a camel spider and gelled its 
remains together with a broken clock. Its voice rasped. 
 
DELICIOUS 
 
 
It lunged towards me. I gripped the keys. No juice 
left. Silk and blood spat from its lips. And then… Then 
she was there. 
 
She wore jester’s motley in red and blue. Steel 
strings wrapped around her fists- she swung a flail of 
razor-edged bass guitars, seven or eight. The sound was 
incredible. A gallop. A thunder. She was crying as she 
smashed the monster open. She looked tired. I didn’t 
understand why. 


 
 
Myths and Monsters 
 
 
The Pit and the Labyrinth are home to 
monstrosities beyond imagining, the sick 
byproduct of Heaven’s fall, the breaking of 
Death and the feverish dreams of the devils 
below. In Skin City, across the ruins of London, 
these creatures of relatively tame- undead, 
stitched constructs, the odd infernal familiar 
scampering and capering after its erstwhile 
master. But, the deeper you go, the more mind-
bending and ersatz they become. Demons take 
on increasingly volatile and disturbing forms; 
spirits of gas and steam erupt from factory 
valves; formless horrors squirm through vents; 
and the corpses of angels sing in Enochian, a 
maddening buzz that drives all who hear it 
insane. What follows is a brief account of these 
creatures, and whereabouts they may be 
found… 
 
The Undead 
 
The Undead are the most common 
creatures of the Pit, being the shambling 
corpses of those killed during London’s fall. 
Those buried in cemeteries around the city also 
rose from their graves as Old Dusty Dandies, or 
‘Dusters’, their minds worn from centuries in the 
coffin, the crypt or the tomb. Walk down any 
given street in Skin City, and you’ll see at least a 
half-dozen Undead. Zombies, ghouls, 
revenants, wights- call ‘em what you will. Just 
keep an eye. That one’s trying to steal your 
wallet. 
 
 
The Undead do not need to breathe, 
eat, drink or sleep. Their hearts are still and un-
beating. Of course, that doesn’t stop them. Old 
habits are hard to break. Besides, for some 
reason, the dead still get drunk, fat, tired or off 
their brains on drugs- most physicians think it’s 
the Archdevils and their sick sense of humour. 
Regardless, in a London pub these days, it’s not 
uncommon to find a skeleton in a suit drinking 
alongside a punk zombie and a half-formed 
carcass of an elderly businesswoman. Death is 
just a fact of life, and, eventually, you get used to 
the smell. 
 
 
The Undead come in a variety of shapes 
and sizes, as well as rates of decomposition. 
The very oldest are nothing more than rags and 
bone, their flesh having rotted away into dust. 
The youngest look almost human, except for 
maybe a yellow-white pallor and some 
suspiciously lethal-looking wounds about their 
temples. Some Undead even take great pains to 
preserve themselves for as long as they can- a 
deathless fashion industry, focused on 
preservatives, stylish brands of formaldehyde 
and other noxious embalming fluids has quite a 
pull in Skin City. 
 
 
Most Undead are no different from you 
and me. Just people, living their un-lives as 
happily as they can. Most just want to be left 
alone, allowed to exist, to make ends meet, 
even as anti-Undead prejudices outside the Pit 
continue to rise and give weight to zombie 
hunters, vampire killers and other fantasists 
with too much time on their hands. In fairness, if 
an Undead un-lives long enough, they do 
eventually go a bit loose in the old brain pan. 
Slavering, biting people, that sort of thing. Still, 
that’s no excuse for these wannabe Van 
Helsing’s. Get a real job, losers. Like playing 
drums in a garage death metal band. Yeah. Sure 
showed them. 
 
“So, I’m dead? Like, really dead?” 
 
“’fraid so, pal. In fairness, most of us down 
‘ere are. Comes with the territory. I was 
there when the city went, y’know? Just 
eatin’ a bagel in Trafalgar Square. Next 
thing I know, I’m six feet under Nelson’s 
fuckin’ column.” 
 
“Whoa. So what do I do? Is there, like, a 
form to fill out? Like, a death certificate- 
or undead certificate, or something?” 
 
“Calm your jets, lad. It ain’t so special. 
You’re just part of the city now, same as 
the rest of us. Find a job that won’t rot 
you too fast, and you’ll do fine. Perks is 
that you don’t gotta eat, or sleep, or shit. 
Welcome to your new life, eh?” 
 
“Damn. I guess that being undead and poor 
still sucks as much as being alive…” 


 
 
‘Great Britain’- The Living Beyond the 
Pit 
 
 
When London fell, the United Kingdom 
fell into chaos. Riots and looting broke out in 
every surviving city; old prejudices and hatreds 
flared; the greedy and the desperate made bids 
for power, stirring up violent coups with 
promises they had no intention to keep. The 
country was placed under martial law, and was 
eventually corralled into a semblance of order 
by the Black Country Sabbat Interregnum 
(BCSI), a military government formed in 
Birmingham that somehow cowed the other 
cities. There is speculation that Birmingham’s 
musical heritage is the source of its power; 
heavy metal was born there, and the Archdevils’ 
influence waxes strong in its presence.  
 
 
The BCSI rules over what’s left of ‘Great 
Britain’ with an iron fist. It reviles the magic of 
the Pit, and prevents the Undead from spreading 
far beyond its walls. Its people are taught to fear 
and hate the supposed ‘devil-worshipping scum’ 
that dwell in the ruins of London; a new wave of 
radical Christianity has seized the BCSI, for 
better or for worse. Given the madness of their 
dead God, chances are it’s the latter. Bishops 
and clerics alike hear whispers they interpret as 
divine ministration; more than likely, they’re 
nothing more than crazed ramblings, driving the 
BCSI to increasingly erratic displays of power 
and loyalty that ultimately mean nothing. 
 
 
Despite the religious tyranny of their 
rulers, the people of ‘Great Britain’ often 
empathise with those trapped in the darkness of 
the Pit. Necromusic is a powerful drug- the spirit 
of rebellion, sex, drugs and rock ‘n’ roll made 
manifest. Many escape the fascistic tedium of 
their lives by fleeing to Skin City- better to burn 
out than fade away, after all. Some have even 
formed rebellious cells in support of the 
Undead, sabotaging the regime of the BCSI as 
best they can from within. 
 
SIBLINGS IN SUFFERING. 
The Undead are NOT your enemy. 
They lie, to divide, to segregate, to blind us to 
the truth. 
The BCSI is NOT freedom. 
The BCSI is NOT strength. 
Long live the spirit of rebellion, and long live rock 
and roll! 
Rebels Without a Cause- The 
Satanarchists, the Nearly-Dead and the 
Nomads 
 
 
Those that choose to abandon ‘Great 
Britain’ and dwell in the Pit flock to one of two 
causes- the chaotic, free-wheeling 
Satanarchists or the hedonistic fatalists known 
as the Nearly-Dead. The Undead find both of 
these factions quite quaint, in their own way, but 
tolerate them nonetheless. Who’s to say what 
people get to do with their lives, and their 
newfound freedom? 
 
 
The Satanarchists are devil-worshipping 
punks with but a single rule: life is nothing but 
glorious chaos. They revel in the madness of a 
life unshackled by the fear of death, raving into 
the night in sweat-stained clubs to the roaring 
sound of hardcore, grind and dissonant death. 
Perhaps one day, when they finally bite it, the 
Archdevils will take an interest in them, raising 
them up to join the ranks of the Necromusicians 
they idolise so much. Satanarchists are almost 
universally edgy, obsessed with the occult, 
conspiracy theories, ghosts, demons, Heaven 
and Hell. Though they’re often completely wrong 
about all of them. Ah, well. Let the kids have 
their fun. They’re not hurting anyone- yet. 
 
 
The Nearly-Dead are best described as 
wannabe Undead posers. They paint their 
disgusting smooth and living flesh in corpse 
paint, spray on some pestilent eau de zombi and 
imagine what life must be like as a member of 
the walking dead. Too attached to life to actually 
go through with surrendering their souls to the 
Archdevils, the Nearly-Dead are amongst the 
most ardent fans of Necromusic and all the 
major rockstar personalities associated with it. 
They buy the merch, come to the shows and talk 
a good talk- and sometimes even fight a good 
fight. Pays to keep ‘em sweet. That way, they 
keep on paying too. 
 
 
The Nomads are self-imposed exiles 
from ‘Great Britain’, living outside the law. They 
form travelling communes of closely-bonded 
families, unwilling to submit to the religious 
xenophobia of the BCSI. They are traders and 
couriers, carriers of news from the outside world 
and of relics from the depths of the Labyrinth. 
Without their merchant caravans, the resources 
of the Pit would dry up quick- as would the trade 
in magical artefacts that has blossomed up in 
secret across the BCSI’s territory… 


 
 
The Stitched- Flesh Constructs and 
Necromagical Engineering 
 
 
Not all the Undead wandering the Pit 
are the corpses of the recently deceased. Some 
are constructs, amalgams of flesh and bone 
built by mad scientists, Necromagicians and 
(shudder) mathematicians as slaves, 
companions or even pets. These 
Frankensteinian creatures are masses of 
stitched skin stretched over metal ligatures, 
cybernetics, rusting machinery and demonic 
incantations, given life by a mysterious cocktail 
of chemicals known as the Dead Fluids. This 
substance, harvested from wells deep below in 
the tunnels of the Labyrinth, glows a neon green 
and animates any dead tissue, no matter how 
damaged or malformed. That’s why you get 
groaning Frankenroadies hauling amplifiers at 
shows. That’s why you get towering Colossi 
guarding the darker passages of the Labyrinth. 
That’s why you get sutured dogs, cats and even 
parakeets squawking at you from the rafters of 
Madam Morticia’s Masterful Menagerie (now on 
Smith Street- 50% off your first rat. Bring the 
kids!). 
 
 
The Stitched sometimes retain 
elements of their original personalities when 
they reawaken. Others are torn between one 
identity and the next, never certain who they 
really are amidst the sea of voices in their head. 
Some embrace the confusion and become 
amalgamate personalities, collectives of people 
that speak with one voice. Some, sadly, fall into 
insanity, gibbering and wailing as the unnatural 
form they occupy refuses to associate with 
itself. 
 
 
In the past, the Stitched were almost 
exclusively created as labourers or, let’s be 
honest, as slaves. Most of them, quite rightly, 
took exception to that. Once an enterprising 
Flesh Construct realises they’re being exploited, 
and that they’re twice the size, strength and 
speed of the dorky necromancer that animated 
them, it’s only a matter of time before they 
perform some… expedient emancipation. 
Communities of liberated Stitched are common 
amongst anarchist communes, in downtrodden 
districts of Skin City, even in isolated pockets of 
the Labyrinth where they feel they can be left 
well enough alone. 
 
 
 
Ghasts- the Echoes of the Broken Machine 
 
 
When the machinery of death 
shattered, not all of the Undead reborn in the 
ruins of London were lucky enough to have a 
body. Flesh rotted away to muck, bones ground 
into powder, all earthly possessions lost and 
forgotten to time- these ancient or unfortunate 
Undead were reborn as Ghasts, formless spirits 
animated by strong emotion and unfinished ties 
to the world of the living. Mostly as a result of 
bitterness, to be honest. The lonely dead have 
had thousands of years to hone their grudges. 
They’ve got spite down to an artform. 
 
 
A Ghast appears as a restless swirl of 
light and sound. Imagine the glow of a gel light 
across a stage shrouded in smoke, mixed with 
the feedback of an untuned guitar and the buzz 
of the microphone stand. Every now and then, 
faces, limbs, even ageing clothes, form in the 
constant motion, reminders that the Ghast used 
to once be a person. Disembodiment drives 
nearly every Ghast completely insane. Their 
motivations range from cackling madness to icy 
cruelty, to petty jealousy and envy of the 
‘bodied’ dead. 
 
 
Ghasts are social pariahs, outcasts 
even in Undead society. Their anti-social 
tendencies, combined with their maddening 
appearance and disconnection from the 
physical world, make it all but impossible for 
them to integrate. Some try, forcing their swirling 
forms in animated construct shells to cling on 
desperately to a sense of purpose and shape. 
But most fail, driven into the Labyrinth by their 
own confusion and inability to reconcile with 
their abstract existence. Despite this, or 
perhaps because of it, Ghasts are deeply tragic 
figures, remnants of a world that has since been 
lost to time. 
 
 
Ghasts have an impressive propensity 
for Necromagic, able to channel its energies 
through their roiling bodies in a way the bodied 
Undead simply cannot match. Unfortunately, 
the more of this Necromagic they channel, the 
more the backlash consumes them- the 
Archdevils love their music to corrupt and 
mislead, and the pliable form of the Ghast lets it 
flow fast and freely. Most Ghasts are snuffed out 
like candles when at last their hold on the magic 
they channel slips suddenly and inexorably 
away from them. 
 


 
 
Demons- Servants of the Archdevils 
 
 
“Hey, baby- I was wondering when you’d 
call. Damn, these digs have got real smart since 
we last got together- is that a Les Paul classic I 
see there on the wall? Very smooth. Must have 
cost a fortune. Of course, that’s not a problem 
for you, any more… is it?” 
 
 
The demon grins, his teeth flashing 
painfully white in the glow of the fire. He’s 
wearing a navy blue three-piece, a cravat in 
scarlet at his throat. His seventeen eyes are 
hidden behind a dark pair of sunglasses, but she 
can still see the hungry flicker, the yellow slitted 
pupils, the lizard-like blink of their third eyelid. 
She shudders. He adjusts his cuffs. Traces his 
hooves around the edge of the circle. 
 
 
“Nice. Triple-layered. Some extra runes 
for reinforcement, a mitre stave- that’s a tricky 
one. Ooh, some Enochian! Did you know it was 
Enochian? Or did you just copy it out your big 
fucking book?” 
 
 
“Can it, Mulciber. You know what I 
want.” She sets her jaw. The grimoire whispers on 
the lectern before her, its pages alive with 
shadowy purple tendrils. She strikes a note on her 
guitar, a resonant thrum that rattles the walls. 
Mulciber stiffens. As if chains locked round his 
wrists and neck. His smile grows pained. 
 
 
“So. Who is it this time? Pretty rival, 
perhaps? Lucid Dream’s been doing well. They 
played the Hammer Smashed Smith Apollo last 
night- Hannah’s looking hot, and plays better 
than you. Is it her?” 
 
 
Angrily, she nods. She hates being 
predictable. Mulciber laughs softly. 
 
 
“Beautiful. Alive- or just breathing?” 
 
Demons are emanations of the 
Archdevils, the backing singers to their infernal 
soprano. They are the echoes of unwritten 
songs, the reverb from a half-finished riff, the 
last dying thought of a scream. They are 
bestowed with the powers of their patron 
Archdevil, in exchange for being shackled to 
them in both body and soul. Demons act as 
messengers, footsoldiers and dealmakers for 
their Infernal Choir- it’s not as if you can expect 
the Archdevils to do all the work themselves! 
Like angels, demons never truly die- the 
ephemeral matter of their soul is returned to 
their patron on their body’s destruction, where it 
is either consumed or reforged into a new, more 
useful form. 
 
 
Demons adopt all manner of guises 
when stalking the mortal world. When striking 
bargains, they appear beatific, if sinister- suited 
gentlemen with rosewood canes, women in red 
dresses with ivory hair, non-binary people with 
mysterious grey eyes and alabaster skin. When 
in battle, they once more enter their true forms, 
which are as hideous as they are potent. 
Bloated flies, wings made of buzzing amplifiers. 
Motor-driven machine devils, belching oil and 
smoke. Masses of flesh like living moshpits, 
bruised and bleeding from unhealing wounds. 
The more hideous, the better- though there is 
seldom a limit to a Demon’s creativity when it 
needs to terrify, inspire or deceive. 
 
 
Demons are capable of singing the 
original notes of the Infernal Choirs, rather than 
distilling their complex forms down to what 
mortals know as Necromagic. As such, they are 
powerful adversaries and not to be lightly fucked 
with. Many demons, at the behest of their 
Archdevils, adopt positions of power in human 
society. Record execs, sleazy producers, 
wealthy businessmen looking for their next 
exploitative score- they become ‘the man’ to 
give Necromusicians something to fight against. 
Of course, ‘the man’ always wins. Can’t let them 
forget who really holds the power. 
 
 
Necromusicians are capable, through 
rite and ritual, of binding a demon into service. 
The demons in question fucking resent this 
indignity, and will wilfully abuse their commands 
to do as much harm to their ‘masters’ as 
possible. Still, by taking this unnerving risk, 
Necromusicians are capable of reaching even 
greater heights of power- all the while knowing 
that the demons are just waiting for them to fall. 
 


 
 
A Layman’s Guide to the Archdevils 
 
Each Infernal Choir comprises four 
Orders of Archdevil, a hierarchy that seldom 
bends or breaks. Those Archdevils of a lower 
Order are magnitudes weaker than those above 
them, and are thus forced into subjugation and 
servitude. Those Archdevils of a higher Order 
are, by contrast, horrifyingly potent beings of 
pure Necromagic attuned to the Diabolus in 
Musica, the undercurrent of Infernal music that 
permeates creation. They rule over their Circles 
of Hell and the Archdevils and demons beneath 
them with an iron fist. 
 
Kings 
 
 
Kings are the mightiest of all the 
Archdevils, and were once Seraphim sworn into 
the service of God. They are embittered, cruel 
and callous creatures, desiring only power and 
dominion over all they survey. Perhaps once 
they were noble rebels, flying in the face of God, 
but now they are but greedy shadows, eager to 
plunder and profit from the wreckage of Her 
Heaven and enslave all denizens of the mortal 
world. 
 
 
Each King wields extraordinary power, 
second only to the greatest of the Angels and of 
God Herself. They are capable of reshaping 
creation to suit their whims, gifting mortals with 
Necromagical power and binding legions of 
demons to their service. All Kings have a true 
form they keep hidden from all but their most 
loyal subjects and most hated enemies. They 
are otherwise free to take whatever guise they 
wish, unshackled by the limits of corporeality. 
 
 
The Kings each dwell in the deepest 
corners of their respective Circles of Hell. Here, 
the veil between reality and Hell grows thin, and 
the landscape adopts a twisted, perverse 
reflection of its master’s own mind. Few ever 
venture to these Kingdoms, for the Kings are not 
beings to trifle with- most are quite insane, and 
have little patience for disloyalty and curiosity. 
 
 
 
 
Princes 
 
 
Princes are the most active of the 
Archdevils, messengers and consorts to their 
Kings and primary corruptors of mortal 
Necromusicians. They exist as scheming, 
conniving slaves to the will of their Kings, ever-
searching for opportunities to depose them. The 
Kings, as such, keep their Princes on a short 
leash, and are watchful for betrayal. A Prince is 
a being of considerable power, perhaps equal to 
a King but fettered by their bonds of service. 
 
 
Princes are commonly the patrons of 
powerful or influential Necromusicians they 
view as useful tools. The power they offer is 
intoxicating, but often dangerous. After all, the 
schemes of the Princes often work against the 
masters of the Infernal Choir they serve. This 
can result in some Necromusicians being 
caught up in devilish conspiracies that end up 
claiming their lives (and souls, to boot). 
 
Dukes 
 
 
Dukes are Archdevils of moderate 
power, not so stately as to rival Princes and 
Kings, but stately enough to keep the unwashed 
masses of demons below them at bay, as well 
as to fend off the greedy aspirations of the Earls. 
The majority of Necromusicians on the Skin City 
circuit will have a Duke as their patron, for they 
are the most eager among the Archdevils to 
prove their power and expand their dominion. 
 
Earls 
 
 
Earls are little more than jumped-up 
demons, reaching for status above their station. 
They exhibit considerable power beyond greater 
demons, but nothing close to the will and magic 
of their superiors. As such, they tend to be 
covetous, envious creatures, eager to promise 
much in exchange of any chance of promotion. 
 
 
 
 
 


 
 
 
The Kings of the Infernal Choirs 
 
Baal, He Who Reigns in Blood 
 
Baal is the King of the Angels of Death, and takes the form of a 
living whirlwind of blood, fire, shredded denim and leather. He is 
bound in chains of Hell Steel and screams with a thousand gnawing 
mouths from his throne of broken beer bottles, snapped guitar strings 
and shattered teeth. His form is in ceaseless motion, twisting and 
churning with frenetic energy, and his patience is legendarily short. To 
cross Baal is to be crushed into liquid, the swirling gory remains 
added to his form in a slick of organs and pasted skin. The only thing 
to rival Baal’s impatience is his anger; he has little to no control over 
sudden sparks of rage that erupt out of him like a burst blister, and has 
often sabotaged his own designs on Heaven as a result of a petty 
grudge or perceived slight. 
 
 
 
Paimon, The Swallower of Every Sun  
 
Paimon is the King of Apokalypsis, and takes the form of an 
imposing, insensate cloud of frozen darkness, its tenebrous body barely 
visible amidst the gloom. Paimon sucks the light and warmth from the 
world around it, enveloping all in a pall of chilling shadow that is split 
through with anxiety, depression and fear. It never seems to move or 
speak; words arrive unbidden in the mind, and its form is always with 
you wherever you turn, like a ghost at the corner of sight. Paimon’s 
motives are inscrutable. It acts in mysterious and contradictory ways, 
undoing its own servants as surely as it undoes those of the Choirs 
levied against it. Few Necromusicians dare to call Paimon their 
Master, for even to think of it is to invite despair and madness. 
 
 


 
 
 
Beleth, Singer of the Flaming Rhapsody (Corpse Crusaders) 
 
Beleth is the King of the Corpse Crusaders, and takes the form 
of an armoured knight atop a motorbike made of bones and the raw 
substance of heavy metal. Her armour shimmers with iridescent colour, 
oscillating in time with the thundering riffs screaming from the mouths 
upon her sword. Beneath her helmet, Beleth’s face is radiant and 
strangely human, albeit with a pair of curving horns escaping her brow. 
Her voice is beatific, full of richness and splendour, evoking images of 
chivalric romance and courtship- in battle, however, this façade soon 
falls apart. Beleth fights with a single-minded fury, so savage and 
unrelenting as to be almost bestial. Her form bursts into a rhapsody of 
fire, engulfed by rage that lashes out in burning tendrils at all who dare 
oppose her. 
 
 
 
 
 
Purson, She Upon the Darkest Throne 
 
Purson is the King of the Cult of the Frozen Moon, and revels 
in the spirit of the hunt. Her form is primal, metamorphic, almost 
protoplasmic- a seething, oily, furry and scaly mass of limbs, faces, 
wings, tails and teeth that seems at once every predator and none of 
them. Reindeer antlers cascade over wolf-life maws, which bleed into 
serpentine scales soaked in sweat, which shift into seven clawed feet 
shod in bloody feathers snatching at the air. It is debatable whether
