6 
 
Attacks 
- 
Demonic Claws (or Tentacles, or 
Horns, or Gribbly Bits): Introduce them 
to the sharp, or sticky, or crushing, or 
spiky, end of your demon’s appendages. 
4D6, Physical. This Attack may re-roll 
any D6 results of 1 (the Warlock must 
accept the roll of any re-rolled dice). 
 
Necromagic 
- 
Four Minor Necromagical Powers 
- 
Three Major Necromagical Powers 
- 
Two Metal Necromagical Powers 
 
Abilities 
- 
Infernal Choir: Demon-Possessed 
Warlocks willingly align themselves 
with an Infernal Choir when they accept 
a demonic binding. Choose one of the 
seven Infernal Choirs when using a 
Demon-Possessed Warlock(s) in your 
game. Their Necromagical Powers and 
appearance will reflect your choice of 
Choir, exactly like they do when creating 
a Necromusician PC. You get to decide 
what their Powers are, and what they 
do! 
 
- 
Jump in the Fire: Demons are always 
willing to fuel the unwary and the 
foolish. If a Demon-Possessed Warlock 
runs out of Necromagic, they can 
choose to immediately lose a point of 
Will or Wounds. If they do, they regain 
D3+2 points of Necromagic and go just 
a little bit more insane from demonic 
backlash and blood sacrifice. Y’know. 
As you do. 
 
- 
Unholy Form: Demon-Possessed 
Warlocks change shape to reflect the 
monstrosity that dwells within their 
flesh. Their bodies are thus inhumanly 
resilient, and difficult to put down. 
When Attacking a Demon-Possessed 
Warlock, a Necromusician cannot use 
Star Power to inflict extra Damage more 
than once per Attack. 
 
The ignorant often wonder why I do not 
fear the night. They speak of blasphemies 
beneath us, of fire and the void, and yet 
they do not see why I do not flinch at the 
flicker of shadows, dancing on the wall. 
Power, my friend. To those with power, 
nothing in this place carries fear. Come, 
let me show you. You too can embrace the 
power I have earned… 
 
Come. Be not afraid. You must join with 
them, my friend. Join with them, and share 
in their gifts. 
 
Yes. Yes, that’s right. Open yourself to 
the darkness. Drink of its sweet nectar. You 
shall be a vessel. A beautiful vessel, in 
which shall carry the power you have always 
sought. You shall be our vessel, and 
through us you shall wield- 
 
Ah. Such weakness. Your brain is leaking 
out of your ears. 


 
 
BCSI Crusader Ad Divintatum 
God’s madness flares like magma from the 
bowels of the Labyrinth. Sometimes, it bubbles 
within Her devotees, infecting them with zeal 
and blind conviction. These are the BCSI’s 
Paladins. Sometimes, though, it boils. A 
Crusader Ad Divinitatum is one touched by the 
very mind of God, a vessel of her ineffable will, 
imbued with the resonance of the Music of 
Creation. They stand over ten feet tall, sweeping 
white-feathered wings arching from their backs, 
lustrous golden hair shimmering in the glow of a 
halo set atop their brow. On the outside, they are 
incarnates of perfection, worshipped as God’s 
messengers by the clerics of the BCSI. Inwardly, 
however, they are completely and utterly insane. 
Driven mad by constant whispers, a Crusader 
Ad Divinitatum dons biomechanical armour and 
religious raiment, taking up flaming sword and 
holy crucifix to carry out acts of insanity, 
violence and cruelty in the name of God. 
 
Rank: Tyrant (4) 
 
Secondaries 
 
Armour 
Defence 
Necromagic 
Physical (3+) 
4+ 
0 
 
Speed 
Will 
Wounds 
Average 
N/A 
25 
 
Attacks 
- 
Flaming Sword: A massive two-handed 
Greatsword wreathed in divine fire. 8D6, 
Physical. This Attack inflicts +1 Damage 
for every result equal to or higher than 
the target’s Defence beyond the first. If 
this Attack inflicts Damage, it inflicts 1 
additional point of Magical Damage and 
sets its target on fire. 
 
- 
The Commandments of Insanity: The 
Crusader whispers words of madness, 
drawn from the mind of God Herself. 
6D6, Magical. This Attack targets ALL 
characters- friend or foe- capable of 
hearing the Crusader. Characters with 
the ‘God-Sent Avenger’ ability are 
immune to this Attack. 
 
- 
Shotgun Wings: With a flap, the 
Crusader’s wings burst into a flurry of 
shotgun barrels, discharging a violent 
salvo of buckshot. 6D6, Physical. A 
target damaged by this Attack is thrown 
backwards at least 2D6 metres and is 
knocked to the floor. If a target is 
damaged by this Attack, the Crusader 
Ad Divinitatum may immediately Move, 
even if they have already moved this 
Turn. 
 
Necromagic 
- 
None 
 
Abilities 
- 
God-Sent Avenger: Crusader Ad 
Divinitata hear the whispers of God 
Herself. This drives them utterly insane, 
but their minds are also completely 
inured to mental invasion. A Crusader 
Ad Divinitatum does not have a Will 
score, and can never lose or gain points 
of Will. In addition, a Crusader Ad 
Divnitatum can never have their mind 
influenced by illusions, brain control or 
other similar effects- they are just too 
fervent for such petty distractions to 
matter! 
 
- 
Music of the Spheres: A Crusader Ad 
Divinitatum is imbued by the very Music 
of Creation, and is utterly inured to 
Necromagic. Crusaders Ad Divinitatum 
can never be directly affected by 
Necromagic, nor any other form of 
demonic or devilish sorcery. They 
cannot lose Wounds as a result of 
Necromagical Attacks, or through the 
use of Black Magic Artefacts. 
 
- 
Symbol of the Realm: The BCSI view 
Crusaders as the ultimate expression of 
their faith and of their power. Members 
of the BSCI military, or of the 
Theological Church, cannot lose Will 
whilst a Crusader Ad Divinitatum is 
within their Line of Sight. They also gain 
Magical Armour (5+), if they do not have 
Magical Armour already, or improve 
their Magical Armour by +1 if they do 
(i.e. a 3+ Magical Armour would become 
a 2+ in the presence of a Crusader). 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 


 
 
Hell-Heads (Hard-as-Fucking-Nails) 
They’ve been called many things. Survivors. 
Action heroes. Final girls. Here, on the outskirts 
of Heaven, they are known as Hell-Heads: 
humans just too damn tough to roll over and die. 
A Hell-Head is a veteran of horror, violence and 
darkness, touched by the very worst the Pit and 
the Labyrinth have to offer and yet still standing 
on the other side. A Hell-Head could be a seven-
foot, muscle-bound war veteran covered in 
military tattoos, brandishing two shotguns in 
Hell-iron bracings; they could be a scar-faced 
woman, a haunted look in her eye and a smirk 
on her face, fashioning a crossbow out of demon 
bones; they could be a lithe non-binary athlete, 
all whip-fast limbs, quick thinking and 
resourcefulness, stocked to the brim with Black 
Magic Artefacts. One thing’s for sure- a Hell-
Head is damn hard to kill. 
 
Rank: Tyrant (1) 
 
Secondaries 
 
Armour 
Defence 
Necromagic 
Any One (3+), 
Any One (4+), 
Any One (5+) 
5+ 
0 
 
Speed 
Will 
Wounds 
Fast 
15 
12 
 
Attacks 
- 
Muscles, Knives, Chains and Heavy 
Objects for Bludgeoning: Hell-Heads 
don’t question the provenance of a 
weapon. The only thing that matters to 
them is how easy it’ll be to stave 
someone’s skull in with it. 5D6, 
Physical. A Hell-Head may re-roll any 
results of 1 when making this Attack. 
They must accept the re-rolled result. 
 
- 
Guns, Explosives, Throwing Stars and 
Other Assorted Munitions: Same as 
before- it doesn’t matter the flavour of 
firearm, as long as it shoots straight. 
6D6, Physical. Choose ONE when 
making this Attack: 1, this Attack inflicts 
+1 Damage for every result equal to or 
higher than the target’s Defence beyond 
the first, 2, this Attack affects all targets 
in close proximity to its main target, 3, 
this Attack affects all targets in a 15m 
cone in front of the Hell-Head, or 4, this 
Attack ignores Physical Armour. 
 
Necromagic 
- 
None 
 
Abilities 
- 
Hard as Hell: Hell-Heads are some of 
the toughest cookies this side of the 
Labyrinth. They survive not through 
demonic blessings, but through sheer 
force of will. Whenever a Hell-Head 
would lose a Wound, they may instead 
choose to lose a point of Will. In 
addition, when rolling Rank to see if a 
Hell-Head dies or falls under the PCs’ 
control, only roll a single D6. On a result 
of 2+, the Hell-Head keeps on fighting. 
They’re just too stubborn to die! 
 
- 
Hangers-On: Among the living, Hell-
Heads are celebrities, celebrated 
vanquishers of evil and survivors of 
terrible fates. They thus attract an 
unfortunate amount of groupies. A Hell-
Head is never without a gaggle of D6 
Hell-Fans, who use the Surface Dweller 
profile given earlier in this Chapter. 
However, they also gain Magical, Mental 
and Physical Armour at 2+ as long as 
their Hell-Head is still alive. Devoted, 
huh? 
 
- 
Black Magic Artefacts: Hell-Heads are 
known to collect and flaunt Black Magic 
Artefacts, either as souvenirs of their 
exploits or as some other sick joke. A 
Hell-Head will always have D3+1 Black 
Magic Artefacts on their person. You 
can use the list from the appropriate 
Chapter earlier in this book to generate 
some at random, or come up with your 
own! 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 


 
 
You see this, mate? Latest Hell Amp from the Fifth 
Circle. Adamantium coils, speaker with in-built Wailer 
Imp, mammoth bone knobs, even a blood jack for when you 
need that personal touch. Fucking mint, innit? 
 
Damn, Rich. What you pull to get hold of ‘er? Last I 
heard, your lot were struggling to play the King’s Head, 
much less the C-O2. 
 
 
Got a buddy who works salvage. Went down there with 
a member of Gigadeth the other day- properly dangerous 
girl, tooled up to the nines. Says she grabbed it out the 
forge, thinkin’ it was the 09-75X. Only an 08, as it 
happens. Lucky me, right? 
 
Hell yeah, you’re lucky! Say, Rich? 
 
 
What’s up, Paul? 
 
Mind grabbing me that screwdriver from up top shelf? No, 
that one there. Right the way up. That’s the ticket… 
Now, just hold still. This won’t take a moment… Ah, 
damn it, Rich. Why’d you have to bleed on my fav socks? 


 
 
The Dead 
Citizens of Skin City, Deathless Constructs, the Long-Deceased 
 
Dead-Head 
A Dead-Head is a catch-all term for a recently 
deceased, drunk and stupid heavy metal 
zombie. Commonly found clad in cheap leather 
and rusting metal studs, they sport a variety of 
singularly out-of-date band merch and an 
attitude that was better off left in the 1980s. 
Most are always spoiling for a fight and often get 
one- often at their own expense, as their rough 
braggadocio and overconfidence are the only 
things more powerful than their stench. Most 
don’t survive very long before someone puts 
them back in the ground. 
 
Rank: Thrall

Defence

- 
Bike Chain or Broken Bottle: A typical 
Dead-Head grabs the nearest sharp 
and/or heavy thing when threatened. 
Watch out for glass!. 1D6, Physical 
 
Abilities 
- 
Drunk and Stupid: Dead-Heads are 
almost always off their face on 
something. If a Dead-Head would lose a 
Wound, they may roll a D6. On a 5+, 
they’re too drunk to care- the Wound is 
not lost! 
 
 
“I told them, I did. I told them not to leave 
their tour van unlocked when they played the Rat 
House. Who’s fault is that, that all their gear got 
nicked? Them Dead-Heads, they see a chance and 
they take it. They’d pawn off the Cursed Skull of 
Albazzar if it got them beer money, for fuck’s 
sake! I told them. You know I told them…” 
Frankenroadie 
A Frankenroadie is a construct of dead flesh 
built out of roadies who sadly perished during 
one of their band’s more… explosive live shows. 
These lumbering, leather-clad giants are 
shocked into life with electrodes, copper wire 
and formaldehyde flasks, before being sent 
straight back to work on the tasks that got them 
killed in the first place. A Frankenroadie is 
strong, dependable and covered in stitches. As 
long as you don’t mind the smell, and keep them 
paid, fed and boozed, they’re hella useful. 
 
Rank: Thrall

drugs, booze and constant frustration. 
They react to pressure accordingly. 
When a Frankenroadie is reduced to 0 
Will, it enters a frenzied state- it starts 
wildly lashing out, attacking friend and 
foe alike! 


 
 
Thrasher 
Thrashers are knots of sinew, adrenaline and 
single-minded aggression. They were lucky 
enough that most of their muscles survived the 
atrophy of their First Death, and now pride 
themselves on the grey tissue that still clings like 
bowstring to their bones. Most Thrashers are 
thrill-seekers, adrenaline junkies hoping for their 
next big score. They often charge headlong into 
danger, heedless of the risks, craving whatever 
burst of excitement comes their way next.

grow to disturbing length, their edges

The Jitters: Thrashers move almost

with astonishing speed, leaping and

uses this ability to jump into close 
combat with an enemy, any Attack it 
makes with its Thrash Talons benefits 
from an Advantage. 
 
No death ‘til leather, 
We thrash until we die! 
No death ‘til leather, 
We murder the sunrise! 
- 
Edgy Thrasher War Chant 
Old Dusty Dandy (Old Duster) 
When the Pit was torn open, the machinery of 
death was forever shattered. Those long 
forgotten beneath the streets of London, the 
languishing dead of that once great city, 
emerged blinking into the light of the new world. 
Affectionately called ‘Old Dusty Dandies’, or ‘Old 
Dusters’ for short, these old ghouls often 
struggle to adapt to life in the modern day. Most 
go a little mad, trying to convince themselves 
that nothing ever changed, even as they take 
great pains to avoid the mod-cons of the city 
around them.

Old and Ignorant: Dandies would

that set in their ways! 
 
 
 
 
 


 
 
Aspiring Necromusician 
Skin City is awash with Aspiring 
Necromusicians- kids who think they’re hot shit, 
just because they’ve got an Archdevil at their 
back. Fucking posers. Still, though, they do 
wield some measure of necromagical power, 
and that shit makes them dangerous. This is how 
every Necromusician starts out, an instrument 
slung over their shoulder and a bad attitude 
stirring in their brain. Get rich, or die (again) 
trying. Un-life is for living, baby! 
 
Rank: Henchman

Ensorcelled Instrument: 
Necromusicians use a wide variety of 
ensorcelled magical instruments- a 
guitar that spits fire, drums that shatter 
bones with sonic booms, even harps 
that shred enemies in a shower of silver 
strings. 3D6, Physical OR Mental OR

- 
Infernal Choir: Aspiring 
Necromusicians are all aligned with an 
Infernal Choir. Choose one of the seven 
Infernal Choirs when using an Aspiring 
Necromusician(s) in your game. Their 
Necromagical Powers and appearance 
will reflect your choice of Choir, exactly 
like they do when creating a 
Necromusician PC. You get to decide 
what their Powers are, and what they 
do! 
 
- 
Deal with the Devil: Blood is power. 
Whenever an Aspiring Necromusician 
loses a Wound, they immediately gain 1 
point of Necromagic. This can take 
them above their starting value. 
Flesh-Cake 
Sometimes people forget that London fell a few 
thousand metres down when the Pit was torn 
open. Sometimes people forget that not 
everyone in the city was so lucky as to have their 
body left intact. Flesh-Cakes are what happened 
to tightly-packed crowds when London fell: 
squished-together amalgams of skin, bone and 
organs that were once six, seven, eight or more 
people that just had one unlucky day. Oozing 
through the Labyrinth, Flesh-Cakes are irascible 
and violent, devouring anything in their way. You 
would be too, if you had to share your body with 
a bunch of complete strangers! 
 
Rank: Henchman

exactly what it says on the tin. 4D6, 
Physical. This Attack inflicts +1 Damage

the target’s Defence beyond the first. 
 
- 
Acidic Bile: The Flesh-Cake vomits out 
a gout of corrosive acid and gall. 3D6, 
Physical. Also inflicts 1 Damage to 
anyone standing in close proximity to 
the target, if the target is damaged.

- 
None

- 
Amorphous Body: A Flesh-Cake is an 
oozing protoplasm of organs and skin. 
Flesh-Cakes can slither and slide 
through small gaps and change their 
shape, size and appearance at will. 
 
- 
Sticky Grasp: The grabbing hands of a 
Flesh-Cake are coated in sticky fluids. 
Anyone touched by a Flesh-Cake must 
make a Guts Test. If they fail, they 
cannot Move during the next Round of 
Combat. 
 


 
 
One-Man Band 
A ‘One-Man Band’ is an euphemistic name for a 
group of unfortunate Necromusicians who have 
all been stitched together. Their original 
personalities have fused into one, a drooling, 
drug-addicted and gnarly rockstar with the 
unnerving strength of a drummer, the precision 
of a lead guitarist, the flair of the keys and the 
arrogance of the singer. One-Man Bands are 
often created by deranged Necrosurgeons when 
provided with corpses that have experienced 
Second Death- it makes it easier to reanimate if 
no soul is left inside! Besides, the muscle 
memory’s there. All they need is some 
formaldehyde, and they’re back in action! 
 
Rank: Henchman

Instruments: All quantity- not so much 
quality. Just keep hitting with whatever 
comes to hand. 5D6, Physical. The 
target of this Attack improves their 
Defence by 1 against it (to a maximum 
of 6+). 
 
Necromagic 
- 
Four Minor Necromagical Powers 
 
Abilities 
- 
The Show Must Go On: One-Man 
Bands have something of the 
Necromagical left in them. 
Unfortunately, they’re just not very good 
at controlling it! In addition to its Move 
and Action on each of its Turns, a One-
Man Band can use a random 
Necromagical Power for free on a D6 
roll of 4+. If successful, randomise 
which of its four Minor Necromagical 
Powers it uses by rolling a D6 and re-
rolling results of 5 or 6. A One-Man 
Band CANNOT use Necromagical 
Powers in any other circumstances! 
 
 
Chain Ghast 
A Chain Ghast is what happens when your 
guitarist lets their significant other manage your 
band. They boss you around, you grow to hate 
them, you murder them out of spite, then they 
come back and make your life even worse. A 
Chain Ghast is a vengeful spirit of a wronged 
partner- but just because they were wronged 
DOESN’T mean they’re not a pain in the ass. 
Chain Ghasts are petty and bitter. Imagine the 
douchiest jock in the locker room, the arrogant 
sales executive who thinks he’s hot shit, the 
preppy, bitchy girls that chat shit behind 
people’s backs. Now imagine them, but lurid 
green ghosts, shackled in wraps of barbed wire. 
 
Rank: Henchman

- 
Razor-Wire Chains: The Chain Ghast 
lashes with its barbed wire whips. 2D6, 
Physical. If this Attack inflicts Damage, 
the target also loses 1 point of Will. 
 
- 
Shriek of Jealousy: The Chain Ghast 
wails against the injustice of its death. 
3D6, Mental. 
 
Abilities 
- 
Ball and Chain: A Chain Ghast is 
always bound to the world of the un-
living by a spirit bond. A Chain Ghast 
cannot lose Will or Wounds, and is 
unaffected by mundane Attacks. The 
only known way to banish a Chain 
Ghast is through Necromagic, or by 
destroying the spirit bond they have 
with the corporeal world. A spirit bond 
is an undead or living vessel to whom 
the Chain Ghast had a strong emotional 
tie. If they are killed or exorcised, the 
Chain Ghast evaporates. 
 
- 
Incorporeal: Chain Ghasts are ghostly, 
spectral entities that represent the last 
spiteful remnants of the people they 
once were. A Chain Ghast can move 
through solid objects. 


 
 
Spirit of the Damned 
When Second Death comes for an undead, the 
Archdevils collect on what is due. They utterly 
consume the mind and essence of their former 
slave, hungrily devouring every last iota of what 
once made them human, sustaining themselves 
on the rich meat of mortal suffering. But, on rare 
occasions, the malice and hatred of a victim of 
Second Death sustains them. They claw their 
way out of the Archdevil’s maw, the last shred of 
self they clung onto all that remains of who they 
were. These spectral beings, incorporeal Spirits 
of the Damned, are embittered and callous 
rockstars from ages past, long since consigned 
to the bin of history. Driven by jealously, they 
hunt down younger Necromusicians out of spite, 
all the while desperate to escape the cheated 
Archdevil in hot pursuit of their soul. 
 
Rank: Elite (3) 
 
Secondaries 
 
Armour 
Defence 
Necromagic 
 Magical (3+) 
6+ 
6 
 
Speed 
Will 
Wounds 
Average 
9 
5 
 
Attacks 
- 
(Un) Life Drain: The Spirit of the 
Damned subsumes the essence of a 
nearby victim. 3D6, Magical. If this 
Attack damages its target, the Spirit of 
the Damned regains 1 spent point of 
Necromagic for every point of damage it 
inflicted. 
 
Necromagic 
- 
Curse of Spite (Minor): The Spirit jinxes 
a number of nearby victims, turning 
good fortune into sour grapes. Up to D3 
targets that the Spirit can see are 
affected by this curse. Until the end of 
their next Turn, all dice rolls of 6 they 
make count as dice rolls of 1. 
 
- 
Curse of Jealousy (Minor): The Spirit 
forces open the jealously and envy 
latent in the human psyche. One target 
of the Spirit’s choice must spend their 
next Turn trying to harm the ally they’re 
the most jealous and/or envious of. The 
exact nature of how they try to harm this 
ally is up to them, but it should be 
needlessly petty and spiteful. Be 
creative! 
 
- 
Curse of Regret (Minor): The Spirit 
afflicts its victim with a karmic binding, 
turning its acts against itself. One target 
of the Spirit’s choice is bound to 
another target for the next D6 Rounds. 
Each time one target suffers damage of 
any kind, the other target suffers the 
exact same damage! 
 
- 
Shriek of Horror (Major): A terrifying, 
cacophonous howl splits the air 
asunder. All enemies that can hear the 
Spirit must immediately flee in terror 
and panic- any that cannot flee, for 
whatever reason, are instead frozen to 
the spot, unable to move or tear their 
eyes away! 
 
- 
Hell Frost (Major): Spears of frigid ice 
surround the Spirit and lance towards 
its foes. The Spirit makes D6 Attacks. 
Each Attack is 5D6 and Magical. 
 
- 
Thorns of Crimson Death (Metal): 
Bloody thorns erupt from the ground, 
ensnaring and lacerating all they can 
catch. All creatures standing on the 
ground within a moderate radius of the 
Spirit must make a Guts Test with a 
Disadvantage. If they fail, they lose 
D3+1 Wounds and cannot Move on their 
next Turn. 
 
Abilities 
- 
Incorporeal: Spirits of the Damned are 
ghostly, spectral entities that represent 
the last spiteful remnants of the people 
they once were. A Spirit of the Damned 
can move through solid objects. 
 
- 
Unending Spite: Spirits of the Damned 
are uniquely envious of all (un)living 
beings. A Spirit of the Damned will 
NEVER cooperate with or serve under 
any demonic entities. In addition, they 
will not cooperate with any undead 
unless they are explicitly the ones in a 
position of power. Such is the price of 
their jealousy and hubris! 
 
 
 
 


 
 
Necro-Colossus 
A Necro-Colossus is an enormous construct 
built out of dead flesh and heavy metal stage 
gear- pyro, animatronics, amplifier stacks, you 
name it. Animated by coruscating electricity and 
infernal magic, Necro-Colossi are bound to 
wander the Circles of the Labyrinth or the 
streets of Skin City, an ever-present reminder of 
the dangers of defying the rich and powerful. 
Mindless, and loyal to a fault, there is little one 
can do to reason with a Necro-Colossus if it 
even understands your words at all. An 
insensate mountain of corpse matter, gel lights, 
exploding bomb pots and ridiculous stage props 
(Viking longship made out of titanium, anyone?), 
they are amongst the most dangerous undead in 
the Pit. 
 
Rank: Elite (6) 
 
Secondaries

Defence

 Mental (2+),

2+ 
0 
 
Speed

Wounds

Attacks 
- 
Massive Biomechanical Limb: The 
Necro-Colossus smashes its foes to 
smithereens with a huge bludgeoning 
fist (or foot). 6D6, Physical. This Attack 
inflicts +1 Damage for every result 
equal to or higher than the target’s 
Defence beyond the first. 
 
- 
Hurl Corpses: The Necro-Colossus 
breaks away chunks of its own 
amalgamated body to throw as necrotic 
missiles. 4D6, Physical.  
 
Necromagic 
- 
None 
 
Abilities 
- 
Feed on Death: A Necro-Colossus can 
rebuild itself by assimilating nearby 
corpses. Whenever a Living or Undead 
creature dies within close proximity to a 
Necro-Colossus, it immediately 
consumes their body and recovers 1 
lost Wound. 
 
- 
Inhumanly Resilient: Killing a Necro-
Colossus is nothing short of a 
superhuman feat, given their insane 
durability. A Necro-Colossus has 
double the usual Rank number for an 
Elite NPC. This represents how 
frustratingly difficult it is to keep one of 
the bastards down! 
 
“Alright, get that lighting rig into place! And 
make sure the colossus stands up straight this 
time. It hunched over so bad on the last show the 
spots only illuminated the singer’s arse. Get to 
it!” 
 
The stage manager storms off into the back of 
the arena, shouting something about a coffee and 
a lack of back-up dancers. The roadies look up, 
and gulp, as the multi-faceted disco-ball eyes of 
the Necro-Colossus gaze insensately down at 
them. Its seething body is covered in gel lights, 
pyro and enormous amplifier stacks, buzzing with 
latent electricity. The lead roadie sighs. He clips 
on a climbing rope and carabiner. 
 
“OK, lads. You heard the boss. We need the 
spots up top, and a support rod up its back. I can 
see the spine slumping. You- yes, you, you 
greasy little fucker. Give me a hand with this 
rope, yeah?” 
 
The roadies start to clamber up the colossus; 
if it cares, it doesn’t show it. It just watches in 
bemusement as they lash the stage gear to its 
scabrous hide, pinning it in place with rusting 
metal hooks and cable ties, with leather straps 
and bolts as thick as a man’s arm. The stage 
manager returns. He looks up. Clicks his tongue 
in annoyance. 
 
“Get the thing a big old top hat- and a cane! 
Yes, a cane. The band wants it to fit in. Shock 
rockers, I swear… They’ll want a boa 
constrictor on it next…” 
 


 
 
World-Famous Necromusician 
Those who thrive in the service of the Archdevils 
become World-Famous Necromusicians of 
terrifying power. Wreathed in smoke and searing 
electricity, dancing from their guitars, these 
Necromusicians wield the most potent of 
Necromagical Powers gifted to them by their 
infernal patrons, as well as a variety of artefacts 
dredged from the depths of the Labyrinth. 
They’re also really rich. Y’know, so rich they can 
afford to buy, like, guns and shit. Fucking with a 
World-Famous Necromusician can also have 
another complication: their fans and hangers-
on. No use trying to take someone’s crown if 
you’re drowned in screaming Dead-Heads the 
moment you get a hand to it. Then again, for a 
World-Famous Necromusician, every day is a 
challenge. The industry is cutthroat (literally) and 
staying on top requires a lot of blood. No, not 
sweat and tears- just blood. They’re undead, for 
fuck’s sake. 
 
Rank: Tyrant (6)

Necromagical Instrument can cause 
some serious damage. 5D6, Magical, 
Mental OR Physical. This Attack inflicts

Necromagic

Five Minor Necromagical Powers

Abilities 
- 
Infernal Choir: All World-Famous 
Necromusicians owe their powers to 
one of the Archdevils, likely a King or a 
Prince. Choose one of the seven 
Infernal Choirs when using a World-
Famous Necromusician(s) in your 
game. Their Necromagical Powers and 
appearance will reflect your choice of 
Choir, exactly like they do when creating 
a Necromusician PC. You get to decide 
what their Powers are, and what they 
do! 
 
- 
Adoring Fans: A World-Famous 
Necromusician is never without their 
adoring- and fucking annoying- fanbase. 
As an Action, a World-Famous 
Necromusician can call upon their 
legions of adoring fans. This either 
summons D3 Elite Rank Undead, D6 
Henchman Rank Undead or 2D6 Thrall 
Rank Undead- exactly who they are and 
how they get here is up to the GM! 
These fans serve the will of the World-
Famous Necromusician 
unquestioningly- if the Necromusician 
perishes, they immediately turn tail and 
flee, or simply crumble to dust. 
 
- 
Deal with the Devil: A World-Famous 
Necromusician is perhaps more 
terrified of Second Death than any 
other. As such, they’ll make all manner 
of bargains to keep themselves alive- no 
matter the cost. The first time during a 
Combat that a World-Famous 
Necromusician is reduced to 0 Will 
and/or 0 Wounds, instead of rolling 
Rank, they immediately reset their Will 
and/or Wounds to 6 and recover all 
spent points of Necromagic. However, 
their Defence is also reduced to 3+, and 
all of their Armour is reduced to 5+ for 
the remainder of the Combat. 
 
- 
Unbridled Charisma: So personable, 
charismatic and sexy is a World-
Famous Necromusician that any 
attempts to charm or manipulate them 
can often seriously backfire. When 
targeted by an Attack attempting to 
reduce their Will, a Necromusician can 
use their Unbridled Charisma. If they do 
so, and the Attack fails to inflict any 
Damage, the Attacker immediately 
loses D3 points of Will. 
 
 
 


 
 
Amp-Pyre 
Not all of those buried in the ruins of Old London 
rise again as mouldering Dandies. There are 
those that were bound to the Archdevils even 
before Heaven’s fall. They are the Amp-Pyres, 
and in them stirs a restless hunger for the music 
of creation. An Amp-Pyre feeds on their victim’s 
musical talent, sucking out their connection to 
Necromagic and the spirit of rock ‘n’ roll. They 
are ethereal, beautiful, ageless beings of 
prodigious strength, and, suffused with music 
drawn from centuries of vampirism, are among 
the greatest metal musicians who ever lived. 
Just… never trust one as far as you can throw 
‘em. Which, given their ferocity, is not very far at 
all. 
 
Rank: Tyrant (7) 
 
Secondaries

 Magical (4+)

Attacks 
- 
Razor-Sharp Talons: Life on the 
bleeding edge, huh? 5D6, Physical. For 
every point of Damage this Attack 
inflicts, the Amp-Pyre regains one point 
of Necromagic, up to a maximum of 12. 
 
- 
Feeding Fangs: The Amp-Pyre sinks 
their teeth into the throat of their victim- 
but drinks no blood. Instead, they drain 
the very music from their victim’s soul. 
6D6, Mental. This Attack inflicts +1 
Damage for every result equal to or 
higher than the target’s Defence beyond 
the first. Instead of inflicting Damage to 
Will, an Amp-Pyre can choose to reduce 
their victim’s Necromagic by the same 
amount and regain that many points of 
Necromagic, up to a maximum of 12. 
 
Necromagic 
- 
Transfix (Minor): The Amp-Pyre stares 
lovingly into their victim’s eyes, 
paralyzing them and sapping their will to 
fight. The target is beguiled by the Amp-
Pyre. This may result in temporary 
paralysis, unwanted attraction or even 
just semi-lucid drooling until the spell 
breaks. 
 
- 
Shapechange (Major): The Amp-Pyre’s 
form unravels and shifts into a… well, a 
bat is a bit fucking cliché, but it’s an 
option. The Amp-Pyre can transform 
into any animal, creature or person 
they’ve previously encountered. The 
illusion is convincing, enough to 
deceive an imperceptive onlooker. 
 
- 
Corpse Explosion (Major): Point at a 
dead body- make it blow up. Bone 
shards included. The Amp-Pyre 
detonates a number of nearby corpses 
like frag grenades full of blood, bones 
and pus. Anyone unfortunate enough to 
be in the splash zone risks being 
shredded to pieces. 
 
- 
Bloodrocuted (Metal): The Amp-Pyre 
uses the spilt blood of its foes as an 
electric outlet, sizzling their veins with a 
discharge of static. Anyone bleeding 
within a short distance of the Amp-Pyre 
is immediately subjected to an 
extremely violent internal electric 
shock, the equivalent of being hooked 
up to an overhead power line. 
 
Abilities 
- 
Vampirism is Sexy: Amp-Pyres enjoy 
the same sexual mythologisation as 
other creatures of the night throughout 
history- for better or worse. Amp-Pyres 
reduce their target’s Will by an 
additional point any time they 
successfully attempt to seduce, beguile 
or persuade them. 
 
- 
Seven Churches: Strangely, Amp-Pyres 
aren’t subject to the usual weaknesses 
of vampires in the old stories. Instead, 
they have a near-fatal allergy to hymns. 
Go figure. For each Round an Amp-Pyre 
is exposed to a hymn, either sung by a 
chorister or played on a church organ, 
they must roll two separate D6s, one for 
Will and one for Wounds. On a result of 
4+, they immediately lose D3 points of 
the corresponding Secondary as the 
hymn blisters and burns their skin. 
 


 
 
The Demonic 
Slaves to Darkness, Choristers Infernal, Shadows on the Edge 
 
Lesser Devil 
Lesser Devils take a cavalcade of forms, from 
hideous amalgams of animal parts to 
biomechanical spectres of gears and living 
shadow. They are the weakest of the Infernal 
Choirs, bound without exception to the will of 
their masters. Lesser Devils perennially scheme 
and plot to overthrow those higher than them in 
the hierarchy, but rarely succeed. They are 
capricious, prideful and spiteful creatures- not 
to be trusted as far as they can be thrown.

line of sight instead of expending a 
point of Necromagic. 
Cambion Spawn 
Devils are deceivers one and all; those mortals 
ensnared by them in depraved unions give birth 
to Cambion Spawn, fusions of infernal blood 
and the human body. Cambion Spawn roil with 
barely contained vitriol and shudder with 
madness. Their very forms rebel against them, 
the devilish essence within tearing at their flesh 
to force itself free. A Cambion Spawn thus 
rapidly mutates, dies and regenerates at a 
horrifying rate, trapped in a cycle of torment 
from which there is no escape.

flows like an ooze. It can force its way 
through small gaps, such as cracks 
under doors, and expand to fill larger 
spaces. 
 


 
 
Skin Shucker 
Demons often walk among the living and the 
dead, seeking to corrupt and ensnare the 
isolated, the foolhardy and the vulnerable. Skin 
Shuckers are demonic opportunists, wretched 
and lesser within their choirs but still more than 
capable of preying on the unwary. Skin Shuckers 
fashion life-like guises out of severed flesh and 
skin, harvested from their victims and the 
recently deceased. They inveigle their way into 
the lives of their targets, feeding on their 
emotions; their influence is scheming and 
malignant, extracting every last drop of 
sentiment before finally consuming their victim.

and skin, imbued with illusory 
Necromagic. To all intents and 
purposes, a Skin-Shucker looks like an 
ordinary human being. To see through 
the illusion requires a successful Brains 
Test, which can only be made if the 
onlooker has reasonable doubts about 
the identity of the Skin-Shucker to begin 
with. Of course, any Necromagic that 
can detect demonic entities will unveil 
a Skin-Shucker’s presence and expose 
their (revolting) true form.  
Demonic Legionary 
Hell is at war with itself. Countless demons are 
slaughtered to further the goals of the 
Archdevils, marched into crucibles of ceaseless 
conflict that soak the soil of the Labyrinth in foul 
black ichor. Demonic Legionaries are the 
footsoldiers of this war; they come in countless 
shapes and sizes, hideous forms that are dark 
reflections of their masters’ infernal will. Some 
are morbid, necrotising creatures; some are 
amalgams of animals, flesh and bone; some are 
dark swirls of energy or living liquid.  
 
Rank: Thrall 
 
Secondaries 
 
Armour 
Defence 
Necromagic 
Magical (6+), 
Physical (5+) 
4+ 
0 
 
Speed 
Will 
Wounds 
Average 
2 
3 
 
Attacks 
- 
Hellforged Weapons: Whether 
classical armaments such as spears 
and shields, or hellish firearms wrought 
of black metal, a Hellforged Weapon is 
made for the sole purpose of killing. 
2D6, Physical OR Magical. 
 
Abilities 
- 
Infernal Choir: All Demonic Legionaries 
owe their allegiance to one of the 
Infernal Choirs. Choose one of the 
seven Infernal Choirs when using a 
Demonic Legionary(s) in your game. A 
Demonic Legionary gains one of the 
following benefits based on this choice: 
 
- 
Angels of Death: The Legionary’s 
Speed increases to Fast. 
- 
Apokalypsis: The Legionary’s Magical 
Armour increases to 5+. 
- 
Corpse Crusaders: The Legionary rolls 
one additional D6 with its Hellforged 
Weapons. 
- 
Cult of the Frozen Moon: The 
Legionary increases its Defence to 5+. 
- 
Legion of the Night: The Legionary 
gains +1 Will. 
- 
Engine of Destruction: The Legionary ‘s 
Physical Armour increases to 4+. 
- 
The Way of All Flesh: The Legionary 
gains +1 Wound. 


 
 
Biomechanical Drummer-Devil 
Imagine a man, but many-limbed and shod in 
glistening wet chitin, like the shell of an insect. 
Imagine grotesque nodules of white muscle 
between fractures in the shell. Imagine knots of 
fat black cabling, strings under tension like 
those on a bass, drumskins across open mouths 
anchored in place with ugly, rusting hooks. 
Imagine an infernal toccata, a thunder of snares, 
a constant, ceaseless buzzing as brushes and 
sticks clatter over bruised machines. That is a 
Biomechanical Drummer-Devil, one of the 
Infernal choristers. You’re welcome. 
 
Rank: Henchman 
 
Secondaries 
 
Armour 
Defence 
Necromagic 
Magical (6+), 
Mental (3+) 
2+ 
0 
 
Speed 
Will 
Wounds 
Slow 
4 
8 
 
Attacks 
- 
Thunder of Drumsticks: The Drummer-
Devil rains down a cacophony of blows 
with its many drumsticks. 3D6, 
Physical. This Attack can be made 
against all enemies within the 
Drummer-Devil’s reach, or against a 
single enemy target. If only a single 
target is chosen, this Attack rolls 4D6 
instead of 3D6, and knocks any 
Damaged target to the ground. 
 
Abilities 
- 
Not Quite My Tempo: A Drummer-
Devil’s constant beat can accelerate or 
decelerate those that listen to it. At the 
start of each of its Turns, a Drummer-
Devil can play a rapid or slow drum 
beat. If it plays a rapid beat, all of its 
allies that can hear it change their 
Speed to Fast- if their Speed was 
already Fast, they now may act before 
all the PCs, regardless of their Speed! If 
it plays a slow beat, all of its allies that 
can hear it change their Speed to Slow- 
but they all immediately regain 1 lost 
point of Will or 1 lost Wound! 
 
 
 
Infernal Rock Star 
A goat-horned man with ivory skin stands on a 
stage made out of the bones of the dead. His 
guitar bleeds from its seven livid eyes; his fingers 
clutch a pick made out of obsidian, and the 
strings he plucks roar with heat and fury. His 
stare burns like embers, sparking in a grate, and 
when he speaks it is in octaves, a wash of 
musical notes, at once atonal and yet strangely 
beautiful, in a way. Infernal Rock Stars are 
demons aping Necromusicians, vainly hoping 
for stardom or recognition. Sad? Maybe. Metal 
as fuck? Hells, yeah. 
 
Rank: Henchman 
 
Secondaries 
 
Armour 
Defence 
Necromagic 
Magical (6+) 
4+ 
5

Wounds

4 
4 
 
Attacks 
- 
Blazing Symphony: An Infernal Rock 
Star speaks in heavy metal. Heavy 
metal that can be set on very real fire. 
3D6, Magical. A target Damaged by this 
Attack is set on fire, with all the usual 
consequences for being set on fire. It’s 
not fun. 
 
Necromagic 
- 
Two Minor Necromagical Powers 
- 
Two Major Necromagical Powers 
 
Abilities 
- 
Infernal Choir: All Infernal Rock Stars 
owe their allegiance to one of the 
Infernal Choirs. Choose one of the 
seven Infernal Choirs when using an 
Infernal Rock Star(s) in your game. Their 
Necromagical Powers and appearance 
will reflect your choice of Choir, exactly 
like they do when creating a 
Necromusician. You get to decide what 
their Powers are, and what they do! 
 
- 
Demonic Call: Infernal Rock Stars can 
bind lesser devils to their service. As an 
Action, an Infernal Rock Star can 
summon one reluctant Lesser Devil to 
serve them. They may only have up to 
three Lesser Devils summoned at a 
time. 


 
 
Chorister of the Damned 
A ball of livid red gore covered in mouths, all of 
which sing in a thousand languages the 
melodies of the Infernal Choirs. A Chorister of 
the Damned is legion, a collective of demonic 
entities bound together in a perverse cyst of 
music made flesh. Its words are at once 
cacophonous and harmonious, discordant and 
serene, and the world around it shakes and 
warps as if rocked by the waves of the sea. 
 
Rank: Henchman 
 
Secondaries

Gnashing Maws: A Chorister of the 
Damned savages its foes with hideous 
peg-like teeth. 2D6, Physical 
 
Abilities 
- 
Symphonies of Sickness: Choristers of 
the Damned are always singing, and 
never stop. To listen to their words is to 
invite madness and horror. At the start 
of each of its Turns, a Chorister may 
choose to sing one of the following 
symphonies. Its effects last until the 
start of its next Turn. 
 
o Symphony of Pain: Each time 
an enemy of the Chorister 
loses a Wound, roll a D6. On a 
5+, they lose an additional 
Wound. 
 
o Symphony of Madness: Each 
time an enemy of the Chorister 
loses a point of Will, roll a D6. 
On a 5+, they lose an additional 
point of Will. 
 
o Symphony of Rebirth: Roll a 
D6 at the start of each of the 
Turns of the Chorister’s allies. 
On a 5+, they immediately 
regain 1 lost Wound or 1 lost 
point of Will. 
Beast-Blooded Horror 
Not all demons are slaves to the Infernal Choirs. 
Some, driven mad by their exile and mutated 
beyond recognition and reason, dwell on the 
fringes of Hell as Beast-Blooded Horrors, 
slavering, animalistic horrors who have utterly 
lost their minds. Beast-Blooded Horrors take 
many forms, all of which are hideous fusions of 
insect, mammal, reptile, bird, fish, amphibian 
and sometimes even human. They hunger for 
meat, consuming the flesh of the dead even as 
their bodies reject mortal sustenance, causing 
them to vomit blood and bone fragments, 
shuddering as they tear themselves apart from 
within. 
 
Rank: Henchman 
 
Secondaries

Bestial Strike: Whether with claws, 
hooves or fangs, the Horror rips into its 
prey. 3D6, Physical. If the target suffers 
Damage, the Horror may immediately 
make a Move, taking the target with it! 
 
Abilities 
- 
The Beast Within: Beast-Blooded 
Horrors are unpredictable and savage. 
When reduced to 0 Will, instead of 
falling under the PC’s control, a Beast-
Blooded Horror rolls a D6 and consults 
the following table: 
o 
1-2: Berserk. The Horror enters 
a frenzy! On its Turns, it will 
lunge at and attack the nearest 
target- friend or foe! 
 
o 
3-4: Cower. The Horror flees, 
searching for safety. On its 
Turns, it will run and try to hide, 
recovering 1 point of Will if it 
spends at least 1 Turn out of 
any enemy’s line of sight. 
 
o 
5-6: Broken Will. The Horror 
falls under the PC’s control, as 
normal. 


 
 
Octane Fiend 
An Octane Fiend is part greater demon and part 
disassembled jet engine. It resembles a 
bleeding profusion of amalgamated skinless 
flesh and firing cylinders, turbo chargers and fan 
belts growing out of it and leaking smoke and oil. 
An Octane Fiend’s fists, feet and face are 
masses of interconnected rusting gears, 
spinning with a razor-edged screech as they 
grind and scrape against one another. Despite 
their size, Octane Fiends are deceptively fast, 
moving in horrifying shudders of gore and petrol 
as the engines within them spark. To escape is a 
challenge; those that fail are ground to paste 
within them, consumed and broken down into 
fuel to feed the Octane Fiend’s turbines. 
 
Rank: Elite (4) 
 
Secondaries 
 
Armour 
Defence 
Necromagic 
Magical (6+), 
Physical (5+) 
4+ 
4 
 
Speed 
Will 
Wounds 
Fast 
6 
9 
 
Attacks 
- 
Mass of Grinding Gears: An Octane 
Fiend attacks with a whirl of razor-
edged gears, grinding together like 
teeth. 5D6, Physical. This Attack can be 
made once against every eligible target 
in close proximity to the Octane Fiend. 
 
Necromagic 
- 
Hit the Nitrous (Minor): The Octane 
Fiend’s body surges with nitrous oxide 
infused with infernal magic. The Octane 
Fiend may take two Actions this Turn 
instead of one. The Octane Fiend can 
use this Necromagic on its Turn without 
expending its Action. 
 
- 
Overheat (Major): Boiling blood and 
petrol spill from the Octane Fiend’s 
engine pipes. ALL targets within a small 
radius around the Octane Fiend are 
drenched in boiling fluids that may 
scald, scorch or even set them on fire. 
 
Abilities 
- 
Frenzied Charge: An Octane Fiend on 
the move is not something any sensible 
person would stand in the way of. 
Octane Fiends can always barge 
through Necromusicians and NPCs 
whilst moving, as long as they’re 
smaller than the Octane Fiend. When it 
does so, anyone barged through in this 
way must roll a Guts Test or suffer 1 
Damage. 
 
 
Watch Out, Watch Out… 
 
Yes, sir. You, in the fancy coat. I’m 
talking to you, you rich sonuva bitch. 
I’m warning you. 
 
There are monsters, down there, in 
the dark. 
 
Oh, you think you’re clever? Mate, 
you ain’t even dead. You haven’t felt 
it. What it’s like to feel your body 
break, the blood on your hands, a 
reminder you’re just another man. 
 
It's a hell of a thing, to realise just 
how vulnerable you really are. 
 
Oh, you think I’m delusional? Just 
another crazy old man? Fuck you, you 
pompous prick. What do you know, 
about what’s down there? Nothing! 
 
Go on, then. Off with you. 
 
The devils will take another, and you’ll 
learn to fear what’s beneath you. 
 


 
 
Archon of Anxieties 
Doubts, fear and depression given form, an 
Archon of Anxieties is the personification of the 
worst excesses of the human mind. Its true form 
is a cloud of freezing black vapour; however, it 
appears differently to all who behold it, preying 
on the darkest corners of the psyche. Some may 
see it as an agglomeration of all the people that 
never believed in them; some may see it as a 
grim reminder of their own mortality; some may 
see it as all the hatred, bile and denial they’ve 
faced for simply being who they are. An Archon 
of Anxieties feeds on the despair it provokes, 
worming its way inside you and hollowing you 
out, taking all the joy and carefree pleasures of 
innocence until nothing is left but cynicism, 
nihilism and regrets. It is amongst the most 
insidious of the demonic threats within the 
Labyrinth. 
 
Rank: Elite (3) 
 
Secondaries 
 
Armour 
Defence 
Necromagic 
None 
5+ 
12 
 
Speed 
Will 
Wounds

10 
N/A 
 
Attacks 
- 
Self-Loathing: The Archon of Anxiety 
evokes feelings of self-destruction and 
doubt. 4D6, Mental. This Attack inflicts 
+1 Damage for every result equal to or 
higher than the target’s Defence beyond 
the first. 
 
- 
Touch of Madness: The Archon reaches 
out and infects its victims with a sudden 
surge of insanity. 6D6, Mental. This 
Attack can only be used once per 
Combat. If this Attack inflicts any 
Damage, the target’s next Turn is 
controlled by the Archon of Anxiety. 
 
Necromagic 
- 
Horrifying Delusions (Minor): The 
Archon conjures up miasmic 
phantasmagoria and broken images to 
confuse and delude its victims. The 
Archon creates a series of vivid illusions 
designed to confuse, mislead and 
misdirect. They should be disturbing, 
cold and laced with dread. 
 
- 
Nihilism (Major): The Archon feasts on 
the will to live of those that stand before 
it. All enemies that can see the Archon 
must roll a number of D6 equal to their 
current Will. For every result of 5+, they 
immediately lose a point of Will. 
 
- 
Naked and Alone (Metal): The Archon 
imprisons its victim inside their own 
mind, swallowing them up in the 
darkness of their psyche. The target is 
trapped within the darkest corners of 
their mind, and must escape their own 
inner demons to once more return to 
the real world. 
 
Abilities 
- 
Preys Upon Your Fears: An Archon of 
Anxiety consumes the fear and doubt of 
those it can break the will of. Whenever 
an enemy is reduced to 0 Will in Line of 
Sight of an Archon of Anxiety, the 
Archon itself immediately gains D3 Will. 
This CAN take it above its starting Will of 
10. 
 
- 
Formless Dread: An Archon of Anxiety 
has no physical form- you cannot slice, 
shoot or punch your own fears, after all. 
An Archon of Anxiety does not have 
Wounds, and is completely immune to 
Physical Attacks. Magical Attacks may 
still be used to damage its Will, and 
Mental Attacks work on the Archon as 
normal. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 


 
 
Greater Demons 
‘Greater Demon’ is a catch-all term for the most 
formidable slaves of the Archdevils, the pinnacle 
of the Infernal Choirs beneath the ranks of Earls, 
Dukes, Princes and Kings. Greater Demons drink 
of their masters’ blood in a sacrament that binds 
their essence to the Diabolus In Musica, 
unshackling their malice, cruelty and ambition 
within bodies unbound to the laws of reality. 
Greater Demons come in many forms. A servant 
of the Cult of the Frozen Moon may appear as a 
colossal, deer-headed spider with seventy-
seven limbs, stridulating and thundering like a 
drumbeat; a servant of the Legion of the Night 
may appear as a radiantly handsome young man 
with washboard abs and alabaster skin- 
underneath which a sea of cockroaches, 
leeches and ticks writhe amidst red-raw muscle 
and seething pus. Greater Demons act as the 
heralds of the Infernal Choirs, and hide in plain 
sight on the streets of Skin City, ensnaring the 
weak, the foolish and the desperate to further 
their ambitions of power. 
 
Rank: Tyrant (5) 
 
Secondaries 
 
Armour 
Defence

Magical (4+), 
Physical (4+)

14 
19 
 
Attacks 
- 
Brutal Strike: Whether with a demonic 
weapon, claws, teeth or talons, the 
Greater Demon carves apart its foes. 
7D6, Physical. This Attack inflicts +1 
Damage for every result equal to or 
higher than the target’s Defence beyond 
the first. If this Attack inflicts at least 3 
Damage, it also throws the target back 
and knocks them to the ground. 
 
- 
Vile Exhalation: Whether a breath of 
poisonous insects, boiling magma or 
churning acid, the Greater Demon 
vomits up a surge of energy to burn its 
foes. 5D6, Magical. This Attack targets 
all enemies within a cone in front of the 
Greater Demon. The Greater Demon 
can also choose to set any target hit 
with this Attack on fire, or to coat them 
in acid. If a target is set on fire, they take 
1 Damage at the start of each of their 
Turns against which Armour does not 
function, until they extinguish the 
flames. If the target is coated in acid, 
their Physical Armour is worsened by 1 
for every time they’ve been coated (i.e. 
a 5+ would become a 6+, and so on). A 
target’s Physical Armour returns to 
normal after the end of Combat. 
 
Necromagic 
- 
Five Minor Necromagical Powers 
- 
Four Major Necromagical Powers 
- 
Three Metal Necromagical Powers 
 
Abilities 
- 
Infernal Choir: All Greater Demons 
owe their allegiance to one of the 
Infernal Choirs. Choose one of the 
seven Infernal Choirs when using a 
Greater Demon(s) in your game. Their 
Necromagical Powers and appearance 
will reflect your choice of Choir, exactly 
like they do when creating a 
Necromusician. You get to decide what 
their Powers are, and what they do! 
 
- 
Hellish Masque: Greater Demons walk 
among us, disguised as record execs, 
politicians, rebels without a cause- they 
lie, cheat and deceive in skin that’s not 
their own. All Greater Demons can 
assume a perfect Undead or Living 
disguise, becoming indistinguishable 
from a normal human or Undead 
denizen of Skin City. This guise will have 
a backstory, name and personal 
history- Greater Demons use these 
identities to manipulate mortals and 
secure positions of power. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 


 
 
Dream-Demons, Echoes of the 
Nightmares of God 
God is dead, yet not dead. In Her lifeless 
slumber, Her tortured mind dreams of a world 
riven by chaos, and from these dreams spring 
formless creatures that haunt the depths of the 
Labyrinth. Not truly demons and not truly 
angels, these monstrosities are the purest 
expression of God’s insanity, hideous, roiling 
creatures that have no beginning or end, 
shuddering from shape to shape in a ceaseless 
twist of colours, emotions, screams and shrieks. 
Dubbed ‘Dream-Demons’ by those fortunate 
enough to survive an encounter with one, they 
are utterly unpredictable, fickle and anarchic, 
acting with no rhyme or reason as they lurch 
through the Labyrinth’s passageways. Neither 
can a Dream-Demon truly die, for what can 
excise horror from the mind of a dead God? 
 
Rank: Tyrant (D6) 
 
Secondaries 
 
Armour 
Defence 
Necromagic 
Magical (X+), 
Mental (X+), 
Physical (X+) 
X+ 
3D6 
 
Speed 
Will 
Wounds 
Fast 
12 
12 
 
Attacks 
- 
Unravelling: The Dream-Demon exerts 
its hideous will upon the world and 
unmakes the matter of creation. 6D6, 
Mental OR Physical. This Attack inflicts 
+1 Damage for every result equal to or 
higher than the target’s Defence beyond 
the first. If this Attack reduces a target 
to 0 Will and/or 0 Wounds, the target is 
erased from reality and ceases to exist! 
 
- 
Perverse Visions of Madness: The 
Dream-Demon opens itself up and 
reveals but a sliver of the roiling mind of 
God. 8D6, Mental. If this Attack causes 
a target to lose Will, they lose an 
additional D6 points of Will. 
 
Necromagic 
- 
Black Mirror: A Dream-Demon has no 
connection to the Diabolus In Musica. It 
is more like a bloated parasite, drinking 
in and spitting out the Necromagic of 
others. A Dream-Demon rolls 3D6 at the 
start of Combat to determine how many 
points of Necromagic it will have. A 
Dream-Demon can then use any 
Necromagical Powers known by any 
creatures within its Line of Sight, 
spending the appropriate amount of 
Necromagic to do so. 
 
Abilities 
- 
Shifting Nightmare: A Dream-Demon 
has no sensate form, and exists only the 
liminal space between reality and 
nightmare. As such, it is all but 
impossible to predict. At the start of 
each Round of Combat, a Dream-
Demon rolls 4D6, one D6 at a time. The 
first result becomes its Magical Armour 
for the Round, the second its Mental 
Armour, the third its Physical Armour 
and the fourth its Defence. For example, 
if the Dream-Demon rolled 1, 4, 6 and 3, 
it would have a Magical Armour of 1+, a 
Mental Armour of 4+, a Physical Armour 
of 6+ and a Defence of 3+. 
 
- 
I Have Seen the Horror: Looking upon 
a Dream-Demon evokes profound 
insanity in the unprepared mind. Their 
very presence causes the boundaries of 
psychology to unravel, and God’s 
madness to spill out into the world. 
Every non-demonic and non-angelic 
character that can draw Line of Sight to 
a Dream-Demon must roll a D6 at the 
start of every Round of Combat (or 
every 30 seconds, outside of Combat). 
On a roll of 6+, they lose a point of Will 
and gain a point of Necromagic- this 
may take them above their starting 
Necromagic value. 
 
An Incantation to Evoke Aby’sst’iious’z, 
Dream-Demon of the Forgotten Deeps

The Deified 
Remnants of a Dead Heaven, Corpse-Angels, God’s Regrets 
 
Angel Corpse 
In the darker corners of the Labyrinth, the sound 
of weeping can be heard, the gentle hiss of tears 
on stone. Light- sickly, pale like a failing torch- 
whispers over the stone, until an Angel Corpse, 
its silver eyes wide with sorrow, approaches. 
Taking the form of beautiful young people clad in 
golden armour, Angel Corpses are the rotting 
remnants of the lesser Angels of Heaven, driven 
mad by sadness and the pain of their exile from 
the divine. They are mindless creatures, 
sustained only by God’s madness. Their wings 
are broken. Their hearts beat no more. 
 
Rank: Thrall 
 
Secondaries 
 
Armour 
Defence 
Necromagic 
Magical (5+) 
4+ 
0 
 
Speed 
Will 
Wounds 
Slow 
1 
1 
 
Attacks 
- 
Unnatural Strength: An Angel Corpse is 
prodigiously strong, capable of breaking 
bone with its bare hands. 2D6, Physical. 
 
- 
Sickening Wail: A scream of horror and 
pain, disgust at oneself and the tragic 
realisation that nothing really matters. 
Edgy as FUCK. 1D6, Mental. If this 
Attack causes Damage, the Angel 
Corpse may immediately make this 
Attack again. 
 
Abilities 
- 
Corrosive Misery: The air around an 
Angel Corpse is infected by an almost 
contagious sorrow, a sadness so 
profound that it unsettles even the most 
resolute of minds. The Mental Armour of 
all non-Angels that can hear the 
laments of an Angel Corpse is 
worsened by 1 (for example, a 5+ 
becomes a 6+, a 6+ becomes no 
Armour at all, and so on). This effect 
does not stack if multiple Angel 
Corpses are present. 
Lament of Haloes 
The haloes of the fallen angels were shattered 
against the flagstones of Heaven until little 
remained beyond yellow fragments. These 
fragments, washed by the tears and blood of 
God, were then reanimated as living clouds of 
sanctified energy, carrying with them the 
conflicted and chaotic memories of all the 
angels who bore them. A Lament of Haloes 
appears as a vortex of halo shards, living light 
and bloody feathers, crying out in a thousand 
voices in protest against the fall of Heaven. 
 
Rank: Thrall 
 
Secondaries

Defence

- 
Whirl of Feathers and Fragments: The 
Lament of Haloes swirls violently. 1D6, 
Physical. This Attack targets all enemies 
within reach of the Lament of Haloes. 
 
Abilities 
- 
Incorporeal: A Lament of Haloes is a 
formless being, a composite of light, 
Heavensgold and angelic memories. As 
such, it’s fuckin’ hard to land a telling hit 
on! Mundane weapons have no effect 
on a Lament of Haloes- it can only 
sustain Damage from Necromagic, or 
magical weapons. 
 
- 
Vessel of Power: A Lament of Haloes is 
imbued with the memories of a 
thousand dead angels. Other divine 
beings can consume this energy in 
times of dire need. An angel of Elite 
Rank or higher can consume a Lament 
of Haloes as a Free Action. If they do so, 
all Tests they make for the remainder of 
the combat gain an Advantage, and they 
recover 1 lost Wound and Will. 
 


 
 
Swarm of Cherubic Dust 
Not all angels had their bodies survive the fall of 
Heaven. Some were ground into powder, their 
skin atomised, their flesh burned away until 
nothing was left but fragments. However, it is not 
so easy to kill an angel, no matter how violated 
their physical form may become. Swarms of 
Cherubic Dust are the animated remnants of 
once mighty angels given form by rage and 
malice. They take the form of whirlwinds of 
scabs, torn flesh and desiccated bone 
fragments. A foul stench hangs on the air as they 
float through the Labyrinth, glowing with 
resentment and forgotten vestiges of power.

Music of Creation: As an angel, a 
Swarm of Cherubic Dust does not 
channel necromagic but rather the very 
music of creation. A Swarm of Cherubic 
Dust has Necromagical Powers in the 
same way as an undead or demon. 
However, the source of their power is 
the Music of Creation, not the Infernal 
Choirs. As such, their Powers are likely 
to be divine in origin and in form. 
 
- 
Bodiless: Swarms of Cherubic Dust 
have no physical or tangible form. A 
Swarm has no Wounds and cannot take 
Damage from Physical Attacks. Any 
Magical Attacks that would cause it to 
lose Wounds instead cause it to lose 
Will. 
 
Archangel 
Archangels were once the messengers of God, 
carrying the word and the scripture throughout 
Heaven and Earth. Upon God’s descent into 
madness, these missives mutated and became 
hideous screeds of insanity that blinded all 
those who dared look upon them. The 
Archangels thus have burned faces and milky-
white eyes, and constantly mutter in confused 
and crazed Enochian from mouths rimmed with 
scar tissue. They seek out those searching for 
knowledge in the depths of the Labyrinth, and 
bestow upon them the gift of witnessing God’s 
insanity for themselves. 
 
Rank: Thrall 
 
Secondaries

2

Archangel speaks the very words of God 
into their victim’s ear. 2D6, Mental.

Abilities 
- 
Music of Creation: As an angel, an 
Archangel does not channel 
necromagic but rather the very music of 
creation. An Archangel has 
Necromagical Powers in the same way 
as an undead or demon. However, the 
source of their power is the Music of 
Creation, not the Infernal Choirs. As 
such, their Powers are likely to be divine 
in origin and in form. 
 
- 
Bearers of the Divine Word: 
Archangels carry with them the infinite 
madness of God; their bodies convulse 
with forbidden knowledge. Any non-
angel that loses one or more points of 
Will within line of sight of an Archangel 
must immediately roll a D6. On a roll of 
5+, they lose an additional point of Will 
(this Ability does not trigger itself). 
 
 


 
 
Powers 
Powers were once the warrior-priests of Heaven, 
defending its golden spires from the predations 
of Hell and the darkness beyond creation. They 
are burning words given form, utterances of 
castigation, retribution and punishment cast in 
livid flames so hot and ferocious as to burn the 
eye. Powers lurk in the ruins of Heaven as 
perennial guardians, ferocious and 
uncompromising to all those that dare stumble 
upon their charge. To fight a Power is to fight a 
living flame; their bodies are constantly 
combusting, spilling out gouts of flame like 
screams from a scorched throat.

-

target that takes Damage from this 
Attack bursts into flames, and takes an 
additional 1 Damage on a roll of 4+ on a 
D6 at the start of each of their Turns, 
until the flames are extinguished.

- 
Blazing Form: A Power is embodied fire, 
forged of heat and the divine word. A 
Power is immune to any Damage 
caused by fire or heat, even if the 
source of it is magical. In addition, a 
Power’s presence can cause flammable 
material nearby to spontaneously 
combust, and pre-existing fires to swell 
violently in size. 
 
 
Dominions 
Dominions were the lesser lords of Heaven, 
commanders and teachers to the Lower Orders 
of angels beneath them. Their benediction, their 
grace, has been replaced by an animalistic, 
primal desire to bind, imprison and control. They 
appear as masses of interconnected orbs, 
microcosmic planets and stars, set in constant 
motion around a single bloodshot eye. They 
maintain vainglorious kingdoms of enslaved 
Angel Corpses in what intact corners of Heaven 
remain. They are jealous and cruel, prone to fits 
of pique towards those that dare defy them.

Merkabah 
The Merkabah were the winged chariots of 
Heaven, taking the form of five interlocking 
wheels of fire from which sprouted wings of 
golden-white feathers. As Heaven fell to ruin, 
these wheels each froze into knots of black ice, 
and the wings lost their feathers and became 
bloody, skeletal and gnawed as if by dogs. The 
Merkabah are pure expressions of shame and 
regret, horrified by what they became as a result 
of God’s madness. They seek to blind all who 
would look upon them, to preserve what dignity 
they have left.

Erelim 
The Erelim are fallen angels in all but name. As 
God slaughtered the citizens of Heaven, they 
rejected Her divinity and blasphemed against 
Her name. This act of spite damned them, 
contorting their treacherous bodies into painful 
parodies of the human form. An Erelim appears 
as a human with every bone broken, stitched 
back together at weird angles and studded 
through with bony shards. Their wings have been 
torn clean from their backs. Erelim are bitter, 
sadistic and vengeful. Yet some deal with 
humans willing to seek them out.

Virtues 
Virtues once embodied all the love, 
mercy and righteousness of God Herself, 
glorious reflections of her purity made manifest 
in shimmering squalls of divine light. Her 
descent into madness shattered them, broke 
their very essence apart and stitched it back 
together in a grotesque and ever-shifting 
patchwork. Virtues are now cascading masses 
of betentacled energy, their formlessness 
shifting in bizarre and painful ways as their 
hidden mouths extol the merits of blasphemy, 
cruelty, sexuality and slaughter. They among the 
angels are prisoners within their own bodies- 
painfully sane, even as their entire physiology 
convulses with madness.

addition, the warping substance of the 
Virtue starts to eat through mundane 
materials it touches, dissolving armour 
and weapons alike.

cannot lose or gain Will in any way. In 
addition, each time a Virtue loses a 
Wound, roll a  D6. On a 4+, a non-angel 
within the Virtue’s line of sight loses a 
point of Will. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 


 
 
Cherubim 
The Cherubim were the guardians of the Garden 
of Eden, the primordial heart of the world God 
cultivated as the birthplace of mankind. Original 
sin poisoned the garden; the Cherubim took up 
arms, and persecuted a terrible war against the 
slaves of Hell, punishing humanity and demons 
alike for their hubris and cruelty. Cherubim are 
four-winged, with four faces, each of which is a 
ruined mess of animal features smeared across 
human cheeks. They are bestial- almost feral- 
and carry with them swords that flame with 
unrelenting blue-white fire. With savage animal 
growls, they hunt down all who would profane 
the sanctity of creation with an unyielding 
fervour, even as their bodies bend and break into 
terrifying parodies of animal life.

pushed to the brink can exude an aura

into a frothing rage. When a Cherubim is 
reduced to less than half of its starting 
Will and/or Wounds, it starts to exude 
an Aura of Retribution. ALL characters,

by 1 (a 4+ becomes a 5+, and so on). In 
addition, affected characters must 
make a Spine Test at the start of their

they once called their allies at the 
discretion of the GM! 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 


 
 
Ophanim 
Ophanim are born of the Music of Creation, and 
are the purest expression of its melodies given 
physical form. An Ophanim is a knot of eye-
studded bands of necrotising flesh revolving 
ceaselessly around a poisonous fume of light. It 
resonates with constant, lilting music, at once 
discordant and harmonious, and the very air 
around them distorts in time to the notes of their 
symphony. To look upon an Ophanim is to invite 
awe and terror; witnesses kneel, stricken, before 
their form, the light within burning away their 
skin and tattooing the flesh beneath in musical 
staves and octaves that sing in tunes that make 
no sense.

Seraphic Corpse 
The greatest of God’s angels, the Seraphim were 
the last to die during the days of the War in 
Heaven. Six-winged, with blood red feathers that 
burned with holy zeal, their corpses now inhabit 
the lowest levels of the Labyrinth crying ‘Holy, 
Holy, Holy’ to all those that dare venture so 
deep. A Seraphic Corpse retains but a vestige of 
the angel it once was, and knows only hatred for 
those that would consort with the infernal and 
the mundane. Sworn to one of God’s three 
Aspects, they enact Her will in a sick parody of 
their former divine glory. To face one is a terrible 
folly; their powers are extraordinary, for each is a 
vessel for the fury of their dead mother.
