In the Constellation of the Black Widow 
An Adventure Module for Dead Metal Rhapsody 
 
What is an Adventure Module? 
 
An Adventure Module is a ready-to-play 
storyline for a tabletop RPG. It comes with all 
the information the GM needs to run it out of the 
box, including plot beats, NPCs, settings and 
challenges. An Adventure Module is perfect for a 
first-time GM, as it takes a lot of the prep work 
and planning out of running a session of Dead 
Metal Rhapsody. You don’t have to worry about 
designing your own story or fleshing out details 
of your own world- instead, you can get right into 
the action with a little bit of reading, and the odd 
bit of improvisation to get you by. 
 
Adventure Modules are designed to 
provide a robust skeleton for your players, a 
solid beginning, middle and end with room for 
them to get creative and experiment throughout. 
Running an Adventure Module is, by its nature, a 
little more restrictive than running your own 
campaign-  it can’t account for every zany 
possibility, crazy choice or unexpected outcome 
your players throw its way, after all! However, if 
you’re just starting out, and need an easy way to 
start, I’d recommend reading on. It might just be 
the right way for you to get your players into the 
game. 
 
 
 
 
IMPORTANT! 
 
IF YOU’RE INTENDING TO PLAY “In The 
Constellation of the Black Widow”, 
AND YOU’RE NOT YOUR GROUP’S 
GM… 
 
STOP READING NOW. 
 
 
 
 
 
How Does this Adventure Module Work? 
 
An Adventure Module consists of 
several sections, all of which combine to tell an 
interesting and engaging story for your players. 
These sections are as follows: 
 
1. Overview: A summary of the plot of the 
module, its main characters, its settings 
and themes. 
 
2. The Hook: The cold open, the smoking 
gun- how the story begins, and why your 
players should care. 
 
3. Plot Beats: A series of key moments 
and scenes that flesh out the story. 
Sometimes in chronological order, 
sometimes a little more free-flowing. 
This is the meat of your game. 
 
4. The Climax: The final, most exciting 
moment in the story, followed by its 
falling action and conclusion. What 
everything else has been leading up to. 
 
5. Appendices: Contains all the details of 
key NPCs and other bits and bobs you’ll 
need to run the game. 
 
The first thing you should do if you’re 
intending to run an Adventure Module is to read 
it through fairly thoroughly, to get a sense for 
how everything flows together and what type of 
story it’s trying to tell. After that, it’s worth 
making a few notes to remind yourself of the key 
plot beats that comprise the story, and any 
points where you think a little tweaking or 
improvisation might be necessary. The point of 
an Adventure Module is to minimise 
preparation- it doesn’t remove the need entirely! 
After you’ve read the Module, and 
familiarised itself with its contents, you’ll be 
ready to start GM’ing. 
 


 
 
Overview 
 
 
 
“His corpse washed up on the 
riverbank, not three hours ago. Couple o’ 
mudlarks dredged him out of the sludge. It’s the 
damnedest thing. He was smilin’. His whole 
face, from ear to ear, like he was the happiest 
man in the world. Doc Sawyer took a look at ‘im- 
I got the necropsy result a few minutes ago, 
before you arrived. ‘is whole body ain’t got any 
moisture in it. The blood’s all gone. No fluid in 
his organs, not even in his stomach. He’s as dry 
as a desert. But he’s smilin’. He’s smilin’, and I 
can’t for the death of me tell you why.” 
 
Danny ‘Dodger’ Sutcliffe, PI, Starfucker Inc. 
Detective Services 
 
 
“In the Constellation of the Black 
Widow” is a modern-day retelling of the story of 
Jack the Ripper- except instead of Spring-Heeled 
Jack, the killer is a grotesque spider-demon 
breeding Necromagical hybrids with the blood 
and sperm of her prey. Set in Skin City, in what 
was once the district of Whitechapel, it follows a 
series of inexplicable murders that the 
Necromusicians’ record label gets them to 
investigate as part of a morbid publicity stunt. 
As the rabbit hole goes deeper, they find 
themselves entrapped in the sinister world of 
the Black Widow, a powerful demon hellbent on 
replacing the rockstars of Skin City with her 
infected young. 
 
 
The story of “In The Constellation of the 
Black Widow” is split into the following sections: 
 
The Hook- A Murder on Whitechapel Lane 
 
A body is discovered, washed up on the 
shores of the River Thames. Coincidentally, it 
washes up directly outside the Necromusicians’ 
studio, right in the middle of band practice! 
Deciding to capitalise on a recent craze for 
urban legends and serial killer documentaries, 
the band’s record label tell them to investigate 
and take pictures for publicity- they market 
them as the ‘Death Metal Detectives’, which 
they say will really break the Japanese audience. 
Reluctantly, the band agree. 
 
Plot Beat A1- The Starfucker Inc. Detective 
Service 
 
Word on the street is that the Starfucker 
Inc. Detective Service has been looking into 
similar murders across the Whitechapel area. 
The Necromusicians can agree to help them out 
with an unrelated case in exchange for 
information, or otherwise steal it for themselves. 
Hey, whatever works! 
 
Starfucker Inc has a list of the victim’s 
names and addresses, including their 
occupations. The key clue is that they all worked 
on the docks for a shipping company called 
Metropolis. Though in what capacity, it remains 
uncertain… 
 
Plot Beat A2- The Type Ooh Negative Strip 
Club 
 
A used condom and a half-empty bottle 
of perfume on the corpse reveals he took a trip 
to the Type Ooh Negative Strip Club on the night 
of his death. The Necromusicians can pay the 
Club a visit and speak to either the patrons, the 
staff or both- with all the usual complications a 
drunk and drugged-up crowd of Dead-Heads 
offers. 
 
The Type Ooh Negative Strip Club 
reveals that the dead man shared a private room 
with a woman NOT employed by the Club itself 
last night. A search of the room reveals her 
name: Maris Pfeiffer, a fashion designer and 
dilettante from the heights of Skin City. 
 
Plot Beat A3- Marrow of a Bone, Artisanal 
Coffees 
 
The last object on the body of the victim 
was a loyalty card for a coffee house called 
‘Marrow of a Bone’, formerly known as Duncan 
Hills Coffee. It’s here that the dead man formed 
part of a band called Dead Sugar, alongside two 
of his friends. 
 
The Marrow of a Bone reveals that all 
the recent murder victims were aspiring 
Necromusicians, and they all disappeared 
around the docks at night. The staff recall they 
were seen near a certain warehouse, but they 
can’t quite remember which. 
 
Plot Beat B- Metropolis Rigging 
 
At this point, the players should know 
they’re looking for Maris Pfeiffer, and that the 
murders took place near the Metropolis Rigging 
Warehouse on the docks near the river. Visiting 
the warehouse, they discover it is full of 
spiderwebs, made out of rusting chains and 
blood. And that’s not the only thing that lurks 
within… 
 
The players have their first major fight 
on their hands at Metropolis Rigging, as they’re 
ambushed by the spider-demons ‘Maris Pfeiffer’ 
has been breeding there. 


 
 
Plot Beat C- The Crawling Tunnels 
 
Below the Metropolis Rigging 
Warehouse, the Necromusicians discover a 
hidden tunnel into the Labyrinth that leads to 
biomechanical, and decidedly insectoid, 
tunnels. Carving their way through the spider-
demons, they eventually uncover ‘Maris’ in her 
lair, where she’s been breeding spider-human 
hybrids out of the fluids of the dead to replace 
high-ranking musicians in Skin City. 
 
The Climax- Confronting the Black Widow 
 
Now that they’ve found Maris, and 
discovered the reason behind the string of 
murders, the players have to decide what to do 
with her. She’s a demon of considerable power 
and malice, sworn to the Legion of the Night, 
and won’t go down without a fight. Alternatively, 
the players could join her- as long as they don’t 
get infected, they stand to make a pretty penny 
from bumping off the competition! 
 
In either case, this is the end of the 
story. Either the players bring an end to ‘Maris’ 
and her reign of terror in Whitechapel, or they 
usher in a new age of terror over which they 
jointly rule. Or they get eaten by spiders. One or 
the other. 
 
 
Main Characters of the Module 
 
Maris Pfeiffer, AKA Nahemoth 
 
Maris Pfeiffer is the human avatar of the 
Greater Demon Nahemoth, a hideous 
biomechanical fusion of a young girl and a 
spider. Her human form is beguiling, beautiful 
and alive with a fiery charisma that manifests in 
her smouldering amber eyes. Nahemoth is 
cruel, cold and calculating- she views her 
victims as but a means to an end, and feels 
nothing but contempt for the living and dead 
alike. She uses her sexuality to deceive and 
entrap, but is not above transforming into her 
monstrous form to consume those that oppose 
her. 
 
Freddie ‘Feather-Fists’ Jonas 
 
Named ‘Feather-Fists’ by his old boxing 
mates, Freddie Jonas is Nahemoth’s most 
recent victim, having washed up on the shores 
of the Thames outside the band’s studio. He was 
a scrawny stevedore who worked for the 
Metropolis Rigging Company, as well as an 
aspiring Necromusician looking to hit the big 
time. He was taken in by Nahemoth’s lies, and 
drained of all his fluids. 
Danny ‘Dodger’ Sutcliffe 
 
‘Dodger’ Sutcliffe is a skeezy skeleton PI 
working for the Starfucker Inc. Detective 
Service. He’s investigating the disappearances 
of Metropolis shipping company employees 
across Whitechapel. He’s a hard-drinking, 
cynical and wisecracking guy with a snide sense 
of humour- his colleagues don’t really like him. 
 
Madame Borgir of the Type Ooh Negative 
 
Madame Borgir is the long-suffering 
proprietress of the Type Ooh Negative. She’s a 
Stitched, a construct of patchwork flesh and 
skin, and she stands some seven feet tall and is 
bound with bone and artificial muscle. Wearing 
a black suit and tie, she takes absolutely no shit 
from her clients and looks out for her girls. 
 
Kenny Iono, Barista 
 
Kenny Iono is one of Freddie’s 
bandmates, the lead singer of Dead Sugar and a 
meek, floppy-fringed newly-dead corpse. He’s 
melancholy, but has a good heart. 
 
Lettie Van Der Waal, Barista 
 
Lettie Van Der Waal is the other of 
Freddie’s bandmates, and is the group’s 
guitarist. She is short-tempered but has a 
wicked sense of humour. 
 
Setting 
Whitechapel 
 
A collapsed slum of red brick houses 
and rubble on the banks of the River Thames. 
 
The Crawling Tunnels 
 
Web-encrusted tunnels of flesh 
beneath the surface of Skin City. 
 
Key Themes for Session Zero 
 
All of these potentially upsetting themes are 
present in this adventure. It is important to 
discuss them with your group before you begin 
the module. Remember to use the safety tools 
listed in the GM’ing Chapter earlier in this book. 
 
Arachnophobia 
Body Horror 
Gore 
Implied Sexual Violence 
 
 


 
 
The Hook 
 
 
 
Read or paraphrase the following text to 
your players when they’re ready to start the 
module: 
 
 
 
“Another beautiful day in Skin City. 
Crawling fog rises off the sunken river, creeping 
through the streets of Whitechapel as the sun 
breaks through the clouds of sulphur up above. 
A drunken Dead-Head snores in the gutter; a 
laughing Goth and his girlfriend walk their 
corpse-hound to a park full of calcified trees; 
two leather-clad punks spray anarchist graffiti 
on an alleyway wall, then add a dick for good 
measure in red and black ink. So far, so normal. 
Just another beautiful day. Right? 
 
 
But you’re not out in the city. You’ve got 
a job to do. Since you signed to Titan Blood 
Records, the suits upstairs have been 
demanding you do so much more boring shit 
than when you were on the pub circuit. Shit like 
‘practice’, ‘learn to play’- y’know, stuff that gets 
in the way of fucking up demons and sinking 
piss. Boring as it may be, though, it does pay the 
bills. So that’s why you’re here. Stuck in a 
recording studio on the riverbank. Tuning up for 
a practice sesh with the execs watching you 
from the booth. Hey. Looks like it’s time to put on 
a show.” 
 
 
 
To get you started, have each of your 
players describe their Necromusicians, and 
their warm-up routine for band practice. What’s 
their name? What do they look like, and what 
instrument do they play? Have they got any 
quirks or unique features, any little personal 
rituals they perform before getting up on stage? 
Also get each player to describe a personal 
connection their Necromusician has with 
another member of the band. That should help 
to get the band dynamic flowing! 
 
Once everyone’s had a chance to 
introduce themselves, work with your players to 
describe the recording studio and the sleazy 
panel of record execs watching the band tune 
up. You don’t have to go into terrific detail, but 
try to have one of the execs act as the band’s 
manager or contract holder. You’ll be using them 
to get the plot moving in a moment. 
 
After the scene has been set, give the 
band a chance to show off. Have them vibe 
together, jam a little, get fancy with their 
instruments- get them to perform. You might call 
for some Tests if they try anything particularly 
exciting or unusual. Give them each enough 
time to do something cool, then, when they’re 
comfortable, read or paraphrase the following 
text: 
 
 
“BANG! Something smashes into the 
window. There’s a slap of cold river water, and 
you look up to see a rotting hand scraping down 
the glass. It’s wearing a studded leather 
wristband, and its fingernails are bitten to the 
quick. Another slap- a waterlogged face, all lank 
hair and a missing eyeball, presses up against 
the window. The river drains out of it. A cry goes 
up from the booth. One of the execs points to 
the fire escape. It’s clear he wants you to deal 
with this unexpected surprise…” 
 
 
 
One way or another, the 
Necromusicians should be forced outside to 
investigate the washed-up corpse. Outside the 
recording studio are the banks of the River 
Thames: muddy, stinking, and full of wreckage 
and loose bones. Mudlarks, orphan undead 
children, pick through the remnants of 
whatever’s drowned in the river; seabirds circle 
overhead, and the shadow of the shattered 
Tower Bridge falls over everything. This is a great 
opportunity to get descriptive- really bring 
Whitechapel to life! You’re looking to hit the 
following points: 
 
• 
Everything feels run-down and sleazy. 
It’s like the post-war malaise never went 
away. Imagine pebbledash houses, red 
brick terraces, dead gardens and 
narrow winding streets. It’s depressing, 
grimy and forgotten. 
 
• 
Everything feels dangerous. The 
mudlarks on the riverbank hold 
sharpened debris and have hungry 
expressions. The people of Whitechapel 
are suspicious, cynical and quick to 
anger. 
 
• 
Everything feels tense. There’s a sense 
that something is close to unravelling, 
or burning up like a shortening fuse. 


 
 
When the Necromusicians arrive at the 
body, they see it is the corpse of a scrawny 
young man, maybe in his mid-twenties before 
his First Death and now utterly deceased. He’s 
wearing a denim patch jacket, a bullet belt and 
tight black jeans- he looks like an 80s thrasher, 
complete with dated hairstyle and faint smell of 
cheap beer. But what’s most unusual about him 
is the fact his body, aside from the water of the 
Thames, is completely dry. 
 
Every inch of the man is desiccated. All the 
fluids- blood, water, spinal fluid, lymph, bile, the 
lot- have been drained out of him. There are no 
marks on his body. No puncture wounds, no 
incisions. He doesn’t appear to have been 
poisoned, or succumbed to any sort of tangible 
injury. If the Necromusicians choose to inspect 
his body, they discover the following clues: 
 
• 
Starfucker Inc. Business Card: The 
man was carrying a laminated business 
card from the Starfucker Inc. Detective 
Agency, registered to one Danny 
‘Dodger’ Sutcliffe. In untidy sharpie on 
the back, Sutcliffe has written the 
words ‘call me if another one goes 
missing’. 
 
• 
Condom and Perfume Bottle: A 
premium condom wrapper in the man’s 
pocket belongs to the Type Ooh 
Negative Strip Club. A bottle of 
expensive-looking perfume with a lime 
ribbon on it can be found in his bag. 
 
• 
Marrow of a Bone Loyalty Card: The 
last item is in a plastic lanyard around 
the man’s neck. Instead of an ID, it’s a 
loyalty card for a coffee shop called 
Marrow of a Bone. It has a reminder 
sticker on it for ‘band practice tonight- 
remind Lettie not to be fuckin’ late’. 
 
The man has nothing else on his person 
aside from these objects. 
 
After the players have had a chance to 
inspect the body, the record execs from the 
recording studio should arrive on the scene. 
Unlike the Necromusicians, who might be 
confused, bemused or horrified, the record 
execs see the corpse and suddenly get very 
excited. They talk to one another in a huddle, 
before striding over to the Necromusicians with 
a grin on their faces. Pick one of the worst, most 
annoying and openly greedy pricks you came up 
with at the start of the module, and read the 
following text in their voice: 
 
 
 
“Me and the guys were talking, yeah? And 
we were thinking, wow, what a stroke of luck! 
Y’see, we think you need a gimmick. The band’s 
just not… it’s just not synergistic, or sexy, enough 
to sell. No reflection on you, yeah? Cool? Cool. 
Now, here’s what we’re going to do. We’re going 
to get in on this murder, and get some free 
publicity to boot. You’re going to be a detective 
band! Should really play with the Japanese 
audience, yeah? Solve this murder, take some 
good publicity shots, quick one with the widow 
and- boom! Household names overnight!” 
 
 
 
Chances are your players will have 
something to say about this. Which is fair 
enough. However, the record execs won’t take 
no for an answer. They can be negotiated with- 
they’re willing to provide additional resources 
and funding at a push, and might even adjust the 
band’s contract if they drive a hard bargain. 
Ultimately, though, these are delusional, self-
obsessed, money-grubbing record execs with no 
idea about the real world. Make sure they come 
across that way, and feel free to make the 
request seem as unreasonable and stupid as 
you like. 
 
 
After the players have spoken to the 
execs, it’s time for them to start following up 
their leads. There are three clues on the man’s 
body for them to investigate- let them pick 
which they want to follow, then turn to the 
relevant section of the module. 
 
But What If They Don’t Follow the Clues? 
 
 
If your players decide to go off-piste 
(take the body to the doctors, report to the 
police, etc.), you’ll need to get improvisational! 
The doctors, and medicine in general, has no 
idea what killed the man. Necrosurgeons can 
inform the players it was likely a magical poison 
or invocation, but little more than that. The 
police don’t give a fuck about the man’s death- 
he’s just another statistic in Skin City’s growing 
list of casualties! Allow them to explore these 
possibilities, and drip-feed them bits and 
pieces, but encourage them- subtly- to get back 
on track and investigate the clues. If they DO, 
however, figure something out early- let them 
have it. Reward creativity and critical thinking! 


 
 
Plot Beat A1- The 
Starfucker Inc. Detective 
Service 
 
Overview 
• 
The Necromusicians meet Danny 
‘Dodger’ Sutcliffe, a PI investigating a 
series of similar murders across 
Whitechapel. 
• 
Either through persuasion, coercion, an 
exchange of favours or good old-
fashioned bribery, the Necromusicians 
get hold of a list of names and 
addresses. 
• 
KEY CLUE: All of the victims worked for 
the Metropolis shipping company. 
 
The Starfucker Inc. Detective Service is little 
more than a dingy, grimy apartment complex 
above a Thai restaurant, sandwiched between 
the mouth of an alley and a collapsed 
Underground station. It’s reached via a rusty fire 
escape, and is guarded half-heartedly by a 
Frankenroadie bouncer named Clive. Clive is 
easy enough to get past- he’s not particularly 
clever, and, despite his hulking Stitched body, 
he's a bit of a coward. Once inside, the players 
see a neo-noir 1920s office, full of cigarette 
smoke and profane altars to Grim’thotkh, the 
demonic patron of nosy parkers. They also see 
Danny ‘Dodger’ Sutcliffe. 
 
 
KEY CHARACTER- Danny ‘Dodger’ Sutcliffe 
 
‘Dodger’ Sutcliffe is a skeezy skeleton PI working 
for the Starfucker Inc. Detective Service. He’s 
investigating the disappearances of Metropolis 
shipping company employees across 
Whitechapel. He’s a hard-drinking, cynical and 
wisecracking guy with a snide sense of humour- 
his colleagues don’t really like him. Besides, 
whenever he drinks, most of the whiskey slides 
straight through his ribcage. 
 
 
 
Danny is initially suspicious of the 
Necromusicians, but opens up to them once he 
realises he can use them to get more intel about 
his case. He offers them a deal- do a little ‘wet 
work’ for him, and he’ll offer up the names and 
addresses of other victims just like the one 
washed up from the river. Try to play Danny like a 
proper hardboiled 1920s detective, Brooklyn 
accent and all. 
 
 
If the players take Danny’s deal, he 
directs them to a warehouse on the edge of 
town where a gang of Thrashers have been 
moving shipments of drugs under the watch of 
Jenny Clay, Aspiring Necromusician (see 
Appendix 1 for Stat Blocks). He instructs them to 
deal with Jenny, either by convincing her to 
abandon her operation, or by putting her six feet 
in the ground. The warehouse is a disused maze 
of shipping containers and rubble. Jenny herself 
is an overconfident, bratty, bubblegum-chewing 
bassist with a bad attitude. She can be talked 
down, with difficulty, but responds violently to 
any attempt to intimidate her or anything she 
perceives as an insult. If a Combat does break 
out, it should involve two Thrashers per 
Necromusician and Jenny herself. 
 
 
Once Jenny has been dealt with, Danny 
will provide the players with the KEY CLUE 
below. 
 
 
If the players do not take Danny’s deal, 
they can instead attempt to browbeat him, or 
bribe him, into giving up the information for free. 
Danny doesn’t break easily- attempts to 
intimidate him have a Disadvantage, and he 
doesn’t respond to appeals to his better nature 
(he doesn’t have one). He does, however, 
respond well to bribery, and will hand over the 
KEY CLUE if paid off well enough. 
 
 
KEY CLUE- The Metropolis Shipping Company 
 
The victim, and twelve other victims found on 
the shores of the Thames, all worked for the 
Metropolis Shipping Company. 
 
The company has a large industrial complex in 
the Whitechapel Docks, and has seven different 
departments. 
 
Danny isn’t sure which department the victims 
worked for. 
 
He does have a list of their addresses. Most of 
them lived in rented flats on the edge of town. 
 
 


 
 
A Couple of Contingencies 
 
What If the Players Go Straight to the 
Metropolis Shipping Company? 
 
 
If the players go to the Metropolis 
Shipping Company and start asking questions 
about the missing employees, it’s clear that 
upper management couldn’t give less of a toss 
about those labouring in their warehouses. The 
atmosphere of the company should be rough 
and ready; minimal paperwork, disregard for 
workplace safety, gruff and burly undead 
shouldering past each other, lots of swearing 
and not a lot of politeness. The workers respond 
to questions guardedly, if at all. 
 
 
If the players push on, or try to break 
into a warehouse or office, there’s a chance they 
might stumble on the tunnels beneath the 
Metropolis Rigging Warehouse. Roll a D6- on a 1, 
they chance upon the tunnel entrance IF they 
successfully infiltrate the warehouse without 
being seen. On any other result, they find little 
more than packing crates and forklifts. 
 
 
If you want, the players can discover the 
name of the victim at the company, with a bit of 
finagling. Perhaps a disgruntled employee spits 
it out, or they find an employee ID lying around. 
This gives them the name Freddie ‘Feather-Fists’ 
Jonas. 
 
What If the Players Follow Up on the 
Addresses of the Other Victims? 
 
 
If the players go to one or more of the 
addresses on Danny’s list, they find dishevelled 
and unpleasant bedsits with very little in them. 
The lifestyles of the victims were solitary- they 
had no family, very few friends, and little to their 
name. However, the players can discover the 
following details by visiting the addresses: 
 
- 
The victims were all aspiring 
Necromusicians. 
- 
The victims all frequented the Type Ooh 
Negative Strip Club within the past 
three months. 
- 
All the victims’ possessions are still in 
their apartments, meaning the 
murderer wasn’t interested in stealing 
from them. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 


 
 
Plot Beat A2- The 
Type Ooh Negative Strip 
Club 
 
Overview 
 
• 
The Necromusicians meet Madame 
Borgir and the Living Dead Girls of the 
Type Ooh Negative. 
• 
Concerned about the victim’s 
whereabouts, Madame Borgir shares 
what she knows willingly. 
• 
KEY CLUE: The victim went into a 
private room the night before his 
disappearance with a woman called 
Maris Pfeiffer, a fashion designer and 
sewing expert from Skin City. 
 
The Type Ooh Negative was once an 
ostentatious West End theatre that was 
smashed into three pieces when London fell. An 
enterprising polycule of self-proclaimed ‘Living 
Dead Girls’, led by Madame Borgir, repaired the 
theatre and converted it into a strip club, leaning 
heavily into the gothic regency aesthetic. The 
interior is a fusion of early Victorian and 90s 
goth. Think beautiful dresses and garters, 
gaslamps and oil paintings, but crossed with HR 
Giger, cyberpunk and far too much black 
eyeliner. 
 
As soon as they enter, the players will be 
accosted by the Living Dead Girls, the undead 
proprietors of the Type Ooh Negative. They’ll be 
offered drinks, drugs and perhaps something 
more. A show is taking place on stage: be as 
vivid as your group allows. The Living Dead Girls 
often use Necromagic to enhance their 
performances- fire, brimstone, enslaved 
demons, whatever exotic melodrama your brain 
can conjure! 
 
Once the players start asking questions 
about the murder victim, the Living Dead Girls 
become withdrawn and suspicious. Eventually, 
one of them will tip off Madame Borgir, who will 
come to speak to the players after they’ve made 
enough enquiries. 
 
 
 
 
KEY CHARACTER- Madame Borgir of the Type 
Ooh Negative 
 
Madame Borgir is the long-suffering proprietress 
of the Type Ooh Negative. She’s a Stitched, a 
construct of patchwork flesh and skin, and she 
stands some seven feet tall and is bound with 
bone and artificial muscle. Wearing a black suit 
and tie, she takes absolutely no shit from her 
clients and looks out for her girls. 
 
 
 
Unlike the cynical and self-centred 
Danny, Madame Borgir is willing to talk. She’s 
genuinely concerned about the victim- he was a 
good customer, polite and friendly, and she 
valued his business. She doesn’t need much 
persuading to share the following KEY CLUE. The 
only thing she doesn’t know, due to the man’s 
shyness, is the victim’s name. 
 
 
KEY CLUE- Maris Pfeiffer 
 
The victim was last seen by Madame Borgir 
entering a private room with a well-dressed 
young woman named Maris Pfeiffer. 
 
Maris is a fashion designer from Skin City, well-
known for her elegant sewing and modern, chic 
designs. 
 
She’s been coming to the Type Ooh Negative 
frequently, and has been seen in the company of 
several other men over the past few months. 
 
 
 
If pushed for more information, 
Madame Borgir informs the players that she 
always felt deeply uncomfortable around Maris. 
There was something about her that disturbed 
her, a sense of unease, a sense that something 
wasn’t quite right. 
 
 
If asked to describe Maris, Madame 
Borgir describes her as beguiling, beautiful and 
alive with a fiery charisma. She was wearing a 
white silk suit, a red cravat and a pair of smoked 
glasses. She had long, flowing auburn hair that 
reached to her waist, and she carried herself 
with an easy confidence that, in anyone else, 
would seem arrogant but in her seemed 
perfectly natural. 
 


 
 
A Couple of Contingencies 
 
What If the Players Go into Skin City to Try 
and Track Down Maris? 
 
 
If the players make enquiries in the 
fashion outlets of Skin City, looking for Maris 
Pfeiffer, they find out that she works as a 
freelancer for a variety of fashion houses. Her 
recent work includes a line of jeans for Pants-
Tera, some haute couture for a French-Japanese 
designer known as Go Go Jira and her own 
personal brand of silk handkerchiefs sold to the 
discerning undead. They are unable to find her 
address, or where she works. In fact, 
suspiciously, no-one is really able to point to a 
design studio she operates out of. Nor has 
anyone met her outside of her work… 
 
What If the Players Stake Out the Type Ooh 
Negative for Maris? 
 
 
If the players try to ensnare Maris on her 
next visit to the Type Ooh Negative, they do 
indeed find her in the company of another 
Metropolis Shipping employee. If confronted, 
she is thoroughly personable and charming, to 
the point of being disarming. She deflects 
questions with witty repartee, and does her best 
to allay the players’ suspicions. She does not 
stoop to violence, unless the players attack her 
first, and cannot be provoked. If a Combat does 
break out, use the Nahemoth Stat Block in 
Appendix 1.  
 
 
If the players do not confront Maris, and 
instead follow her and her latest victim, they 
track her down to the Metropolis Rigging 
Warehouse, where she lures her victim inside. 
This leads the players to the Crawling Tunnels. 
 
 


 
 
Plot Beat A3- Marrow 
of a Bone Coffee House 
 
Overview 
• 
The Necromusicians meet Freddie’s 
bandmates, Kenny Iono and Lettie Van 
Der Waal. 
• 
After convincing them that they’re 
investigating Freddie’s death, and not 
looking to profit off it, the twosome 
inform the players where Freddie was 
last seen. 
• 
KEY CLUE: The victims all disappeared 
near a Rigging Warehouse on the docks, 
although the company to which the 
warehouse belonged is unknown. 
 
Marrow of a Bone is a local coffee shop 
overlooking the river. It’s proudly independent: 
loud death metal blasts from boomboxes taped 
to the walls, and big tubs of vinyl records for sale 
and trade stand next to all the tables. It’s a lively 
place, with plenty of local colour. It smells of 
roasted coffee beans and Irish whiskey. 
 
Inside, the players immediately meet two 
bickering baristas, arguing over the correct use 
of a milk frother. They are Kenny Iono and Lettie 
Van Der Waal, and they’re wrestling like children 
over the machine. 
 
 
KEY CHARACTER- Kenny Iono 
 
Kenny Iono is one of Freddie’s bandmates, the 
lead singer of Dead Sugar and a meek, floppy-
fringed newly-dead corpse. He’s melancholy, 
but has a good heart. 
 
 
 
KEY CHARACTER- Lettie Van Der Waal 
 
Lettie Van Der Waal is the other of Freddie’s 
bandmates, and is the group’s guitarist. She is 
short-tempered but has a wicked sense of 
humour. 
 
 
 
 
 
The players are able to break up the 
scuffle between Kenny and Lettie quite easily- 
it’s more playfighting than an actual argument. If 
asked about Freddie, both of them will 
immediately get defensive and suspicious. The 
players will need to persuade them to open up- 
they do not respond well to threats or 
intimidation, but soften if the players express 
that they’re trying to help. If convinced, Kenny 
and Lettie admit the players to a backroom, 
converted into a garage studio. Freddie’s drums 
are here, as well as the other two band 
members’ instruments. 
 
 
In the backroom, Kenny and Lettie 
provide the following KEY CLUE to the players: 
 
 
KEY CLUE- Freddie’s Disappearance 
 
The players learn the victim’s name: Freddie 
Jonas, known as ‘Feather-Fists’ to his friends. 
 
Freddie went missing near the docks. He told his 
bandmates he was going to skip practice for the 
night via a text. He said he had a ‘hot date’, and 
hoped they understood. 
 
Kenny and Lettie don’t know where on the docks 
Freddie went specifically, only that he was on 
his way to a Rigging Warehouse. They don’t 
know which company the warehouse belonged 
to. 
 
 
 
 
Kenny and Lettie can also provide the 
following pieces of information about Freddie, if 
asked: 
 
- 
Freddie was ambitious. He wanted their 
band, Dead Sugar, to be major players 
on the Skin City circuit. 
 
- 
Freddie was quite a potent 
Necromusician. He wielded 
considerable power from his patron in 
the Angels of Death. 
 
- 
Freddie was profoundly lonely. He was 
always insecure about his appearance, 
and had been through a series of bad 
relationships recently. 
 
 
 


 
 
A Couple of Contingencies 
 
What If the Players Start Checking Every 
Single Rigging Warehouse on the Docks? 
 
 
If the players start investigating all of 
the rigging warehouses on the Thames, they find 
themselves bogged down in hours upon hours of 
looking at ropes. For each warehouse they 
check, secretly roll 2D6. On a roll of 12, they 
chance upon the Metropolis Rigging Warehouse. 
On any other result, it’s all just ropes and boxes. 
 
What If the Players Go and Find Freddie’s 
Home? 
 
 
Freddie’s home is an apartment on the 
thirteenth floor of a concrete tower block. 
There’s not a lot inside. He didn’t have a family, 
and his only friends were Kenny and Lettie. The 
players can find his company ID in his home, 
however, that reveals he worked for the 
Metropolis Shipping Company, if they didn’t 
know that fact already. 
 
 
Help! My Players are Stuck, 
and I Don’t Know What to Do! 
 
 
With all the KEY CLUES on the table, the 
players should be able to figure out that Maris 
Pfeiffer is likely the serial killer responsible for 
the string of murders in Whitechapel. They 
should also be able to figure out that the 
Metropolis Shipping Company connects all the 
victims, and that the Rigging Warehouse is 
where all the victims went missing. This should 
lead them nicely to Plot Beat B- Metropolis 
Rigging. 
 
 
If, however, your players get stuck and 
don’t know how to proceed, it’s time to subtly 
nudge them in the right direction. Try to drip-
feed them useful information in a thematic way 
to get them back on track. Perhaps Kenny and 
Lettie suddenly remember some useful details, 
or the players receive a tip from the record execs 
or from viewers online. Maybe Maris makes a 
mistake, and gives away her hunting ground by 
accident. The key is to nudge, not to force. Let 
the players feel their way to discovering the 
truth, but gently adjust their course when you 
need to. Give hints, give more clues, and don’t 
be afraid to do so if it gets the story moving. 
 
Putting the Pieces Together 
 
 
Once the players have completed Plot 
Beats A1, A2 and A3, they should have the 
following KEY CLUES: 
 
KEY CLUE- The Metropolis Shipping Company 
 
The victim, and twelve other victims found on 
the shores of the Thames, all worked for the 
Metropolis Shipping Company. 
 
The company has a large industrial complex in 
the Whitechapel Docks, and has seven different 
departments. 
 
Danny isn’t sure which department the victims 
worked for. 
 
He does have a list of their addresses. Most of 
them lived in rented flats on the edge of town. 
 
KEY CLUE- Maris Pfeiffer 
 
The victim was last seen by Madame Borgir 
entering a private room with a well-dressed 
young woman named Maris Pfeiffer. 
 
Maris is a fashion designer from Skin City, well-
known for her elegant sewing and modern, chic 
designs. 
 
She’s been coming to the Type Ooh Negative 
frequently, and has been seen in the company of 
several other men over the past few months. 
 
KEY CLUE- Freddie’s Disappearance 
 
The players learn the victim’s name: Freddie 
Jonas, known as ‘Feather-Fists’ to his friends. 
 
Freddie went missing near the docks. He told his 
bandmates he was going to skip practice for the 
night via a text. He said he had a ‘hot date’, and 
hoped they understood. 
 
Kenny and Lettie don’t know where on the docks 
Freddie went specifically, only that he was on 
his way to a Rigging Warehouse. They don’t 
know which company the warehouse belonged 
to. 
 
 
Putting these all together, the players 
should know to check out the Rigging 
Warehouse for the Metropolis Shipping 
Company, and to search for Maris Pfeiffer there. 


 
 
Plot Beat B- Metropolis 
Rigging  
 
Overview  
• 
The Necromusicians enter the 
Metropolis Rigging Warehouse and 
discover its gruesome interior. 
• 
The players are then ambushed by 
hybrid spider-demons bred by 
Nahemoth to guard her hatchery. 
• 
LINK: After defeating the spider-
demons, the players discover a foul-
smelling tunnel leading into the 
Labyrinth below. 
 
 
When the players arrive at the Metropolis 
Rigging Warehouse, they find it suspiciously 
silent. There are no security guards, lights or 
cameras. Its doors are locked up tight, but there 
are other ways for them to creep inside, 
including half-open windows, service hatches or 
good old-fashioned brute force. Once inside, 
read or paraphrase the following text to your 
players: 
 
 
“The interior of the warehouse is 
shrouded in darkness. There are no lights here. 
The air is full of the sound of machinery and 
metal, clanking and grinding. You take a step 
forward. Your feet sink into something soft and 
sticky, but hard, sharp and hot all at once. You 
look down. Then up. The entire warehouse is full 
of enormous spiderwebs. But they’re not made 
of silk. Instead, they’re woven out of solidified 
blood, mucus, razor wire and lengths of chain. 
You see desiccated bodies hanging from the 
rafters. This place reeks of demons and death.” 
 
 
 
Give the players some time to explore 
the warehouse before the inevitable ambush. If 
they’re lucky, perhaps they spot the lurking 
spider-demons before they’re jumped, avoiding 
being taken by surprise. Inside the warehouse, 
the players find evidence of many other dead 
employees of Metropolis Shipping, their bodies 
drained of all their fluids just like Freddie’s. 
Eventually, once you’ve built enough tension, 
you can spring the trap. Read or paraphrase the 
following text: 
 
 
“Suddenly, there is a hideous chittering 
sound. It’s like a mouthful of broken gears being 
chewed up, then spat out. It’s like a monstrous 
cello, being strummed by a chainsaw. Then they 
erupt out of the webs, and lunge towards you. 
Huge biomechanical spiders. Their bodies are 
hybrids of necrotic flesh, cyborg machinery and 
inscribed demonic runes. Fangs glittering with 
poison, they lunge towards you.” 
 
 
 
This triggers the start of a Combat. If the 
players were caught unawares, all of their Tests 
during the first Round suffer a Disadvantage. 
There should be one Hybrid Spider-Demon (see 
Appendix 1) per Necromusician. The creatures 
cannot be reasoned with- they’re slavering, 
insane and animalistic, more like feral dogs then 
sentient beings. 
 
 
Once the Necromusicians have 
destroyed the demons, the warehouse falls 
silent. Further exploration reveals the bodies of 
the other victims being investigated by Danny 
‘Dodger’ Sutcliffe and the Starfucker Inc. 
Detective Agency. In addition, a couple of Living 
Dead Girls from the Type Ooh Negative can also 
be found strung from the rafters. The last thing 
the players find is a disturbing sight indeed. 
Read the following text to your players: 
 
 
“There is a body lying on a slab in front 
of you. And another body lying next to it. The first 
seems to belong to a record exec- you’d 
recognise that cheap, shit-ass suit anywhere. 
The other, though, is halfway between the exec 
and a spider-demon. Almost as if one was 
turning into the other. Flasks of fluid sit on the 
slab next to the bodies. Blood. Sweat. Lymph. 
Brain juice. They’re all labelled in a spiky hand.” 
 
 
 
This is the first clue as to Nahemoth’s 
ultimate plan. Once the players have finished 
investigating the rest of the warehouse, which 
contains little more than spider lairs and filth, 
they find a tunnel entrance, heading 
downwards, lined with chains and webs. This is 
a gateway into the Labyrinth. A gateway to 
Nahemoth’s domain. 
 


 
 
Plot Beat C- The 
Crawling Tunnels 
 
Overview  
• 
The Necromusicians enter the 
Labyrinth and find themselves in a hive-
like warren full of biomechanics and 
insectoid architecture. 
• 
The players must overcome a number 
of dangerous environmental challenges 
to progress. 
• 
LINK: After navigating the Labyrinth, the 
players confront Nahemoth, aka ‘Maris 
Pfeiffer’ and the Climax of the module 
begins. 
 
When the players enter the tunnels below 
the Metropolis Rigging Warehouse, read or 
paraphrase the following text to them:  
 
 
“There’s no doubt- you’re crossing into 
the depths of the Labyrinth. The tunnels are 
narrow, winding and claustrophobic. They seem 
to be made of a foul-smelling resin, and are shot 
through with wires, fat black cables and 
unintelligible machinery. The air is sticky and 
unpleasantly warm; there’s a constant clank 
and hiss, and a distant rumble, like the churning 
wheels of a factory turning in the fog. Every 
surface is covered in the same bloody chain 
webs you saw in the warehouse. Spiders the size 
of your palms scuttle across the floor.” 
 
 
 
The Crawling Tunnels are a corner of the 
Fifth Circle of Hell, merged with the arachnoid 
corruption of Nahemoth herself. They are a 
nightmarish maze of narrow passageways, 
chasms and conveyor belts that wind through 
biomechanical factory floors. As the players 
explore them, try to stress how unnatural and 
unpleasant things are here. The architecture is 
mangled; everything is grimy; it’s hot, gore-
stained and damp; the ground beneath your feet 
feels hostile. 
 
 
During their journey through the 
Crawling Tunnels, the players should always feel 
as if they are at risk of attack. Build tension, and 
make them jump at shadows. In reality, 
Nahemoth awaits them in her lair. But there’s no 
reason they shouldn’t think that the spider-
demons are actively hunting them down! 
 
 
In addition to the suspense of delving 
through the passageways, the players should 
encounter at least THREE of the following 
environmental hazards. You can use the rules 
for exploring the Labyrinth in “In the Nightside 
Eclipse” earlier in this book to describe how 
they overcome these dangers. For reference, 
clocks should be no more than 3 or 4 segments, 
if used. 
 
- 
A monstrous wall of webbing, covered 
in bloody scabs and cogs, blocks your 
path. 
- 
A pit of molten metal and razor blades 
yawns in the passage ahead. There is no 
bridge. 
- 
The tunnels grow so complex and 
nonsensical that navigating them 
becomes all but impossible. 
- 
A series of fume-filled trapdoors, locked 
shut with barbed wire, appear to be the 
only way forward. 
- 
A channel of filthy water flows into a 
turbine engine, which blocks the tunnel 
entirely. 
- 
The passage becomes ferociously hot, 
blistering the skin, and is almost 
impossible to explore safely. 
- 
A conveyor belt oscillates furiously 
between directions, each of its ends 
plunging into a meat grinder. 
- 
A sealed doorway is locked shut with a 
demonic cipher, burning runes binding 
it in webs. 
 
Example: the players encounter the fume-
filled trapdoors. This could be handled as a 
simple Brains Check to determine a safe way 
through, or a Brains Check followed by Guts to 
heave open the correct passage. It could also be 
adjudicated with clocks, to ramp up the tension 
of the flowing poisonous gas. The choice is up to 
you! 
 
Once the players have overcome at least 
three environmental hazards, and have had 
enough time to grow appropriately fearful of the 
Crawling Tunnels, you can proceed to the 
Climax of the module- the final confrontation 
with Nahemoth herself. 
 
 
 


 
 
The Climax- Confronting 
the Black Widow 
 
Overview 
- 
The Necromusicians confront Maris 
Pfeiffer, aka the Greater Demon 
Nahemoth, in her subterranean lair. 
- 
Nahemoth reveals her plan to use the 
visceral fluids of her victims to make 
doppelgangers she intends to implant 
into the records industry, allowing her to 
pull important strings. 
- 
Nahemoth offers the Necromusicians 
the chance to join her, and benefit from 
her plans. She promises to replace their 
record execs with loyal, devoted slaves, 
in exchange for their silence. 
- 
Depending on their choice, one of the 
following outcomes will occur: 
 
o 
The Necromusicians Battle 
Nahemoth: Nahemoth is a 
deadly enemy, and will not die 
easily. But this is the only way 
Freddie will be avenged. 
 
o 
The Necromusicians Join 
Nahemoth: The 
Necromusicians happily have 
their record execs transformed 
into spider puppets, and 
benefit from increased funding 
and record sales. 
 
o 
Something Else Weird and 
Wonky: Hey, sometimes 
players get a bit mad with it. If 
something unexpected 
happens, let it!  
 
This is it! The moment of truth. The big 
reveal. Why were all those men murdered? Why 
were their bodies drained of all their fluids? Why 
were Necromusicians the ones targeted? And 
who really is Maris Pfeiffer?  
 
Once the players discover Nahemoth’s 
lair at the heart of the Crawling Tunnels, read or 
paraphrase the following text to them: 
 
 
 
 
“The chamber before you is a vast 
hatchery- a nursery of translucent, 
biomechanical eggs, fed from jars of fluid. You 
see the jars are labelled by victim, and by 
substance. Freddie’s blood filters into an egg 
sac nearby; another man’s tears are siphoned 
into a leathery casing, inside which something 
twitches. 
A woman, dressed in a sharp white suit, 
stands at the centre of the hatchery. She’s 
smiling, luxuriantly, like a cat. In one hand, she 
toys with a wine glass brimming with blood, and 
in the other she toys with a half-gnawed bone. 
Her smile widens as you approach. 
‘How delightful. Inquisitive, aren’t you? 
And strong- strong enough to survive my 
children, and the tunnels above. Come, won’t 
you talk to me? I’d love to talk to you. You have 
so much left to learn.’ 
She drains her glass, and points to the 
eggs. Inside them, protoplasmic figures writhe. 
Half-spider, half-human. They’re transforming. 
Transforming into perfect clones of the record 
execs you’ve seen on TV.” 
 
 
 
Maris Pfeiffer, or Nahemoth, is willing to 
talk with the Necromusicians, if they are willing 
to listen. She compliments them on their 
tenacity and their fruitful investigation; she did 
not expect them to survive their encounter in the 
warehouse. As a Greater Demon, Nahemoth is 
perversely attracted to those she can ensnare 
and manipulate; she thus offers the 
Necromusicians a deal, after explaining the 
purpose of Freddie’s murder. 
 
 
KEY CHARACTER- Nahemoth, or ‘Maris 
Pfeiffer’ 
 
Maris Pfeiffer is the human avatar of the Greater 
Demon Nahemoth, a hideous biomechanical 
fusion of a young girl and a spider. Her human 
form is beguiling, beautiful and alive with a fiery 
charisma that manifests in her smouldering 
amber eyes. Nahemoth is cruel, cold and 
calculating- she views her victims as but a 
means to an end, and feels nothing but 
contempt for the living and dead alike. She uses 
her sexuality to deceive and entrap, but is not 
above transforming into her monstrous form to 
consume those that oppose her. 
 
 


 
 
 
Nahemoth explains the following 
details of her plan: 
 
- 
The fluids drained out of the murder 
victims across Whitechapel are used to 
nurture human-spider hybrids, along 
with the biomechanical technology of 
the Labyrinth. 
- 
These hybrids can assume the 
appearance of others, mutating into 
exact copies undetectable by mundane 
or magical means. 
- 
Nahemoth intends to replace key 
record execs with her human-spider 
children, enabling her to expand her 
influence across the hierarchy of Skin 
City’s music industry. 
- 
The victims needed to be 
Necromusicians to ensure their fluids 
had the necessary magical potency. 
 
Nahemoth then suggests that the players 
stand to benefit from aiding her in completing 
her plan. She promises to consume their record 
execs (the bumbling fools from the start of the 
module) and replace them with doppelgangers. 
This would give the Necromusicians competent 
managers, and let them get ahead in the 
recording industry. All it would cost is a few 
dead suits, and a willingness to turn a blind eye 
to Freddie’s death. 
 
If the players accept this deal, Nahemoth 
holds true to her word. When the players return 
to the surface, they find their recording studio 
awash with gore, spiderwebs and scorched 
metal. The executives have vanished; they will 
return two days later, with strangely vacant 
smiles, and yet will be far more committed and 
competent than ever before. 
 
If the players refuse Nahemoth’s deal, she 
says they are welcome to walk away unharmed, 
as long as they keep silent. If they choose to do 
so, she keeps her word, but sends spider-
demons to spy on them to ensure they remain 
dumb. If they do subsequently sell her out, she 
comes to the surface to hunt them herself. 
 
If the players attack Nahemoth, or refuse to 
walk away, she hungrily transforms into her true 
form. Read, or paraphrase, the following to the 
players: 
 
 
 
 
 
“Maris Pfeiffer’s body shudders. There’s 
an unpleasant squelching sound. Then it’s as if 
every bone is her body is pulped, all at once. Her 
skin deflates like a split balloon, and gore pulses 
out of her mouth and nose. She then swells up, 
nightmarishly large, and out of her spills eight 
enormous mechanical legs covered in hair and 
barbed wire. Eight amber eyes stare out from a 
fanged head. Sprouting from the monster’s 
abdomen, the body of a young girl, hideously 
scarred, laughs and cackles to the sound of a 
diesel motor. Crawler tracks and wheels burst 
into being with popping sounds. 
The horrifying form of Nahemoth looms 
over you. Then she lunges forward, like a ton of 
bricks, her legs clicking like breaking glass.” 
 
 
 
The fight against Nahemoth (see 
Appendix 1) is not one the players can easily 
win. As a Greater Demon, she is extremely 
powerful and has at her command a legion of 
spider-demons. However, there are a number of 
tactics the players can employ against her. 
These tactics will weaken her, and make it 
possible for them to prevail. Do not tell the 
players about these tactics- let them figure it out 
for themselves, or give them subtle hints to help 
them out. 
 
- 
Attacking the Eggs: If the 
Necromusicians damage Nahemoth’s 
eggs, she lunges to repair them with her 
silk. There are twelve eggs in the room. 
Whenever an egg is damaged, 
Nahemoth must roll a D6. On a 4+, she 
must spend her next Action binding the 
egg shut. Eggs that are bound shut 
cannot be damaged. 
 
- 
Destroying the Fluid Jars: Each egg is 
connected to a jar of fluid, which all link 
to a large machine at the heart of the 
chamber. Destroying a fluid jar 
immediately causes Nahemoth to lose 
1 Wound and 1 Will. 
 
IMPORTANT NOTE- ALWAYS GIVE THE 
PLAYERS A CHANCE TO ESCAPE! 
 
Nahemoth is an extremely dangerous enemy for 
novice Necromusicians. If shit is going south, 
give your players a chance to escape and fight 
another day. Perhaps Nahemoth can become a 
recurring antagonist in your future games! 


 
 
Concluding the Module- 
The Aftermath 
 
 
Depending on their choices, “In the 
Constellation of the Black Widow” can end in a 
variety of different ways for your players. 
Depending on how the story came to its 
conclusion, they’ll also earn a variety of 
different rewards and payoffs that could even 
set up future stories. Here are the ways in which 
your version of the module could end. 
 
The Necromusicians Destroy Nahemoth 
 
No easy task, but fuckin’ impressive if 
you can pull it off! Nahemoth is a deadly foe, 
and destroying her is one hell of a way to leave a 
mark on Skin City. If the Necromusicians defeat 
Nahemoth, they avenge the deaths of Freddie 
and his colleagues at Metropolis Shipping. If 
they report their success to the Starfucker Inc. 
Detective Agency, Danny will cut them in on a 
share of the profits, and Kenny and Lettie will 
offer a small reward, too, in addition to Freddie’s 
old necromagical drumkit. Finally, pleased with 
their success as rockstar detectives, the record 
execs fast-track the band to play support at a big 
time show, up at the C02 Academy. Hey, can’t 
win ‘em all, right? 
 
Rewards 
- 
+3 Bonus Experience Points (in addition 
to normal EXP) 
- 
+3 Fortune 
- 
“Hell’s Thunder”, a Necromagical 
Drumkit that unleashes supercharged 
sonic booms 
 
The Necromusicians Join Nahemoth 
Hey, what’s another deal with the devil? 
You’ve already done it once, right? And besides, 
those useless fuckers at the label aren’t exactly 
prioritising you right now. Maybe the spider-
demons will do a better job. In fact, they straight 
up do. By siding with Nahemoth, the 
Necromusicians find themselves moving up in 
the world quite rapidly, buoyed aloft as their 
newly replaced record execs pull the strings 
behind the scenes. The murders continue, but 
the world moves forward. Money is money, and 
not being murdered by a giant demonic spider is 
also a big plus. 
 
Rewards 
- 
+1 Bonus Experience Point (in addition 
to normal EXP) 
- 
+6 Fortune 
 
The Necromusicians Escape from Nahemoth 
 
Running away doesn’t mean giving up- 
it just means giving up right now. If the 
Necromusicians escape from Nahemoth, they 
may well be able to return to her lair in future 
and put a stop to her murderous plans. They 
have, however, made a powerful and influential 
enemy, and will need to be careful if they’re 
going to survive long enough to get their own 
back. 
 
Rewards 
- 
+2 Bonus Experience Points (in addition 
to normal EXP) 
 
 
What If It Ended Unexpectedly, or 
Everyone Died (Again)? 
 
 
If the module ended in a weird or wacky 
way that wasn’t planned for (typical players!), 
then it’s your responsibility as a GM to figure out 
exactly how this ending fits and what its rewards 
may be. I know- not exactly satisfying, not 
exactly comprehensive. Unfortunately, that’s the 
reality of GM’ing. I can provide you with the main 
ways things will go- from there, though, it’s up to 
you. 
 
 
By contrast, if everyone biffed it during 
the adventure, you’ve got a few options. A great 
one is to create a new band of vengeful friends, 
out to hunt down Nahemoth and take revenge 
on her. Another is to pivot, and start play as a 
rival band, perhaps working under Nahemoth to 
start with- see things from the other side! Or, 
alternatively, just re-rack and go again. No-one’s 
going to stop you! 
 
 
It’s worth noting that a ‘Total Party Kill’, 
or TPK, is something that sometimes happens in 
TTRPGs. Even with the best will in the world, and 
with all the chances available to them, 
sometimes the players will just get unlucky. If 
that happens, commiserate, soften the blow 
and work together to find a way forward. 
Remember, as a GM, you’re not here to win or 
outwit your players. Sometimes, you need to 
pull your punches to avoid an unsatisfying 
ending for everyone. 
 


 
 
So, Where Do You Go From Here? 
 
 
Congratulations- you’ve reached the 
end of “In the Constellation of the Black 
Widow”. This module is designed to take 
between one and three sessions to complete, 
assuming each session is about three to four 
hours long. That should be enough for you to 
have got a feel for Dead Metal Rhapsody and for 
you to start considering running a full campaign. 
 
 
If you’d like to continue your 
Necromusicians’ adventures following this 
module, depending on the outcome your players 
achieved, you’ve got a few options for seeding 
new stories and things to explore. Some 
examples include: 
 
 
1. After news spreads of Nahemoth’s 
destruction, a high-ranking manager 
from Spinecrusher CDs approaches the 
band, a contract in her hand. She tells 
them that, if they hunt down and kill 
another Greater Demon, hiding in the 
skyscrapers of Skin City, she’ll make 
sure their next record is a goddamn hit. 
 
2. When playing a packed-out show at a 
local bar, the band are surprised when 
the entire floor collapses and their 
audience is swallowed up by a mutating 
pit of flesh. It’s Nahemoth’s allies, 
taking vengeance! The band has to 
venture into Hell, and get their audience 
back. Those tickets won’t pay for 
themselves. 
 
3. Nahemoth, now working with the band, 
orders them into the darkness of the 
Labyrinth to excavate an ancient relic 
that once belonged to the Legion of the 
Night. When they arrive, it’s already 
being fought over by two horrible hosts 
of demons, and neither side is backing 
down… 
 
4. The band are due to put a new album 
out, and the spider-execs want it to be 
extra heavy. Someone has the bright 
idea to press the vinyl with Hell Steel, 
and so a journey into the underworld is 
needed to recover the meteoric slabs of 
black metal and make the gnarliest 
album of all time… 
 
5. The band are still licking their wounds 
from Nahemoth’s lair, and know too 
well that she’s still out there. They 
decide to track down her spider-execs, 
and cut them like a cancer out of 
corporate society. Maybe then, the 
record labels will finally listen to their 
demo! 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 


 
 
Appendix 1- Monsters 
and Mayhem 
 
Thrasher 
Thrashers are knots of sinew, adrenaline and 
single-minded aggression. They were lucky 
enough that most of their muscles survived the 
atrophy of their First Death, and now pride 
themselves on the grey tissue that still clings like 
bowstring to their bones. Most Thrashers are 
thrill-seekers, adrenaline junkies hoping for their 
next big score. They often charge headlong into 
danger, heedless of the risks, craving whatever 
burst of excitement comes their way next. 
 
Rank: Thrall 
 
Secondaries 
 
Armour 
Defence 
Necromagic 
Mental (5+) 
5+ 
0

Wounds 
Fast 
1 
2 
 
Attacks 
- 
Thrash Talons: A Thrasher’s fingernails 
grow to disturbing length, their edges 
jagged with crusted blood and snot. 
Urgh. 1D6, Physical 
 
Abilities 
- 
The Jitters: Thrashers move almost 
constantly, unable to sit still for more 
than a few seconds at a time. If a 
Thrasher is targeted by an Attack, it may 
immediately Move after the effects of 
the Attack are resolved. 
 
- 
Leaping Predators: Thrashers move 
with astonishing speed, leaping and 
jumping from place to place with ease. 
A Thrasher can jump alarmingly far. If it 
uses this ability to jump into close 
combat with an enemy, any Attack it 
makes with its Thrash Talons benefits 
from an Advantage. 
 
Jenny Clay, Aspiring Necromusician 
Jenny Clay is an overconfident bratty witch bitch 
with very little patience for posers and losers 
alike. She’s a member of the Angels of Death, 
rocks hard and fast, and tears through her 
victims in a whirl of guitar strings and bad 
language. 
 
Rank: Henchman 
 
Secondaries

Attacks 
- 
Ensorcelled Bass: Necromusicians use 
a wide variety of ensorcelled magical 
instruments- a guitar that spits fire, 
drums that shatter bones with sonic 
booms, even harps that shred enemies 
in a shower of silver strings. 3D6, 
Physical OR Mental OR Magical. 
 
Necromagic 
- 
Overdose of Death (Minor): Just a little 
tiny heart attack. Nothing serious. Just 
cardiac arrest. For fun. The target must 
immediately make a Guts Test. If they 
fail, they lose D3 Wounds. 
 
- 
Raining Blood (Major): The air fills with 
a slick of blood and gore. Visibility 
plummets as blood rains from the sky. 
The ground becomes horribly slippery 
and caked in gore. 
 
- 
Screaming for Vengeance (Metal): 
Jenny unleashes a frenzied howl, 
sending her foes into a psychotic rage. 
Each Necromusician affected by this 
Necromagical Power must immediately 
Move towards and Attack their nearest 
ally. This Attack receives +1D6 to its roll 
(to a maximum of 6D6). 
 
Abilities 
- 
Deal with the Devil: Blood is power. 
Whenever Jenny Clay loses a Wound, 
she immediately gains 1 point of 
Necromagic. This can take het above 
their starting value. 


 
 
Hybrid Spider-Demon 
Fusions of bear-sized spider and human limbs, 
Nahemoth’s Hybrid-Spider Demons are hideous, 
wretched monsters that crawl and stumble 
towards their foes, hissing and stridulating as 
they go. Drinkers of blood, eaters of flesh, Hybrid 
Spider-Demons have no sentience. They are 
ravenous animals, and nothing more. 
 
Rank: Henchman

Attacks 
- 
Venomous Grasp: The Spider-Demon 
rips into its prey and drags it into its 
web. 3D6, Physical. If the target suffers 
Damage, the Spider-Demon may 
immediately make a Move, taking the 
target with it! 
 
- 
Web Shot: The Spider-Demon 
unleashes a gummy stream of web. 
2D6, Physical. This Attack does not 
inflict Damage. Instead, if it would 
inflict at least 1 point of Damage, it 
instead renders the target unable to 
Move until they break free. 
 
Abilities 
- 
The Beast Within: Spider-Demons are 
unpredictable and savage. When 
reduced to 0 Will, instead of falling 
under the PC’s control, a Spider-Demon 
rolls a D6 and consults the following 
table: 
o 
1-2: Berserk. The Spider-
Demon enters a frenzy! On its 
Turns, it will lunge at and attack 
the nearest target- friend or foe! 
o 
3-4: Cower. The Spider-Demon 
flees, searching for safety. On 
its Turns, it will run and try to 
hide, recovering 1 point of Will 
if it spends at least 1 Turn out of 
any enemy’s line of sight. 
o 
5-6: Broken Will. The Spider-
Demon falls under the PC’s 
control, as normal. 
Nahemoth 
Nahemoth is a Greater Demon of the Legion of 
the Night, and takes the form of a colossal 
biomechanical spider out of which sprouts the 
burnt and mutilated body of a young girl. She is 
forever hungry, a cold and calculating 
monstrosity eager to extend her influence over 
the world of the living. 
 
Rank: Tyrant (5) 
 
Secondaries

Attacks 
- 
Stabbing Chitinous Legs: Nahemoth 
lances her biomechanical legs into the 
hearts of her victims. 7D6, Physical. 
This Attack inflicts +1 Damage for every 
result equal to or higher than the 
target’s Defence beyond the first. If this 
Attack inflicts at least 3 Damage, it also 
throws the target back and knocks them 
to the ground. 
 
- 
Vile Exhalation: Nahemoth spits forth a 
gout of poison, maggots and spiders. 
5D6, Magical. This Attack targets all 
enemies within a cone in front of 
Nahemoth. She can also choose to coat 
them in acid. If the target is coated in 
acid, their Physical Armour is worsened 
by 1 for every time they’ve been coated 
(i.e. a 5+ would become a 6+, and so 
on). A target’s Physical Armour returns 
to normal after the end of Combat.

- 
Infernal Choir: Nahemoth owes her 
allegiance to the Legion of the Night. 
Nahemoth’s Necromagical Powers are 
themed around the Legion of the Night, 
spiders, insects and other crawling 
beasts. Choose them as you would 
Powers for a Necromusician, or 


 
 
improvise as and when you need to 
during a battle involving her. Keep the 
players on their toes! 
 
- 
Hellish Masque: Greater Demons walk 
among us, disguised as record execs, 
politicians, rebels without a cause- they 
lie, cheat and deceive in skin that’s not 
their own. All Greater Demons, 
including Nahemoth, can assume a 
perfect Undead or Living disguise, 
becoming indistinguishable from a 
normal human or Undead denizen of 
Skin City. This guise will have a 
backstory, name and personal history- 
Greater Demons use these identities to 
manipulate mortals and secure 
positions of power. Nahemoth’s cover 
identity is Maris Pfeiffer, a fashionista 
and weaver from Skin City, who uses 
her beauty and sexuality to entrap her 
victims. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 


 
 
Appendix 2- Tips for 
Running “In the 
Constellation of the 
Black Widow” 
 
Tip the First 
 
“Thou Shalt Reward Your Players with 
Discovery” 
 
 
As a murder mystery meets demon-
stomping dungeon romp, “In the Constellation 
of the Black Widow” requires your players to 
piece together clues and figure out the culprit. 
But nothing’s more frustrating than an 
investigation that leads nowhere! When your 
players are on the hunt for Nahemoth, make 
sure that their investigations get them 
somewhere, even if they screw up. Perhaps they 
find a sneaky hint, or get tipped off by a 
sympathetic Living Dead Girl, or get lucky and 
steal a docket from Danny ‘Dodger’s’ desk. Make 
sure the story keeps moving, and don’t 
stonewall your players for no reason. At the 
same time, make sure you’re not just telling 
them all the facts. They’ve got to figure it out for 
themselves in order for it to be fun! 
 
Tip the Second 
 
“Thou Shalt Prepare to Improvise, and to Go 
Off-Script” 
 
 
One of the biggest traps new GMs fall 
into is not being flexible enough to react when 
their players do something they didn’t plan for. 
Unfortunately, them’s the brakes. Roleplaying 
games are not predictable- pre-written 
adventure modules, like this one, can only 
account for so much. As such, when shit hits 
the fan, you as a GM need to be ready to 
accommodate and adapt to what you can. Your 
players want to bust down the doors to 
Metropolis Shipping and demand answers? OK, 
cool. Now they have to fight Frankenroadies on 
supercharged forklifts as they try to break 
through to the boss. Your players immediately 
attack Maris when they meet her in the Type 
Ooh Negative? Guess you’ve got a demon strip 
club fight on your hands! You need to be 
empowered to say ‘yes’, but in a way that keeps 
the story exciting and doesn’t cheapen it. That 
being said… 
 
Tip the Third 
 
“Thou Shalt Keep the Players on Track” 
 
 
In order for a TTRPG to be fun, players 
need to feel like they’re making progress and 
that the story is going somewhere. This means 
keeping the story on track. Absolute freedom to 
fuck about is NOT as fun as your players think it 
is- it just results in frustration, eventually 
boredom, and then subsequently 
disconnection. You need to rock a middle 
ground, especially in a story where clues 
connect together. If your players are going too 
far in the wrong direction, nudge them back in 
the right one. Be subtle, and be gentle. Let them 
fumble about for a bit, and then steer them back 
on course. 
 
Tip the Fourth 
 
“Thou Shalt Play Up the Horror- But Be 
Mindful” 
 
 
This module deals with gore, 
arachnophobia, implied sexual violence and 
other themes some players may be 
uncomfortable with. You’re encouraged to lean 
into the horror elements- this is Dead Metal 
Rhapsody, after all- but please have a proper 
Session Zero to discuss what elements might 
need to be toned down. Don’t skip it. DON’T skip 
it, you fucker. I see you. Be nice to other people 
for once, OK? Maybe the spider-demons could 
be goat-demons, or centipede-demons, 
instead. Use your imagination, if it makes 
people more comfortable and helps them have 
more fun. 
 
Tip the Fifth 
 
“Thou Shalt Be Loosey-Goosey” 
 
 
If this is your first time GM’ing, don’t 
worry about everything being perfect. It won’t 
be. You’ll make mistakes. You’ll fuck up. Just use 
the framework we’ve given you, and tell the 
most fun story with it you can. Try not to stress, 
and enjoy the experience. Like anything, GM’ing 
is a skill- you’ll get better in time.


 
 
Afterword 
 
To everyone who picked up this book, 
and somehow managed to make it this far, thank 
you. As an author, game designer and neurotic 
creative, it’s heartwarming to know that 
somewhere, out there in the big wide world, a 
stranger thought it worth their time to read what 
you wrote and, maybe- just maybe- might have 
even enjoyed it. Dead Metal Rhapsody was 
always intended as a labour of love, an 
experiment to see if I could even if the should 
was in short supply. So, thank you. I hope it was 
worth your time. And, if not, oh, well. I can’t win 
them all. 
 
Dead Metal Rhapsody began with two 
words: “undead rockstars”. From there, filtered 
through so much heavy metal and sleep 
deprivation, it steadily evolved from a 30-page 
pamphlet into this enormous, sprawling thing 
you have before you today. Going in, I knew I had 
no money, and very little time. I wouldn’t be able 
to polish, to pay deserving artists for their 
efforts, to afford fancy layouts and book designs 
that were beyond my ability to make in Microsoft 
Word. But I still did it. On the off-chance 
someone liked it. And, hey, I like it. I may never 
get a chance to play it. But I made it, and that, 
for me, is enough. 
 
As well as thanking you for your time, I’d 
like to thank my long-suffering group of players, 
for whom I’m a forever GM. They include Dom, 
Edric, the two Dans, Millie, Connor and Anna, 
but also many other people I’ve had the 
pleasure of running games for in the past. You 
might see their names crop up in the examples 
throughout this book. Keep an eye out for them- 
they’re all wonderful people, and have helped 
me develop as a designer and writer for years at 
this point, and have also helped to keep me 
sane. Thanks, folks. It means a lot. 
 
I also want to thank all the heavy metal 
bands out there who made this book possible. 
Without your music, there would be no Dead 
Metal Rhapsody, no Pit, no Labyrinth, no 
nothing. You are all an inspiration, from the most 
serious, beardy, po-faced post-metal band all 
the way through to the peak of 2000s MySpace 
metalcore. Love you all- keep rockin’. 
 
The last thing I want to say is that this 
version of Dead Metal Rhapsody will always be 
available for free. You will never have to pay to 
get this PDF in your hands, and you’re more than 
welcome to share this PDF with others. 
Sometimes, it’s enough just to make something, 
and to enjoy the creative process. Not 
everything has to be monetised, not everything 
has to be a hustle. I just hope you enjoy the 
game. 
 
This does, of course, mean no art, as 
I’m no artist and cannot afford to pay talented 
people for their time and effort. I refuse to use AI 
art, because fuck it. Fuck it, and its art-thieving 
data scraping, and its soullessness, and its 
environmental impact. If that bothers you, so be 
it. But that’s the reason. I’d rather have a simple, 
text-heavy design, than pad out the page count 
with AI slop. Games industry, take note. Pay your 
artists. They deserve it. 
 
That’s all from me. Have a great time 
exploring the depths of the Pit, and be good 
people. 
 
All the best, 
 
Ben 
 
AKA, Exogenesis Games
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 


 
 
Life’s too short to spend it dying. 
 
Death’s too short to do anything but live. 
 
Don’t fear the end. 
 
Fear wasting the time that’s given to you. 
 
Live fast, die young, try not to die again. 
 
You’re a Necromusician now. 
 
You’re dead and loving it. 
 
You’re not going to let anyone stand in your way. 
 
- 
 
The First Necromusician 
Bang Your Head, Until You’re Dead
